<meta>This page follows the title page of the "Theosophical Questions." It moves from the formal title into the author's personal address to the reader, establishing the spiritual requirements for understanding the profound mysteries that follow.</meta>

XIIX. <note>This Roman numeral 18 likely refers to the section or page numbering within a larger collection of Boehme's works.</note>

# THEOSOPHICAL QUESTIONS <note>original: "QUAESTIONES THEOSOPHICAE"</note>
### or
# A Consideration of Divine Revelation,
### posed in 177 Questions.

### Author’s Preface to the Reader.

It is written: *The natural man perceives nothing of the Spirit of God; it is foolishness to him, and he cannot understand it.* (1 Corinthians 2:14.) And again it is written: *The Spirit searches all things, even the depths of the Godhead.* (1 Corinthians 2:10.)

If <term>Master Smart-Aleck<note>original: "Meister Klügling." This is a derogatory term used by Boehme to describe pedantic scholars or rationalists who try to understand spiritual mysteries using only human logic rather than divine inspiration.</note></term> should wish to set himself to these questions and explain them without Divine Light, he would not be able to do so; and he might even consider it a sin to ask so deeply, because he himself cannot understand it. To such a person we say: let him leave it to the one to whom the Spirit of God wishes to give it (the Spirit who searches all things through the spirit of man), because to him it is still an incomprehensibility, and seems to him to be impossible.

But to those who love Jesus, we say that it is very much possible to search and to understand, and it is no impossible thing. For in a true Christian dwells Christ, in whom all the treasures of hidden wisdom are revealed; he alone knows it in the Spirit of Christ, and not in his own nature and ability. As we have clearly set forth and descri- <note>The text cuts off here due to the page break, continuing the word "described" on the next page.</note>

<sig>A</sig>
<meta>catchword: -bed</meta>

<summary>This preface establishes the theological framework for the book, arguing that divine mysteries can only be understood through the Holy Spirit and the "Spirit of Christ" rather than through human reason alone. It warns rationalist critics (Master Smart-Aleck) that the text will remain incomprehensible to them.</summary>
<keywords>Preface, 1 Corinthians 2, Divine Light, Master Smart-Aleck, Spirit of Christ, Revelation, Wisdom, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues from the Author's Preface. The text completes the final sentence of the preface before transitioning into the first of the 177 "Theosophical Questions." This section establishes Jacob Boehme’s foundational view of God as an infinite, non-spatial Unity that exists prior to all creation.</meta>

XIX. <note>original: "Quæstiones Theosophicæ. Fr. 1." Theosophical Questions. Question 1.</note>

...bed [described] them, albeit briefly and summarily, yet in our other writings quite extensively and necessarily; and we commend the reader of this to the revelation of our Lord Jesus Christ.

->Here follow the Theosophical Questions.<-

->The 1st Question.<-

## What is God apart from nature and creature in Himself?

### Summary Points.

God is the eternal Unity, § 1. which flows out of itself, and thereby leads into willing and movement; 2. and has no place where He dwells particularly, but is in, with, and through us, in the <term>ground of the soul<note>In Boehme’s theology, the "ground" (Grund) refers to the deepest, most fundamental essence of a being, where the human soul meets the divine.</note></term>. 3.

### Answer.

God is the eternal Unity, as the immeasurable, only Good, which has nothing behind or before it that might give or contribute anything to Him, or that might move Him; without all inclinations and properties, which without origin of time is only One in itself, as a pure clarity, without contact, which has no place or position anywhere, nor needs any for His dwelling; but is at once outside the world and in the world, and is deeper than any thought can soar; yes, if one were to speak numbers for a hundred thousand years regarding His greatness and depth, one would still not have begun to express His depth, for He is Infinity. Everything that can be counted and measured is natural and figurative; but the Unity of God cannot be expressed, for it is through everything at once, and is therefore called and known as "Good," because it is the eternal gentleness and the highest beneficence within the sensitivity of <term>nature and creature<note>Boehme distinguishes between "Nature" (the governing forces and laws of the universe) and "Creature" (individual living beings).</note></term>, as the sensitive, sweet love.

2. For the Unity, as the Good, flows out from itself, and conducts itself with the outflow into willing and movement; there the Unity loves or dwells through the

<meta>Catchword: willing</meta>

<summary>This page begins the formal inquiry into the nature of God, defining Him as an eternal, infinite Unity that exists beyond time, space, and the physical world. Boehme describes God as the "Good" who flows out from Himself into the human soul.</summary>
<keywords>Eternal Unity, Nature and Creature, Ground of the Soul, Divine Outflow, Infinity, Theosophical Questions, Jacob Boehme</keywords>

Fr. 2. Contemplation of Divine Revelation.

A 2

<meta>catchword: 2. Therefore</meta>

...willing or welling, and this welling or willing perceives the gentleness of the Unity; this is the ground of love within the Unity, of which Moses says: The Lord our God is one God, and none other. <note>original: "Exod. 20. Deut. 4: 39. C. 6: 4." Exodus 20, Deuteronomy 4:39 and 6:4.</note>

3. And it is not as Reason supposes, that God dwells alone above the stars, outside the place of this world. No place is prepared for Him where He might dwell separately, but rather His revelation is merely diverse; He is in, by, and through us; and wherever He becomes active with His love in a life, there God is manifest in His operation. That is to say, His love, as the Unity, is there out-flowing, willing, and perceptible. There God has made Himself a place, as in the <term>ground of the soul<note>Boehme uses "ground" (Grund) to mean the deepest, uncreated essence of a person where they connect with God.</note></term>, in the eternal <term>Idea<note>original: "Idea"; here meaning the eternal blueprint or spiritual mirror of all possibilities within God.</note></term> or reflection of the eternal willing in love, wherein love wills and perceives itself, as is to be understood in the case of angels and blessed souls.

## The 2nd Question.

# What is the abyss of all things, where there is no creature, but only the unfathomable Nothing?

->Summary Points.<-

The abyss is a dwelling of the Unity of God, §. 1. where Father, Son, and Spirit give birth to the Eternal Unity; 2. 3. and that which has gone forth is the Wisdom of God. 4. In the perceptibility of the love of life, five audible <term>Sensus<note>original: "Sensus"; meaning senses, meanings, or phonetic sounds.</note></term> are understood: A. E. I. O. U. 5. The Trinity reveals itself with a threefold breath, called JEHOVAH. 6. Theosophical explanation of the Divine Name JEHOVAH. 7. 8. The <term>Sensus</term> of the perceptible opening-up is called ADONAJ. 9. Theosophical explanation of the Divine Name ADONAJ. 10. 11. This abyss, or the Eternal Nothing, sees through everything unhindered, as an eye of eternal vision, 12. and God is Himself the seeing of the Nothing. 13. Why it is called a "Nothing"? ibid.

1. Answer. IT is a dwelling of the Unity of God, for the opening-up, or the <term>Ichts<note>Boehme’s invented word "Ichts" (Somethingness) is a play on "Nichts" (Nothingness). It represents the first stirrings of existence emerging from the void.</note></term> of the Nothing, is God Himself. The opening-up is the Unity, as an Eternal Life and willing, a pure will, which yet has nothing that it can will, except only itself.

<vocab>Göttliche Offenbarung, Abgrund, Nichts, Ichts, JEHOVAH, ADONAJ, Einheit, Sensus, Idea, Jacob Böhme</vocab>

<summary>This page defines God not as a distant deity above the stars, but as a Unity dwelling within the "ground of the soul." It introduces the concept of the Abyss (the Nothing) and begins a linguistic-mystical breakdown of the divine names Jehovah and Adonai.</summary>

### XIIX. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 2.
<meta>catchword: as</meta>

2. Therefore, the will is a pure, willing love-delight, acting as an out-going of itself toward its own perceptibility. The will is (1) the Eternal Father of the <term>ground<note>The "ground" (Grund) refers to the foundation or abyss of the divine nature.</note></term>; and the perceptibility of love is (2) the Eternal Son, whom the will brings forth within itself to be a perceptible love-power; and the out-going of the willing, perceptible love is (3) the Spirit of Divine Life.

3. And thus the Eternal Unity is a threefold, immeasurable, and beginningless life, which exists as pure willing, as the grasping and perceiving of itself, and as an eternal out-going of itself.

4. And that which has gone forth from the willing, loving, and living is the Wisdom of God; it is the Divine Contemplation and the joy of the Unity of God, through which love eternally introduces itself into powers, colors, wonders, and virtues.

5. In this opening life of Divine Unity, five audible <term>senses<note>original: "Sensus." Böhme correlates the five vowels with the five senses and the process of divine revelation.</note></term> are understood within the perceptibility of the love of life—namely A, E, I, O, U—in which the Divine willing and working reside. These lead themselves into an exhalation toward differentiation and toward the understanding of the Unified Trinity, through which the eternal life perceives and understands itself.

6. The Trinity reveals itself out of the Unity with a threefold breathing, so that this threefold breathing enters into itself in a threefold way to become its own being; and this same threefold sense is called by its <term>sensory name<note>original: "sensualisischen Namen." Böhme uses this to mean a name that can be felt, heard, or perceived by the spiritual senses.</note></term> JEHOVAH.

7. For the Unity, as the I, goes into itself into a threefold essence, which is called JE; and the JE is the Father, who leads himself with his breathing will into the HO, as into a containment of love. In the HO, the Word of all powers is understood, for it creates a <term>circumference<note>original: "Circumferens"; a boundary or enclosure.</note></term> or an enclosing of itself, as the eternal <term>Somethingness<note>original: "Ichts." This is Böhme’s invented word, the opposite of "Nichts" (Nothing). It represents the first movement toward substance or "I-ness."</note></term>. From this, the love-delight goes forth; this out-going is the Spirit, which grasps and forms itself into the VA. For the V is the Spirit, as the out-going, and A is the Wisdom, in which the Spirit grasps itself to become an active life.

8. Thus, this threefold breathing life is then called within itself O. JAH. For the contained delight is the O,

<summary>Böhme explains how the divine will moves from a state of "Nothing" into a perceptible Trinity through the "Wisdom of God." He provides a mystical etymology of the name JEHOVAH, linking its sounds to the Father, Son, Spirit, and the emergence of "Somethingness" from the Abyss.</summary>
<keywords>Trinity, Jehovah, Wisdom of God, five senses, Ichts, divine breathing, Unity, Father, Son, Holy Spirit</keywords>

### Fr. 2. Contemplation of Divine Revelation.
A 3
<meta>catchword: The</meta>

...as an eye of the singular seeing, a pure seeing; and the **JAH** <note>original: "JAH," a shortened form of the Hebrew divine name Yahweh, representing the first breath of manifestation.</note> is the threefold entrance of itself, as to the perceptibility of the willing, which opens itself through the eternal breathing.

9. The opening, as the property in the <term>sense<note>original Latin: "Sensu."</note></term> of perceptible opening, is called **ADONAI** <note>Hebrew: "Lord."</note>, and there are six powers, from which the **Great Mystery** <note>original Latin: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhme's thought, this is the chaotic potentiality or the "womb" from which the world is spoken into existence.</note>, as the exalted name **TETRAGRAMMATON** <note>The "Four-Letter Name" (YHWH). Böhme views this name as the source of all formative power in the universe.</note> arises, from which all beings of the visible and invisible have sprouted, and have come into form and formation.

10. In the word **ADONAI**, as in these six powers, lie the six properties of eternal nature, as of the natural life, from which the angels and souls have flowed according to the inwardness of their **IDEA** <note>original: "IDEA." This refers to the eternal archetypes or the divine "blueprints" of all created things.</note>; and also the six days of the creation of this world, which are enclosed for rest along with the seeing life (which forms itself into being as the **O**), wherein the six powers stand and rest in silent love, as in the eternal unity, and yet with their self-working, they will and go forth without ceasing.

11. And that is the **O**, the seventh day, wherein God has rested from all his works, and eternally rests; that is, the six powers [as (1) desire, (2) mobility, (3) perceptibility, (4) fire or life, (5) light or love, (6) sound, differentiation, or understanding] rest in that from which they sprang, namely in the **O**, as in the place of God, wherein eternal love is indicated, as the unity, or the <term>Aught<note>original: "Ichts." A term Böhme coined to represent the spiritual "somethingness" that exists within the "Nothing."</note></term> of the unity, which is the eternal Sabbath of all things of the good being.

12. Thus we understand (1) how the Eternal Nothing, outside of all beginnings, is a pure appearance, as the eye of eternal seeing. For all things see within it as a nothing, because the **Something** has sprouted from this seeing; thus the Nothing, as the Eternal Unity, stands unhindered through all.

13. And we understand (2) further, that God himself is the seeing and perceiving of the Nothing, and is therefore called a Nothing (even though it is God himself), because it is incomprehensible and inexpressible.

It correlates these powers with the six days of creation, culminating in the seventh day or the "O," which represents eternal rest and the divine Sabbath.</summary>

<meta>This page transitions from the general nature of the divine name to a specific inquiry into the relationship between God's Love and His Wrath. It continues the use of the term "JAH" as the foundational "Yes" of existence, established on page 5.</meta>

<header>XII. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 3.</header>

### The 3rd Question.

### ->What is God’s Love and Wrath? How is He an angry, jealous God, while He Himself is unchangeable Love? How can Love and Wrath be one and the same thing?<-

->**Summaries.**<-

From the "Yes" <note>original German: "Jah." Böhme plays on the German word for "Yes" ("Ja") and the Hebrew divine name "JAH" used on the previous page.</note> comes Truth itself. §. 1. 2. "Yes" and "No" are one thing, yet they divide into two centers. 3. 4. From the self-will arises the "No"; and the Unity is the outpouring "Yes." 5. 6. The self-will desires itself, 7. and it desires the Unity for movement, knowledge, and understanding; 8. It creates desire and is magnetic: just as the Unity is giving. 9. 10. The desiring will is the drawing-in, from which the first four forms are born, 11. 12. according to which God is a consuming fire; 13. and in the darkness, Hell is formed, as mentioned before <note>original Latin: "ibid."</note>. The first property of the drawing-in is the "No," 14. as the ground of Hell. 15. But the Love-fire in the fifth property is the ground of Light. 16. This Love and Light penetrate the Fire; 17. just as the burning of the fire makes the Love effective. 18. Thus, the Wrath-fire and Love-fire create two centers. 19. The Wrath-fire is the ground of eternal nature, 20. and the center of Love is the "Yes," in which the angelic and soulful spirit is understood; 21. just as the ground of souls and angels is understood in the eternal nature-fire. 22. The damnation of souls lies in selfhood: 23. as their gentle well-being lies in Love. 24. In this fire God dwells in His Trinity <note>original German: "Dreyheit."</note>; 25. and from this holy fire the "Yes," Jesus, has flowed forth. 26. In God there is no wrath. 27. These two, Love and Wrath, create two principles <note>original Latin: "Principia." These are the two fundamental starting points or realms of existence.</note>, 28. 29. where the "Yes" of God and the "No" of wrath is the foundation. 30. The sixth form, 31. in which the holy names of God stand, 32. and the miracle-working deed according to both fires, 33. which is the foundation of Cabala <note>A mystical interpretation of the Bible; Böhme uses it to describe the inner secrets of divine manifestation.</note> and Magic <note>original: "Magiä." For Böhme, this is the spiritual power of the will to act upon nature.</note>. 34. The seventh form is substantiality <note>original: "Wesenheit." This refers to the actual "body" or essence of the spiritual world.</note>, from which the visible world has sprung; 35. in all its beings are both central fires, 36. and within lies the holy fire hidden, and locked away by wrath. 37. This holy fire was Paradise, and must first be found within. 38. 39.

1. Answer. Although we might be difficult for the reader to understand here, he may nevertheless understand everything through divine power and the invocation of God, if he is truly earnest.

2. The reader should know that all things consist in "Yes" and "No," whether they be divine, devilish, earthly, or whatever they may be called. The One, as the "Yes," is pure power and life, and is the truth of God or God Himself. This

<meta>catchword: would be</meta>

<summary>This page introduces the dialectic between God's Love and Wrath, explaining them as the "Yes" and "No" that constitute all existence. It outlines how self-will creates the "No" (Wrath) which serves as the necessary ground for the "Yes" (Love) to become manifest and effective.</summary>
<keywords>God's Wrath, Divine Love, Jah and Nein, the two centers, self-will, eternal nature, Cabala, Magic, substantiality, Paradise</keywords>

--- Page 17 ---
<meta>This page continues the exploration of the "Yes" and "No" dynamic within the Divine nature. The previous page established that the Divine name "JAH" represents the foundational "Yes" or Truth, while the "No" provides the necessary contrast for the "Yes" to become manifest and perceptible.</meta>

<header>Fr. 3. Contemplation of Divine Revelation.</header>
<sig>A 4</sig>

[it] would be unrecognizable in itself, and there would be in it no joy, prominence, or sensitivity without the No. The No is a counter-strike <note>original: "Gegenwurf," literally a "counter-throw" or "opposition."</note> to the Yes, or to the Truth, so that the Truth might be revealed and be something in which there is a contrary, in which eternal love is active, perceptible, willing, and something that can be loved.

3. And yet we cannot say that the Yes is separated from the No, or that they are two things standing side-by-side; rather, they are only One Thing, but they divide themselves into two beginnings, or principles <note>original: "Principia"</note>, and create two centers <note>original: "Centra"</note>, where each works and wills within itself. Just as the day is in the night, and the night in the day, they are two centers and yet unseparated, except that they are divided by will and desire. For they have two kinds of fires within them: (1) the Day, the hot, opening fire, and (2) the Night, the cold, enclosing fire. And yet together they are only One Fire, and neither would be revealed or active without the other. For the cold is the root of the heat, and the heat is the cause of the cold becoming perceptible. Without these two, which nonetheless stand in constant conflict, all things would be a Nothing, and would stand still without motion.

4. Likewise, this is how the eternal unity of Divine power is to be understood: if the eternal will did not flow out from itself and introduce itself into receptivity <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit." In Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to the state where the spirit becomes "perceptible" or "receivable" by the senses.</note>, there would be no form or distinctness. Instead, all powers would be only One Power; thus, no understanding could exist. For understanding originates in the distinctness of multiplicity, where one property sees, tests, and wills another.

5. Joy likewise resides within this: but if a receptivity is to originate, there must be a specific desire for its own self-sensitivity—a self-will for receptivity—which is not equal to and does not desire the same thing as the single Will. For the single Will only desires the single Good, which it itself is; it only desires itself in equality. But the outgoing will <note>original: "ausgeflossene Wille," meaning the will that has flowed out from the unity into creation.</note> desires inequality, so that it may be distinguished from the equality and be its own "Something," so that there might be something for the Eternal Seeing to see and feel. And from this self-will arises the No; for it leads itself into selfhood <note>original: "Eigenheit," the state of being an individual or self-centered entity.</note> as into a receptivity of itself; it wants to be Something, and does not liken itself to the Unity, for the Unity is...

<summary>This page explains Böhme’s core dialectic: that existence and perception require the tension between "Yes" (Unity/Love) and "No" (Selfhood/Conflict). He uses the metaphor of day and night—two centers of one fire—to show how divine properties only become "sensible" or "real" through their opposites.</summary>

<keywords>Yes and No, duality, Divine Unity, self-will, principles, centers, cold and hot fire, perception, multiplicity</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "Yes" (JAH) and "No" dynamic. On the previous page, Böhme explained that the Divine "Yes" requires a "No" (an opposition) to be perceptible and active. The catchword from the previous page was "comes."</meta>

### XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 3.

[it] comes an outflowing Yes <note>original: "Jah." Böhme uses this both as the Divine name and the affirmative "Yes" of existence.</note>, which stands eternally thus in the breathing of itself, and is an insensitivity <note>In this context, a state of being where nothing is felt or perceived because there is no contrast.</note>, for it has nothing within it in which it may perceive itself, except only in the receptivity <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit," referring to the capacity of the will to receive or "take in" the divine light.</note> of the departed will, as in the No, which is an opposition <note>original: "Gegenwurf," literally a "counter-throw" or counter-image.</note> of the Yes, in which the Yes becomes manifest, and in which it has something that it can will.

6. For the One has nothing in itself that it can will, unless it doubles itself so that it be Two; thus it also cannot perceive itself in the unity, but in the duality it perceives itself.

7. Therefore, now understand the ground correctly: The separated will has gone out from the equality of the eternal willing, and also has nothing that it can will except only itself: But because it is a Something over against the unity (which is as a Nothing, and yet is Everything), it introduces itself into a desire for itself, and desires itself, and also the unity from which it has flowed.

8. It desires the unity for the sake of sensitive love-pleasure, so that the unity may be perceptible within it, and it desires itself for the sake of movement, knowledge, and understanding, so that there may be a distinguishability <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit," the state of being divisible or distinct.</note> in the unity, so that powers may originate: And although power has no ground nor beginning, yet in the receptivity differences arise, from which differences Nature originates <note>original: "urständet," a word Böhme coined to describe the process of taking a stand or emerging into existence.</note>.

9. This outflowing will introduces itself into desire, and the desire is magnetic, as if drawing inward, and the unity is outflowing. Now it is an opposition <note>original: "Contrarium."</note>, as Yes and No: For the outflowing has no ground, but the drawing-inward creates a ground. The Nothing wants to go out from itself so that it may be manifest, and the Something wants to go into itself so that it may be perceptible in the Nothing, so that the unity may become perceptible in it. Thus, Out and In are an inequality.

10. And the No is called a No because it is an inward-turned desire, as if enclosing "No-wards": And the Yes is called Yes because it is an eternal exit, and the ground of all beings, as pure truth. For it has no No before it, but rather the No first originates in the outflowing will of receptivity.

11. This outflowing, desiring will is drawing-inward, and grasps itself within itself; from this come formations <note>original: "Gestältnisse," or forms.</note> and properties. (1) The first property is **Sharpness**: from which...

<summary>Böhme explains that the Divine Unity (the Yes) must divide into a duality (Yes and No) to achieve self-perception and create the "powers" of Nature. He describes the "No" as a magnetic, inward-drawing force that provides a "ground" for the otherwise groundless, outflowing "Yes."</summary>

[there] comes hardness, coldness, dryness and darkness. For the unrestrained nature <note>original: "Ungezogenheit." In Böhme’s thought, this refers to the "undisciplined" or contracting state of the will that has not yet been refined by light.</note> overshadows itself; and this is the true ground of the eternal and also temporal darkness; and the hardness and sharpness is the ground for sensibility <note>The capacity to feel or perceive.</note>. (2) The second property is the movement in attraction, which is a cause of separation. (3) The third property is the true sensation between the hardness and the movement, in which the will perceives itself, for it finds itself in great sharpness, like a great anxiety, so to speak, against the unity. (4) The fourth property is the fire, as the lightning-flash of brilliance; this originates in the conjunction of the great anxious sharpness and the unity: for the unity is gentle and still; And the moving hard sharpness is terrible, as a ground of painfulness.

12. So it is a terror <note>original: "Schrack." Böhme uses this to describe the sudden, transformative "shock" or "crack" when the dark properties meet the light of the unity, resulting in a flash of fire/light.</note> in the conjunction; and in this terror the unity is seized, so that it becomes a glance or luster, as an uplifting joy. For thus the light originates in the midst of the darkness: Then the unity becomes a light, and the pleasantness of the desiring will in the properties becomes a spirit-fire, which takes its source <note>original: "Quall." Böhme frequently puns on the words for "source" (Quelle) and "torment" (Qual) to show how life's energy and its suffering are linked.</note> and origin from the harsh, cold sharpness, in the movement and sensibility in the darkness; and it is the very essence of it, as a terrible consuming power.

13. And thereafter God is called an angry, jealous God, and a consuming fire; Not according to what He is in Himself outside of all receptivity, but according to the eternal fire-ground; and in the darkness the foundation of hell is understood as a forgetfulness of the good, which darkness is completely hidden in the light, as the night in the day, as is to be read in John 1:5 <note>original: "Johannis I: 5."</note>.

14. Thus we see in the above-mentioned properties God's wrath, as the first property of drawing into the No: for it does not liken itself with the Yes, as with the unity, for it makes a darkness in itself, that is a loss of the good.

15. Secondly, it makes a sharpness in itself, that is the ground of the eternal dying of gentleness, from the gentle unity. Thirdly, it makes a hardness in itself, that is eternal death, as a powerlessness. Fourthly, it makes in itself

### 18. Theosophical Questions <note>original Latin: "Quæstiones Theosophicæ"</note>. Fr. 1.

[...] itself in such hardness of death, an everlasting painful sensation. Fifthly, it creates within itself an anxious fire-source <note>original: "Feuer-Quall." Jacob Böhme uses this term to suggest both a "source" (Quelle) and "torment" (Qual), describing the raw, churning energy of existence.</note>. And in these properties, God's wrath and the hellish fire are understood: And it is therefore called Hell <note>original: "Hölle"</note> or a Hollow <note>original: "Höhle"</note>, because it is a hiddenness or an enclosure; It is also called an enmity of God because it is painful, while the Unity of God is a pure gentleness; they are against each other like fire and water: From which also the essences of fire and water in the creation of this world have taken their origin.

16. The fifth property in such an ignition of the outflowing will is now the sensibility of the Unity of God, as Love, which becomes mobile and desiring within the fire, and makes within the fire (as within the painfulness) another <term>principle (Principium): A fundamental realm or state of being. Böhme describes the universe as being composed of three principles: the dark world, the light world, and the visible world.</term>, as a great love-fire. For it is the cause and the ground of the light, so that the light springs forth in the fire-essence; it is the power of love within the light, for thus the Unity introduces itself into movement and sensibility, so that the Eternal Power may be sensible, and that a willing, desire, and discernibility <note>original: "Schiedlichkeit." This refers to the capacity for things to be distinct, separate, and recognizable from one another, rather than being lost in an undifferentiated void.</note> may be within it; otherwise the Unity would be an eternal stillness and insensible.

17. This love and light dwell in the fire and penetrate the fire, so that the essence of the fire is transformed into the highest realm of joy, and no more wrath <note>original: "Grimm"</note> is perceived, but rather a pure taste of love of Divine sensibility.

18. For thus the eternal Unity over-inflames itself, so that it may be a love, and that there may be something to be loved. For if the love of the Unity did not stand in a fire-burning manner, it would not be actual, and there would be no joy or movement in the Unity.

19. Thus one now understands God's wrath in the fire-essence: and the Divine love-fire in the sensibility of love, as in the sensible Unity; these make two centers in one ground, as two kinds of fire.

20. (1) The wrath-fire in the outflowing will of unpleasantness is a ground of eternal nature, from which the angels and the soul of man have received their ground, and it is called the <term>Great Mystery (Mysterium Magnum): The vast, hidden potentiality or "chaos" from which God brings forth the manifestation of the universe.</term>; from which eternal nature this visible world also sprouted and was created, as a reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf." Literally a "counter-throw" or projection; Böhme views the physical world as a mirrored reflection of the internal spiritual reality.</note> of the inwardness.

### Question 3. Contemplation of Divine Revelation

### Fr. 3. Betrachtung Göttl. Offenb.

21. (2) And the Center of Love is the *Jah* <note>original: "Jah." A mystical shortening of the name Yahweh, used by Böhme to signify the divine breath or the "Yes" of existence.</note>, as the fire-flaming breath, which is called God’s Word; it is the breath of the Unity of God, the foundation of power. Within it, the true Holy Spirit is understood in the outflowing of the love-breath, acting as the movement or the life of love. Furthermore, the angelic spirit as well as the soul-spirit are understood to exist within this, in which God is manifest and dwells.

22. But the ground of souls and angels, according to their nature, is understood to be in the eternal **nature-fire**. For the clear Deity does not become a creature (for it is an eternal Unity), but rather it dwells through nature, just as a fire glows through iron.

23. And at this point, we understand the possibility of the damnation of angels and souls: if they lose the **love-fire**, they separate themselves from the Divine Unity and enter into their own desire. Then, the **wrath-fire** burns within them and becomes their actual life.

24. But when the Divine **love-fire** burns in their **central fire**, then their fire-life is a pure joy and a gentle well-being; then God’s fire and the fire of nature stand within them as a single foundation.

25. In this fifth property, the glory and majesty of God become manifest as a light of love. Of this, the Scripture says (1 Timothy 6:16): *God dwells in a light that no one can approach*; this indicates that no creature was ever born directly out of the **Central Fire** of love, for it is the most holy fire, and is God Himself in His Trinity.

26. And out of this holy fire has flowed the *Jah*, as a ray of the sensible Unity; this is the precious name **JESUS**, who redeemed the poor soul once again from the **wrath-fire**. In assuming humanity, He gave Himself into the deviated **Central Wrath-Fire** of God's anger within the soul, and ignited it again with the **love-fire**, uniting it once more with God.

27. O, you people, take note of this! Understand now the true foundation: In God there is no wrath; He is pure, unadulterated love. Only in the foundation—through which love becomes mobile—is there **wrath-fire**; but in God, it is only a cause of the—

## XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 3.

...kingdom of joy and of powers; and in the center of the **wrath-fire**, there is the greatest, most terrible darkness, pain, and torment.

28. And these two are within one another like day and night, where neither can comprehend the other, but one dwells in the other, and they constitute two *Principles* <term>Principia: foundational sources or origins of reality</term>, as two eternal beginnings.

29. The first beginning is called the Kingdom of God in Love; and the other beginning is called the Kingdom of God’s Wrath, as the foundation of Hell, in which the cast-out spirits dwell.

30. The foundation of the Kingdom of God is pure *Yes* <note>original: "Jah." Böhme uses this as a mystical abbreviation for the divine name and as a symbol of the creative "Yes."</note>, as the powers of the distinguishing Word; and the foundation of the Wrath of God is pure *No*, from which lies originate. For this reason, Christ said: the devil is a father of lies, for his foundation is pure *No* and a contradiction of the truth, which is the *Yes*.

31. The sixth property in the outflowing will is resonance, sound, understanding, speech, or discernment—as true intellect. It stands in both central fires at once. In the center of the self-gratification of the natural fire, without the cooperation of the Holy Fire (as far as these two fires are separated, as is understood with the devils and damned souls), it is not true understanding, but only cunning and shrewdness, as a testing of the foundation of nature; a vain misuse of nature’s powers, from which deception, suspicion, folly, foolishness, and frivolity originate.

32. Within this sixth property stand the Holy Names, as the divine powers in the unfolding of the Unity, in acting and willing; and they stand in both fires simultaneously—in the fire of natural mobility and in the fire of the love-flame.

33. Here stands the wonder-working Word in its operation, for the great name of God, the <term>TETRAGRAMMATON</term> <note>original: "TETRAGRAMMATON" (Greek: "four letters"). Refers to the sacred Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note> (<term>JEHOVAH</term>), is the center of God’s wonders here; it works in both central fires, which the evil spirits misuse in their transformation according to the center of nature’s fire.

Böhme refers to the tradition of mystical interpretation of the divine names and the structure of the universe.</note> and <term>MAGIC</term> <note>original: "Magia." In this context, Böhme means the spiritual art of directing the will and powers of nature, rather than common sorcery.</note> is contained within this ground; for these are the actual powers where the insensible works within the sensible. 35. The seventh property of the outflowing, longing will is the **essentiality** <term>Wesenheit: the substance or "body" of a spiritual power</term>, in which all powers lie and work as an essence, as a **subject** <term>Subjectum: the underlying foundation or vessel</term> of all powers, from which the visible world originated, and through the movement of the **wonder-working** Name flowed out, and passed into distinction and formality.

36. Therefore, in all beings of this world, both **central fires** are present, according to God's love and wrath, as can be seen in the creatures.

37. But the Holy Fire lies hidden within, which the curse (as the movement of God's wrath) keeps closed up with sin, as can be understood by the **tincture** <term>Tinctur: in Böhme’s thought, a spiritual "dye" or medicinal force that can transform and "tinge" base matter into a higher state</term>, and yet an entrance is possible through God's permission.

38. That same outflowing Holy Fire, while it still worked through the earth, was **Paradise**, and it still is; but man has been cast out, and many seek themselves to death for this fire and yet do not find it, unless they have first found it within themselves.

39. Thus understand us in this question concerning God's love and wrath: that two kinds of fire are understood. (1.) A Love-Fire, where there is pure light; this is called God's love, as the sensitive unity. (2.) And a Wrath-Fire from the relish of the outflowing self-will, through which the Love-Fire becomes manifest. This Wrath-Fire is a foundation of eternal nature, and in the center of its inwardness it is called an eternal darkness and pain. And yet, both fires are only a single ground, and have been so from eternity to eternity; they remain one, but separate into two eternal beginnings, just as one might reflect upon the relationship between a burning fire and its light.

## What existed before the angels and creation were?

->Summary.<-

God was with the two central fires, as an eternal generative source of wonders, where all creatures lay within as an **IDEA** <note>original: "IDEA." Böhme uses this Latin term to describe the eternal, unmanifested patterns of all things within the divine mind.</note>, and both central fires were a single essence, however...

--- Page 24 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "central fires" mentioned on page 13. The catchword from the previous page was "under," which connects to the opening phrase "in two Principles."</meta>

->XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 4.<- <note>original: "XIIX. Quæstiones Theosophicæ. Fr. 4."</note>

...in 2 Principles. 2. Since the central fire of the [creature's] own-will has moved itself: so the creation has proceeded, so that the Idea became pictorial; 3. In which movement the hellish foundation also broke forth, 4. after Lucifer turned away from God's love into the central fire of wrath, 5. which is now a center of the visible world, and is called Satan. 6.

1. Answer. **GOD** was with the two Central Fires, with the great powers, as an eternal infinite birthing of wonders, colors, and virtues, where the angels and the soul of man, along with all creatures—of this world and the inner angelic spiritual world—lay within an **IDEA** or spiritual in-modeling. Within this, **GOD** has seen all His works from eternity, not in a creaturely, formed manner or as a [distinct] separation, but in the formality of the powers, where **God’s** Spirit played with itself. <term>Inmodelung: the internal shaping or "modeling" of a spiritual concept before it takes physical form.</term>

2. In these Central Fires was the Element, and both Central Fires were a single being, only distinguished in two principles, like fire and light. <note>In Böhme's thought, the "dark" fire of nature and the "light" fire of love are two sides of the same divine reality.</note>

3. But when the Central Fire of the own-will <term>Eigen-Wille: the individual will that seeks its own path rather than God’s</term> moved itself and introduced itself into a greater desire for its own visibility and formation, then creation proceeded. This was moved by the eternal will of **GOD** according to both fires, so that the Idea became pictorial for the praise of the wonders of **GOD**.

4. And in such movement, the hellish foundation of **GOD’S** wrath broke forth, which **GOD** cast out from His [harmonious] operation and enclosed in darkness; there it stands even today, like a hungry palate, full of desire for the creation, and wants also to be creaturely and pictorial. <note>The "hungry palate" is a vivid metaphor for the greed and consuming nature of evil/wrath.</note>

5. And that is the ground and the cause why the Throne-Prince Lucifer turned away from **GOD’S** love into the Central Fire of wrath, wherein he thought to rule over **GOD’S** gentleness and love; but for that reason, he was cast out from the Central Love-Fire, lost his throne in the light, and now possesses hell; the same happens to the damned souls.

6. This hellish foundation in the curse of **GOD’S** wrath is a center of the visible world, and is called Satan.

## The 5th Question.

**What was the ground and the essence from**
**which the angels were created; what**
**was that same power in the Word of God**
**which flowed out and became**
**creaturely?**

### Summaries.

1. Scripture calls the angels "flames of fire." §. 1. 2. 
2. The <term>IDEA</term> <note>In Böhme’s thought, the "Idea" is the eternal, spiritual blueprint of a thing before it is physically created.</note> was a form of the Divine Name, 3. and became manifest through the breathing in the fire. 4. 
3. With the <term>Jah</term> <note>original: "Jah" — the first half of the sacred name of God, representing the divine "Yes" or light.</note> the "No" also formed itself as the "own-will," 5. which broke away from the <term>Jah</term> in <term>Lucifer</term>, 6. and for that reason the devil is a vain liar. 7. 
4. The <term>IDEA</term> of the Holy Angel is a figure of the Holy Name of God; 8. As many names as there are, so many are the Ideas, like the stars. 9. 10. 
5. The <term>Idea</term> sprouted forth from the thrones. 11. 
6. The angels are distinguished into thrones and offices, 12. and they dwell in the inner element of fire and light. 13. 
7. Their foundation is from both central fires, 14. from the Divine <term>senses</term> <note>original Latin: "Sensibus." Böhme views the divine powers as having a form of sensory perception.</note>. 15. 
8. The power of the prince-angels stands in the strength of the fire-power. 16. 
9. There are also princes in the elements; however, they are not equal to the inner ones. 17. 
10. The spirits of the letters and their composition can open the understanding, 18. as in the angelic realm, so also in the human kingdom. 19. 
11. The <term>Word of the Lord</term> <note>original Latin: "Verbum Domini"</note> is the understanding: the angels are its letters; 20. the prince-angels indicate the letters, and the ministering angels indicate the composition: 21. just as the power for the <term>meaning</term> <note>original Latin: "Sensu"</note> lies within the letters, so it is also in the prince-angels. 22. 
12. Man does not know the power in his own mouth, 23. in that he carries the power of all things in his mouth. 24. 25.

## 1. Answer.

The Scripture says of **GOD**: Psalm 104:4.
> You make your servants winds, and your angels flames of fire.

In these words lies the entire understanding; for with the word "servants," the pictorial <term>Idea</term> is understood as the spirit of the angels, [coming] from the breath or breathing of Divine strength and power, from the Holy Name of **God**.

2. And the expression "flames of fire" indicates the central fire of eternal nature, wherein the creature [finds] its 

<meta>catchword: essence</meta>

<summary>This page explores the origin and nature of angels, defining them as "flames of fire" and "letters" of the Divine Word. It explains that angels are structured hierarchically (thrones and offices) and are manifestations of God's holy names and breath.</summary>
<keywords>Angels, Fire-flames, Idea, Divine Name, Jah, Lucifer, Word of God, Thrones, Senses, Composition of Letters</keywords>

--- Page 26 ---
<meta>This page continues the discussion of the "own-will" and how the creaturely existence relates to the divine "Idea." The catchword from the previous page, "barung," indicates the transition into the word "revelation" (Offenbarung) which begins the next section.</meta>

...being, just as the own-will is of its own being. This is understood thus:

3. The <term>IDEA</term> <note>In Böhme’s theology, the "Idea" is the eternal, spiritual blueprint or image of a thing as it exists in God’s mind before physical creation.</note> or the image of **God** was a form of the Divine Name within the outgoing name of **God**, in which **God** has known all things from eternity, as an imagination <note>original: "Imagination" — here meaning a divine process of "imaging" or "forming" within the mind.</note> of the Divine Will, wherein the will of the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>The "Groundless" or "Abyss": Böhme's term for the absolute, undifferentiated divine nothingness that precedes all existence.</note> has formed itself into a shape, and yet was no creature, but only an <term>IDEA</term>, just as an image forms in a mirror; thus has the imagination of Divine Power formed itself in the outgoing name of **God**.

4. But when **God** wished to have such an <term>IDEA</term> in a living creature, as in its own-will, He moved and separated the <term>Central Fire</term> of eternal nature; thus the <term>IDEA</term> became manifest in the fire, which occurred through the breathing or the <term>Jah</term> <note>The "Jah" represents the first half of the holy name (Jehovah) and signifies the divine "Yes" or the light-breath of God.</note>.

5. So the "No"—as the outgoing will of self-appropriation—formed itself along with the breathed-out "Yes," so that the creature might stand in its own-will: which own-will is understood in the <term>Central Fire</term>, as in the properties of fire, wherein the creaturely life stands.

6. For if that had not been, then Lucifer could not have broken himself away from the Good in his own-will and fallen. Had he not possessed an own-will, then **God’s** power must have fallen; but instead, the creature broke itself away from the Good and wished to rule in the might and in the properties of the <term>Central Nature-Fire</term>, as in the *transformation* and *fantasy*, into which he also entered.

7. Therefore Christ called him a murderer and a liar from the beginning, and this because the "No" gained the dominion within him, so he is a vain liar.

8. The essence and being of the good angels is a power of the <term>Central Fire</term> and the <term>Central Light</term>; therein stands their image: But the <term>IDEA</term> within them is a figure of the Holy Name of **God**, as the wonder-working Word.

9. And just as the Divine (powers and) Names are many and without number, so there is also a distinction among the <term>IDEAS</term> within them; just as one power has a different effect than another, even though they are equal in **God**, they are nonetheless distinct in the outflow—as in Wisdom—because of the revel- <note>The text cuts off mid-word: "Offenbarung" (revelation).</note>

### Fr. 5. Consideration of Divine Revelation

revelation and miracles. Thus, there is also a distinction of the angelic **IDEA** in their powers; and one always has a greater power and might than another, as well as a different virtue.

10. Just as the stars in the firmament are distinct, so too are the angels, so that there may be a <term>Harmony</term> <note>original: "Harmonia"</note>, as a joy and knowledge of the Divine powers.

11. Their true foundation stands in the thrones of the powers of **God**, and they are all together servants in such thrones of powers. For out of the thrones—as from the holy out-flowing name of **God**, from the eternal Unity—the **IDEA** has sprouted; therefore, there are distinctions and dominions among them.

12. Although they are all **God’s** servants, each throne nonetheless has its offices and legions with specific names, according to those same thrones and powers. For this reason, there are Prince-Angels among them, all according to the property of each throne; whatever power the throne possesses, such is the Prince-Angel. The others are attendants <note>original: "Diener"</note>, not slaves <note>original: "Knechte" — Böhme often distinguishes between a "Diener" (a free, willing participant) and a "Knecht" (a menial or bound servant).</note>, but rather voices in the harmony of the throne, all to the praise of the great **God**.

13. The entire depth within the world and outside the world is full of such thrones and dominions, yet not in the four elements, but in the pure element of the inner fire and light; they possess another <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium" — In this context, a 'Principle' refers to a distinct realm of existence or a fundamental state of being.</note>, namely another world, which is indeed also within this world, but in a different quality, in another <term>Chaos</term> <note>original: "Chaos" — Here used to mean a hidden, spiritual "atmosphere" or "space" rather than disorder.</note>.

14. Thus understand the foundation of the angels: they are from the essence of both inner eternal <term>Central Fires</term>; their powers are the great out-flowing names of **God**, all sprouted from the **Yes**, and led into the **No**, so that the powers might become manifest; thus there had to be an opposition <note>original: "Gegenwurf" — Literally a 'counter-throw'; a reflection or resistance that allows the divine light to become visible and distinct.</note> wherein a distinction might exist.

15. The name Jachiel (the **Lord**, the Living One, my **God**) is the distinction of the **Idea** of virtues; from this flows Eliel (**God** my **God**) and the holy names according to Divine properties, of which there are many. This is only to indicate to the reader that they all flow from the Divine <term>Senses</term> <note>original: "Sensibus" — In Böhme's thought, the "Senses" of God are the active, perceiving powers or thoughts that become the basis of creation.</note>.

16. But the names of the Prince-Angels which carry the R, T, or S in their <term>Sense</term> <note>original: "Sensu"</note>, their power stands in the strength of the fire-might, from the high name <term>Tetragrammaton</term> <note>The sacred four-letter Hebrew name of God, YHWH.</note>,

### XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fr. 5.

(**JEHOVAH**) <note>The Tetragrammaton, the four-letter name of God. Böhme often breaks down the sounds of this name to explain the process of creation.</note> and are princes over the stars and elements, because they have their dwelling in the inner element, from which the four elements flowed out and still flow out.

17. Although there are also other external princes who dwell in the four elements and are called stellar spirits, such as <term>ascendants</term> <note>original: "Ascendenten." In astrology, the rising sign or influences that govern the physical world.</note>, which also have their properties in the world, yet they are not equal to the inner spirits. For they possess a different <term>Chaos</term> <note>Böhme uses "Chaos" not as "disorder," but in the Paracelsian sense: a hidden, formless atmospheric substance or "spiritual space" from which things manifest.</note> of an external nature, about which no more will be said here due to the risk of misuse and superstition.

18. If we wish to contemplate the powers and virtues of the angels and understand them correctly—not clinging to literal images as foolish reason always does—then we should simply look at the spirits of the letters, and the sense <note>original: "Sensu"</note> and power in which each one stands. Afterward, consider the joining together of those same literal spirits, from which the word or understanding arises; thus we have the entire foundation with **Yes** and **No**, <note>The "Yes" and "No" are Böhme's fundamental dialectic: the "Yes" is the divine light/expansion, and the "No" is the fire/contraction. All things exist through the tension of these two poles.</note> their whole basis.

19. Just as words are distinguished by their literal spirits (vowels), so too is their distinction in a creaturely, figurative manner. This is also how the human kingdom is structured, and everything that is called a "creature." They are all together merely divine senses <note>original: "Sensus"</note> from the two central fires, like composite words of divine powers.

20. For just as the entire alphabet is the complete understanding of all beings, so the Word of **GOD** is the sole understanding of all things, and the angels are His letters.

21. The prince-angels signify the letters, as the thrones; and the ministering angels signify the composition of the senses <note>original: "Sensuum"</note>. They allow themselves to be used for the harmony of divine understanding to the praise of **GOD**.

22. Just as the authority and power to distinguish meanings lies within the letters, so the greatest power lies in the throne-angels. The others are like the joining of letters for the sake of distinction and are co-powers. Their rule and order are to be understood like a tree with its branches. For all generations within the four elements, including humanity, exist in such a form; almost every race has its superiors, all of which is a figure of the inner spiritual world.

23. 24. He carries the power of all things hovering in his mouth: if he had the faith that he might form this power, then he would have the ground of all mysteries, and could perform wonders like the angels; which the Scripture also testifies: 
> If you have faith, as a grain of mustard seed, you may say to the mountain: Cast yourself into the sea. (Matthew 21:21.)

But only to the worthy. Enough here.

## The 6th Question.

# What is the office and work of the angels, and why does God's power introduce itself into formation?

### Summaries.

God governs through the angels, §. 1. who are powers of the Word of God, 2. how the senses come from the Mind <note>original: "Gemüth." This is a central term for Böhme, referring to the seat of the soul's desires and thoughts.</note>. 3. God is the eternal Mind as the Understanding. 4. The **Yes** <note>original: "Jah."</note> is Divine: the **No** is selfhood. 5. In God there are no thoughts. 6. He works all things through the business of angels, 7. who are workers of the wonders of God, 8. 9. and it becomes for them what they desire. 10. The angels' joy in their knowledge. 11. Their nourishment is a creation. 12. The creation of the inner and outer world is a formation of the powers of God. 13. The visible world is the outflowed Word. 14. The more inward in the power, the nearer to God. 15. 16. The angels are playmates of the kingdom of joy of God. 17. The heathens did not have the inner ground. 18.

1. Answer. Just as we humans on earth [perceive] all things, as the entire understanding with the distinctiveness of words,

## XVIII. Theosophical Questions. Fragment 6.

governs; even so God works and governs, as the eternal Mind <note>original: "Gemüthe." For Böhme, this refers to the deep seat of the soul's desires, thoughts, and will.</note> of the unified power, with such figurative words in Wisdom.

2. The angels are purely formed powers of the Word of God: for the human mind is a counterpart to the eternal power of God.

3. For all senses come from the Mind, and from the senses come right thoughts, as a conclusion or Imagination, from which delight arises; this delight goes into a being, from which the sensible desire arises, and from that, the work.

4. Likewise, God is also the eternal Mind, as the Understanding; and yet there would be no distinction within Him if He did not flow out from Himself. His outflows are the powers, just as the senses are in man, and the powers lead themselves into an Imagination, wherein stands the <term>Angelic Idea</term> <note>original: "Englische Idea." In the German of this period, "Englisch" frequently means "Angelic" (pertaining to angels/Engel) rather than the English language.</note>, and the Imagination leads itself into delight toward sensibility—that is, the **Yes**—and the delight leads itself into desire, which is the ground of one's own acceptability, as the **No**; and the desire leads itself into properties, even unto the fire, out of which the light springs. Thus, these same properties of desire are Divine thoughts, to be understood in two centers, as in the **Yes** and the **No**.

5. The **Yes** is Divine, and the **No** is the selfhood of nature, as the sensibility of desire. This desire of sensibility has become a work, namely the angels, who are nothing other than thoughts of God according to Love and Wrath, as a revelation of His Mind or Will.

6. Not that there are thoughts *in* God, but rather in His outflowing, desiring will, which introduces itself into fire and light for the sake of sensibility.

7. And just as a man governs the world and all beings with his thoughts, so God, as the eternal Unity, governs all things through the office of the angels: yet the power and the working are God's alone; they are His instruments with which He delights and moves Himself, through which and with which He reveals the eternal powers and wonders, and leads them into a Love-play <note>original: "Liebe-Spiel." Böhme uses this term to describe the harmonious, non-competitive interaction of divine forces.</note>.

8. They are altogether but strings in the great harmony of the Divine Kingdom of Joy, in the song and sound of the pow-

### Fragment 6: Contemplation of Divine Revelation

...and are altogether workers of God’s wonders, acting as the designers of the powers, the Holy Names of God.

9. Just as we humans form the powers of our *senses* <note>original Latin: "Sensuum"</note> within our mouths and give them shape as audible words, so too is their work a pure formation of Divine powers and forms.

10. For whatever they will and desire is brought into form and shape through their <term>Imagination</term> <note>In this context, "Imagination" is not mere fantasy, but the creative power of the mind to pull spiritual energy into a defined shape or "idea."</note>. These forms are pure ideas, in the same way that the Divine powers formed themselves into such ideas before the creation of the angels; their work is an imitation of this.

11. And herein stands the Holy <term>Kabbalah</term> <note>referring to the mystical interpretation of the structure of God and creation</note> of changes, and the great kingdom of joy, in which Divine wisdom and science are shaped and formed through the central fire-spirits and light-spirits. Such a joy of knowledge exists within it that, out of great joy and recognition, they eternally bow and humble themselves before such majesty, so that the **No** does not gain dominion over them and they are not robbed of such honor and glory.

12. Their nourishment is a drawing-forth, or an indrawing desire for the Unity of God. From this, their central fire receives a <term>Balsam</term> <note>A healing, cooling spiritual essence that tempers the harshness of the "fire" or wrath.</note> so that the wrath does not awaken. Therefore, they also live eternally in composed humility, so that the **No** does not arise, as happened with the devils; the fall of Lucifer stands before them as a mirror.

13. Thus, understand us rightly: The entire creation of the inner and outer world (both in the Holy, pure element and in the four elements) is a pure shaping and forming of Divine powers, but according to both central fires, namely in **Yes** <note>original: "Jah." A play on the German "Ja" (Yes) and the Hebrew divine name "Jah."</note> and **No**. One outflow has simply proceeded from the other, down to the very coarsest matter or compaction of earth and stones.

14. For the visible world is only the outflowing Word, with the two central fires, which have again made for themselves a *subject* <note>original Latin: "Subjectum." Meaning a physical vessel or underlying matter.</note> with the outer elemental fires, in which the external creatures live.

15. The more inward one can come into the power of a thing, the closer one comes to the Godhead, as can be understood in metals and growing plants, as well as in all living things. For the outermost part is the four elements; the other...

...next is the Astral Body; the third is the Quintessence <note>original Latin: "Quinta Essentia," referring to the "fifth essence" or the purest spiritual substance beyond the four physical elements</note>, as the foundation of the flowed-forth Holy Element; the fourth is the Tincture <term>Tincture</term> <note>In theosophical thought, a "Tincture" is the highest spiritual power or life-force that permeates and "colors" reality, uniting the fires of wrath and love into a single expression.</note>, as the highest power of the flowed-forth Word, within which both inner Central Fires lie in one Subject <note>original Latin: "Subjecto"</note>: and through this, God in His pure clarity is understood.

16. If only we would once wake up from our Adamic slumber <note>The state of spiritual "sleep" or material consciousness that followed the Fall of Adam, blinding humanity to the spiritual world.</note>, and look around us for once, we might well see wonders; if the earth were not so dear to us, we might well see Heaven—this is understood well enough by our own.

17. Thus, this is the conclusion regarding the angels' deeds: that they are playfellows of the Divine Kingdom of Joy, and are members and branches of the great trees of Divine Names, upon which the Heavenly fruits grow. They receive their nourishment from the sap of their trees, just as every angel does from his throne; and as the throne is—namely, the Name of God—so also is the office of those same angels, but the entire tree is God.

18. The wise pagans understood the subject <note>original Latin: "Subjectum"</note> as the counterpart of such thrones and honored them as gods, but they still lacked the true foundation of inwardness. Among the Christians, however, it has become quite silent, except for a few to whom God has revealed it, who have kept it secret in a parabolic manner <note>using parables, metaphors, or riddles to hide deep truths from the unworthy</note>.

## The Seventh Question.

# What moved Lucifer to lust against God and turn away from the Good?

->Summaries<-

1. Out of the **No**, as self-gratification, the devil was moved; 2. for he did not want to be yielded <note>referring to *Gelassenheit*, the essential mystical practice of surrendering the self-will to God</note>; 3. his throne moved him; 4. and in the creation of the angels, the properties were powerfully at work. 5. When the self-will broke away from the Unity, 6. and wished to rule with the **No** over the **Yes**; 7. and immediately the properties became manifest in him.

It explains that the Fall occurred when the angelic self-will (the "No") attempted to dominate the divine unity (the "Yes").</summary>

### Frag. 7. Consideration of Divine Revelation

1. Answer: His own self-appropriation <note>German: *Annehmlichkeit*. In this context, it refers to the ego's desire to take things for itself rather than remaining in divine harmony.</note>, acting as the "No," moved him; the flowed-forth will in the Central Fire of eternal nature is the cause of it. This will surged up within him and desired to form the Divine Power into the Power of Fire.

2. He desired to test the quality of eternal nature and did not wish to stand in surrender <term>Gelassenheit</term> <note>A foundational concept in German mysticism meaning a state of yieldedness or letting go of the self-will to allow God to act through the soul.</note>, but wanted to rule in and with the Holy Name of the throne.

3. The cause, however, which moved him to such desire is the throne in which he was a prince. He indeed remains there eternally according to the quality of the Fire-Power, but he did not remain within it according to the Holy Name of the Light-Power; instead, he remains according to the darkness.

4. When the movement toward the creation of the angels occurred, the flowed-forth will of self-appropriation rose up. The qualities stood in great activity and desired to become creaturely <note>To have a distinct, independent existence as a "creature" or individual being.</note>.

5. Lucifer’s creaturely will drew from these qualities; as he recognized the omnipotence within them and found the wonder-working power in himself, his creaturely will rose up according to the power of the fire and misused the Holy Name within him. He did not want to remain in surrender, but wanted to rule over the thrones and broke himself away from the Unity.

6. He wanted to rule with the "No" over the "Yes" <note>In this theology, the "Yes" is the divine light and affirmation, while the "No" is the dark, necessary center of nature that provides contrast and form.</note>, for the "No" had raised itself within him and despised the "Yes." Because the power for distinction and form resided in the "No," the creaturely will in the "No"—as if in a state of transformation—wanted to rule, and broke away from **God’s** Unity, entering into the self-appropriation of the qualities.

7. Immediately the qualities became manifest in him, such as the cold fire, likewise <note>original Latin: "item"</note> the sharpness, harshness, hardness, bitterness, stinging, enmity, anxiety, and the torment of the fire; thus he became an enemy of all love, humility, and gentleness, for the foundation of **God’s** wrath captured the false will.

## The 8th Question.

# How could an angel become a devil, or what is a devil? In what essence and being does he exist after the fall?

### Summaries.

God’s Holy Name, or Light-Power, is not fashioned into shapes; §. 1. rather, the devil broke himself off from God’s will; 2. so then the **No** remains in him as a creaturely thing, 3. and a painful fire-essence. 4. In such a state, the devil cannot become an angel. 5. He flees from love and lives only in wrath; 6. he indeed has remorse, but no faith, 7. and curses God's power. 8. For his own self-will has swung itself upward and has forfeited the Divine Idea. 9. 10. However, the devils are of varying qualities; 11. for every Divine power has a contrary <note>original: "Contrarium"</note>, 12. 13. which becomes manifest through the separation. 14.

1. Answer. It is not to be understood that the Holy Name, in which Lucifer was a Throne-Angel, turned into a devil within him: much less the Central Love-Fire, acting as the Light-Power; no, that cannot be.

2. For when Lucifer directed his desire into his own power, he broke himself off from the will of God, and so the Holy Name separated from him, and the light in his fire-life was extinguished; for he broke himself off from the Unity, which is a balm to the fire, in which the fire receives its light-shimmer.

3. Thus, only the **No** <note>In this mystical context, the "No" represents the negative, dark, or contracting principle of existence, as opposed to the divine "Yes."</note> remained in him as a formed creature, and the **Yes** <note>original: "Jah." Likely a play on the Hebrew "Yah" (God) and the German "Ja" (Yes), representing the affirmative divine life.</note> departed from him; for the **No** separated itself from the **Yes** into its own will, and did not want to be under the **Yes** (as under the Divine Breathing <term>Hauchen</term> <note>Refers to the emanation or "breathing out" of God's Word into creation.</note> of Unity), but wanted to be its own breathing.

4. Thus he remained a pure cold, sharp, hard, pointed, bitter, stinging, poisonous, anxious, painful fire-essence, in which the Central Fire stands in nothing but strife, hunger, and thirst, and can reach no refreshment.

5. For if Lucifer were to become an angel again, he would have to draw again from God's Unity and Love, and this fire-life would have to be put to death by love and changed into humility <term>Demuth</term>; this, the hellish foundation...

<summary>This page explores the metaphysical mechanics of Lucifer's fall, explaining that he became a devil by severing his will from the divine Unity (the "Yes"), leaving him with only a painful, lightless "fire-essence."</summary>

(in the devils) cannot do, and can no longer do, for there is no longer any desire or longing in any of them for humility or repentance.

6. Their whole life is nothing other than the hellish foundation, a wellspring of **God's** wrath, sheer poison and stench, and a dying torment. When they hear of love and humility, they flee from it, for love is the death of their false life.

7. They certainly have an eternal regret for their lost inheritance, as for that which is good, but they can have no faith that they might attain grace; instead, eternal doubt is their faith.

8. They have been separated from **God**: therefore they curse or flee from **God's** power and will. It is an unbearable thing to them, for the holy power of **God** would weaken them if they were touched by it, for it kills the self-will, and the self-will does not want that, for it would lose its power and might.

9. Thus **Lucifer**, who was a Prince of Thrones, abandoned the holy name within him, and his self-will swung itself upward, as the creature <note>That is, he acted as an independent being rather than an instrument of God.</note>—understand the <term>Central Fire-Life<note>In this philosophy, 'Fire-Life' represents the raw, spiritual energy and drive of a being before it is tempered by Divine Light.</note></term>—according to the properties of eternal nature.

10. Thus he forfeited the Divine Image, as the **Idea** <note>original: "Ideam." This refers to the eternal, spiritual archetype or blueprint of the soul in the mind of God.</note>, so that it has become mute and inactive, and is like a withered tree without Divine power, a figure without movement, of which he is eternally ashamed; for an Angelic character still stands within him <note>Meaning the memory or vestige of his original form remains as a reminder of what he lost.</note>, and yet he is no longer in Angelic kind and form. He has completely lost the image of **God** and has become like the poisonous worms and beasts whose life exists in poison.

11. The devils have received such a deformity, each according to their property, for their properties are entirely different, all according to the foundation of the hellish essence. They also have their Princely regimes among themselves, all according to their properties, such as: devils of pride, devils of greed, devils of envy, devils of wrath, devils of lies, devils of sorcery, and many other such kinds, acting as a **contrary** <note>original: "Contrarium."</note> against **God’s** wisdom and truth.

12. Every Divine good power has a **contrary** <note>original: "Contrarium."</note> in the hellish foundation, as in the **No**, so that the **Yes** <note>original: "Jah." This is a linguistic play on the German "Ja" (Yes) and the Hebrew "Yah" (a name for God), representing the affirmative, creative power of the Divine.</note>

18. Theosophical Questions. Question 9.
<meta>catchword: they</meta>

as the Truth, is recognized: And thus the darkness, as the foundation of **God’s** wrath, has also become figurative <note>That is, the darkness became a visible or manifested representation of the hidden wrath.</note>.

13. But when one asks: How is it possible that from a beautiful angel a hideous, fierce devil should emerge?

14. This happens through the two eternal <term>Central Fires<note>Böhme envisions two core energies within every being: a "dark fire" of raw existence and a "light fire" of divine love. Normally they are united, but if separated, the dark fire becomes a hellish torment.</note></term>, in which the will stands within the properties; as soon as one separates from the other, it is already done: for it happens even with humans, that a good person goes astray and is ruined.

### The 9th Question.

->Since **God** is Almighty, why did<-
->He not resist **Lucifer**,<-
->and allow such a thing to happen?<-

### Summaries.

**God** does not desire the devil, just as the devil does not desire **God**. §. 1. The devil did not want [to remain], even though **God** did want [him to]. 2. Now Love and Wrath have an eternal will; 3. (though Love indeed transforms Wrath into Love, 4. yet it does not resist it with force: ) 5. and the "No" <note>The "No" refers to the rebellious, self-centered rejection of the Divine "Yes."</note> in the devil has gone into its own kingdom, 6. which happened in the creature’s own will; 7. so that consequently not **God**, but the <term>Selfhood<note>original: "Selbheit." This refers to the egoistic state of being turned inward toward oneself rather than outward toward God.</note></term> of the Fall is to blame; 8. which its own <term>Grimness<note>original: "Grimm." This denotes a fierce, burning, and structural fury or bitterness inherent in the foundation of nature.</note></term> has hardened. 9. Reason is blind in this matter. 10.

1. **Answer.** When fire and light once separate, there is a great enmity toward one another; just as water and fire are enemies, and neither desires the other anymore, for one is a death to the other: but because they stand together in a growing life, in **One** foundation, they love each other and stand together in great joy. So it is also to be thought regarding the devil and **God**: **God** no longer desires the devil, and the devil no longer desires **God**.

2. But when one asks why **God** did not prevent that in the movement? We say: **God** had given him His love and warned him against it, just as He warned Adam; but the <term>Central eternal Fire-Will</term>, acting as the wrath of **God**, would not [yield]; it separated itself in its own way within them.

3. Here one must distinguish between **God’s** Love and Wrath:

<summary>This page explores why an omnipotent God allowed Lucifer to fall, explaining that God's love does not use force to override the "Fire-Will" or selfhood of a creature. It describes the ontological separation of fire and light as the mechanism of the fall, where the creature's own "No" creates its own isolated kingdom.</summary>
