### Explanation.

9. The word **ADONAI** <note>Hebrew: "Lord." Böhme uses the letters of the name to map out the internal dynamics of God's self-revelation.</note> indicates the opening or self-movement of the <term>bottomless</term> <note>original: "ungründlichen," referring to the *Ungrund*, the primal nothingness or groundless void from which God emerges.</note> eternal Unity—what the eternal begetting, opening, and outflowing of the Trinity of God is within Himself.

The **(A)** is a threefold **I**, which encompasses itself in the form of a cross, acting as a beginning, an entrance, and an exit.

The **(D)** is the movement of the threefold **I**, acting as the opening force.

The **(O)** is the **circumference** <note>The outer boundary or circle of the divine influence.</note> of the threefold **I**, being the birth of the "place" of God within Himself.

The **(N)** is the threefold Spirit, which proceeds from the circumference, out of itself as a threefold **I**.

The lower **(A)** is the **counter-response** <note>original: "Gegenwurf." A central concept in Böhme’s thought, meaning the "mirroring" or "objectification" of the Divine will that allows it to perceive itself.</note> or the working of the threefold **I** or Spirit, from which eternal movement, power, colors, and virtues arise or originate.

The **(I)** is the substantial outflow of the threefold **I**, where the Trinity flows out within the Unity; and in this entire word **ADONAI**, one understands the eternal life of the Unity of God.

10. The word **Father** is the eternal beginning of the working and willing in the threefold **I** of the Unity.

11. The word **Son** is the effect produced by the Power, as the comprehensibility of the Will, within which the threefold Spirit closes itself as a place of the Divine **Selfhood** <note>original: "Ichheit." The realization of individual identity or "I-ness" within the Divine essence.</note>.

<meta>catchword: 12. The</meta>

<summary>This page provides a mystical linguistic analysis of the name ADONAI, using its individual letters to describe the internal movement of God from a groundless Unity into a Trinity. It establishes how the Divine Will creates a "counter-response" to perceive itself, leading to the manifestation of power, color, and wisdom.</summary>
<keywords>ADONAI, Trinity, Unity, Father, Son, Spirit, Science, Circumference, Selfhood, Counter-response, Ungrund</keywords>

--- Page 74 ---
<meta>This page continues the detailed glossary and explanation of the "Tables of the Principles" from page 59. Having explored the letters of the divine name, Böhme now defines the specific metaphysical terms used in his mystical system, such as Spirit, Power, and Will.</meta>

60
<header>XIX. Tables of the Principles. Schema.</header>

12. The word **Spirit** is the living, outgoing movement within the contained power, which can be understood through the likeness of a flower. The opening or active growth is the beginning: the power of the acting is the enclosure and bodily casing of the growth; and the scent, which goes out from the power, is the movement or the growing, outgoing life-of-joy of the power, from which the flower springs. In this, one sees a likeness of how the birthing of Divine power takes its form.

13. The word **Power** <note>original: "Kraft." In Böhme’s thought, this is the "juice" or potential energy within the divine being, similar to the sap in a plant.</note> points to the breathing, outgoing, intelligible, and sensory life, acting as the ground and fountain of the out-flowing knowledge of differentiation.

14. The word **Colors** points to the <term>Object</term> <note>original: "Objectum oder Gegenwurf." Böhme uses these terms to describe how the unified Light of God reflects off a "counterpart" to produce the variety of the visible and spiritual world, much like light hitting a prism.</note> of the power, where the differentiation and origin of the sensory life of understanding is perceived, and where an eternal contemplation originates.

15. The word **Will** points to the willing or moving within the opening Unity, by which the Unity wills itself into a Trinity—as the "Nothing" wills itself into its own "Something," wherein it has its accomplishment and desire.

16. The word **Delight** <note>original: "Lust." This refers to the divine pleasure or "longing" that initiates creation, rather than carnal desire.</note> points to the actual perceptibility of the will or willing, acting as the highest ground of the original Love; here, the will of the <term>bottomless</term> <note>original: "Ungrundes," the primal void or nothingness before God’s self-manifestation.</note> perceives itself within its "Something," surrendering itself to that Something as its own perception, and acting and willing within that sensitivity in its own taste.

17. The word **Science** <note>original: "Scientz." As noted previously, this is the internal "attraction" or pre-conscious knowledge within the Divine Will.</note> points to the actual, perceptible knowledge and understanding within the taste of Love; it is a root of the five senses and a ground of eternal life, from which understanding wells up and wherein the eternal Unity establishes its foundation.

18. The word **Word** points to how the eternal Love of the perceptible Unity, together with Science, speaks itself out eternally into an Object; thus, this same Word is the speaking-out or breathing of the Will from the Power through the understanding. It is the driving and forming of the eternal (unified) Power into infinity and multiplicity, acting as the creator of power out of the eternal power into <term>virtues</term> <note>original: "Tugenden." Meaning inherent powers or medicinal-like efficacies.</note>.

19. The word **Wisdom** is the out-flowed Word, as

It defines the mechanics of how the divine Unity manifests as a perceptible Trinity and a multi-colored creation.</summary>

...a counterpart <note>original: "Gegenwurf." This refers to a reflection or an object through which God's hidden nature becomes visible or "knowable."</note> of Divine Knowledge and Divine Will, as the essential power of the great love of God, from which all things have received their movement and possibility: a ground of all three principles; a revelation of the unity of God: a passive being of Divine activity and the ground of humility; a mother of all knowledges of the creatures, and an eternal casing of the working love of God, a ray and breath of the almighty spirit.

20. The word **Jehovah** is the most holy name of God, as the Divine, sensory life, the One Good, within which the Holy Trinity is understood with glory and omnipotence; a life of the <term>Ungrund</term> <note>original: "Ungrund." Often translated as the "Abyss" or "Groundless," it refers to the absolute nothingness or pure potentiality of God before any creation or manifestation.</note> as the Unity, which primarily stands in the eternal (single) love; and within it the most holy name **Jesus** is understood as the out-flowing **I**: a ground and fountain of the breathing of the Unity of God: a formation of the understanding, for the outflow of the Unity leads itself with the **I** into the **E** as into a vision or seeing of a chaos, within which the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." A term borrowed from Paracelsus, referring to the "great chaos" or the spiritual "mother" of all things from which the world was formed.</note> is understood after the Divine manner; and it is a triple breathing of the power.

21. **JE** is a breathing of the Unity, and the **HO** is a breathing of the **JE**, and the **VA** is a breathing of the **HO**, and yet it is only One breathing, and it makes a triple exit of three centers or containers: and we understand therein how the triple **I** finally closes itself into the **A** as into a beginning for nature.

22. Beneath that stands **Life**, which indicates that this triple breathing is a pure life and power. And beneath that stands **Virtue**, <note>original: "Tugend." In the 17th century, this word meant "efficacy" or "power" (from the Latin "virtus") rather than just moral behavior.</note> which indicates the immeasurable power of such breathing life.

23. In this table or schema, it is now rightly understood what God is outside of nature and creature in <term>Trinity</term>, as in a triple breathing-out of the Unity into itself, where one cannot speak of a place or site of his dwelling, nor of any measurability or divisibility; for he is neither here nor there, but everywhere at once; just as one considers the <term>Ungrund</term> to be the eternal Unity outside of nature and creature, so he is an actual power and being of the Unity.

<meta>This page continues the explanation of the first "Tetragrammaton" table, transitioning from the foundational properties of the "Dark World" to the interplay between the two primary Principles: the Darkness (the root of nature) and the Light (the revelation of God's love). It begins a mystical deconstruction of the word "Tincture" as it relates to these divine properties.</meta>

<header>Plate 1. Explanation of the Plates of the 3 Principles.</header>

27. But in eternity, there is thus an eternal light and darkness within one another. The darkness is the ground of nature, and the light is the ground of the joyful realm of Divine Revelation: Thus the dark world—as the ground of the properties of one's own desire and will—is called the first <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium." In Böhme's system, a "Principle" is one of the three fundamental layers of reality: the dark world of God's wrath, the light world of God's love, and the visible world of our universe.</note>, because it is a cause of Divine Revelation according to sensibility, and it also makes a kingdom of its own within itself, as painful torment; according to which God calls Himself an angry and jealous God, and a consuming fire. And the light, which becomes manifest in the fire, wherein the unity of the Divine outflow of love is understood, is called the second <term>Principle</term>, as the Divine Power-World, wherein God’s love is a love-fire and actual life; as it is written: God dwells in a light to which no one can come; for the power of the unity of God works in the light, and that same power is God. But the fiery nature within the light is the eternal nature, wherein the eternal love of the Unity loves and feels itself.

28. Beneath the first and second <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principio."</note> there stand seven numbers (I. II. III. IV. V. VI. VII.) in the seven <term>spaces</term> <note>original: "Spatiis."</note>; these signify the seven properties of eternal nature. And beneath them stands <term>TINCTURE</term> <note>original: "TINCTVR." To Böhme, the Tincture is a spiritual "medicine" or transformative power that transmutes the harshness of the dark world into the beauty of the light world.</note> divided into the seven spaces; this signifies the Divine Word, a <term>temperament</term> <note>original: "Temperament." This refers to a state of perfect balance where no single quality dominates the others, resulting in harmony.</note> or equal property, wherein the Divine powers lie within an equal will, work, and essence, as the outflowing Name of God. Within this name, the great mystery of Divine power and operation is understood, along with the characters of the letters in their distribution among the seven properties.

29. The word <term>TINCTURE</term> is the distinctive word <note>The word that separates or differentiates the Unity into many forms.</note> from which the seven properties flow. The letter T is the "Tau" <note>The letter Tau (T) often represents the cross or a gateway in mystical traditions.</note> or the opening-up of the Unity, as the † of the threefold I, a ground for breathing <note>original: "Hauchen." This refers to the divine breath or the process of the Spirit moving out of the silence.</note>. I is the outflow from the T or the exit of the Unity, as the <term>cross-hinge</term> <note>original: "Creuz-Angel." A mystical term describing the "pivot point" or axis where the spiritual world turns into the natural world.</note> of life. N is the outflow of the sounding, threefold Spirit. C is the cutting-asunder of the sounding, where the I, as the outflow of the Unity, separates itself again from the darkness and breaks the pleasantness of the eternal will. The second T under Number V is the Holy Tau or the opening-up of Glory in the fiery sensibility with the burning love, as that by which God’s kingdom opens itself; and it signifies the great power of the light-force.

XIX. Table of Principles.

# The 1st Plate.
## THE TETRAGRAMMATON <note>original: "TETRAGRAMMATON." This refers to the sacred four-letter Name of God in Hebrew (YHWH). In this mystical context, it signifies the unfolding of the Divine Being into the created universe.</note>.

In this table, the outflow of the eternal Divine Word is considered: how the Work, through Wisdom, introduces itself from Unity into multiplicity and differentiation, into both Light and Darkness. Through this process, God calls Himself an angry, jealous God and a consuming fire, as well as a dear, merciful God. Within this, one understands the foundation of the angels and souls, and how they may receive either salvation or damnation. In the horizontal <term>spaces</term> <note>original: "Spatiis."</note>, the seven properties of Eternal Nature are understood as the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." For Böhme, this is the "chaos" or potentiality out of which all things are manifested.</note>. Up to the fourth line, at the Fire, the first <term>Principle</term> is understood as the Darkness and the causes of movement and the "Painful Life." From the Fire up to the seventh property, the other <term>Principle</term> is understood as the Angelic World; and in the vertical spaces, the various properties of life are understood.

<header>XIX. Table of Principles. Table 1.</header>

24. That such power and virtue of Unity is truly understood therein, is grasped through His outflowing power in the world and creatures, which is born from His breathing out; and there is no thing in the essence of this world that does not bear witness to this, if one observes it closely.

## Explanation of the First Table:
# TETRAGRAMMATON <note>original: "TETRAGRAMMATON." This refers to the four-letter Hebrew name of God (YHWH), signifying the divine essence unfolding into the world.</note>.

25. In this table, it is presented how the Holy Name of eternal power—along with knowledge and science from eternity to eternity—introduces itself into Nature as eternal Light and Darkness in various properties; how the Word of the "breathing out" introduces itself into an *Objectum* or <term>counter-stroke</term> <note>original: "Gegenwurf." A central concept in Böhme’s thought, meaning a reflection or resistance that allows the divine will to perceive itself.</note>, and how within that counter-stroke, a self-will and the "acceptability" of the properties arises. Within this, one always understands two essences: first, God's own outflow, and then the properties' own "acceptability" within free will. In this "acceptability," a counter-stroke of an external kind is again understood, so that the Unity becomes increasingly external in its outflow. Through this, eternal Love introduces itself into sensitivity and a fire-flaming manner, acting as a working of Divine powers.

26. Above the table stands God's Wrath, the Dark World, and beneath it the first <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium." One of the three fundamental layers of reality in Böhme's system: the dark fire-world, the light world, and the physical world.</note>; and opposite, from the fourth number to the seventh, stands God's Love, the Light-World, and beneath it the second Principle. This indicates how the outflowing Will encloses and overshadows itself with the "acceptability" of its own desire, introduces itself into properties with the desire of the "Selfhood" <note>original: "Ichheit." The ego or individualizing drive that leads to darkness if not surrendered.</note>, and turns into Darkness. There, the outflowing "One" becomes manifest and perceptible in the Darkness through the fire in the Light, and is a cause of the Light. In this Light, God's Love takes on a fiery effect from the fire of eternal Nature, and shines out through the dark, painful "acceptability" within the fire—like a light from a candle, or like day in the night. From this, Day and Night also received their original foundation, ground, and names in Time.

<meta>catchword: 27. But</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>63</page-num>
<header>Table 1. Explanation of the Tables of the 3 Principles.</header>

27. But in Eternity, there is thus an eternal Light and Darkness within one another. The Darkness is the ground of Nature, and the Light is the ground of the joy-filled Divine revelation. Thus, the Dark World—as the ground of the properties of one's own desire and will—is called the first Principle, because it is a cause of Divine revelation according to sensitivity, and it also makes a realm of its own within itself, as painful torment. According to this, God calls Himself an angry and jealous God, and a consuming fire. And the Light which becomes manifest in the fire, wherein the Unity of the Divine outflow of Love is understood, is called the other Principle, as the Divine Power-World. There, God’s Love is a Love-Fire and an actual life, as it is written: *God dwells in a light to which no one can come* <note>A reference to 1 Timothy 6:16.</note>, for the power of the Unity of God works in the Light, and that same power is God. But the fiery manner in the Light is the eternal Nature, wherein the eternal Love of Unity loves and perceives itself.

28. Under the first and second Principle, in the seven spaces, stand seven numbers (I. II. III. IV. V. VI. VII.). These indicate the seven properties of eternal Nature, and beneath them stands TINCTUR <note>original: "TINCTUR." A term from alchemy used by Böhme to describe the spiritual "dye" or "essence" that transforms and brings life to matter.</note> divided across the seven spaces. This indicates the Divine Word, a <term>temperament</term> <note>original: "Temperament." A state of perfect balance where no single property dominates another.</note> or equal property, wherein the Divine powers lie within an equal will, work, and essence, as the outflowing Name of God. Within this, the Great Mystery of Divine power and work is understood, along with the characters of the letters in their distribution across the seven properties.

29. The word TINCTUR is the "separating word" from which the seven properties flow. The letter **T** is the *Tau* or the opening of the Unity, like the **+** <note>The cross symbol.</note> of the threefold **I**, a ground for the breathing. **I** is the outflow from the **T** or the exit of the Unity, as the "cross-pivot" of life. **N** is the outflow of the sounding threefold Spirit. **C** is the dividing of the sounding, where the **I** (as the outflow of the Unity) separates itself again from the Darkness and breaks the "acceptability" of the eternal will. The second **T** under number **V.** is the Holy *Tau* or the opening of Glory in the fiery sensitivity with fiery Love, by which God’s Kingdom opens itself; it indicates the great power of the Light-force.

<header>XIX. Tables of Principles. Table I.</header>

The character of the Holy Spirit is now shown with three points; two pointing upward signify fire and light, and the third pointing downward signifies the Unity in Love, which is humility. With the letter **R**, the holy fire and light are grasped into an actual natural being; for it indicates the Kingdom as the Throne, and it is hereby shown how the Holy Name, with the outflowing will, introduces itself into the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhme's thought, this is the "chaos" or womb of all possibilities from which the world is created.</note> as into the eternal secret from which the visible world has sprouted.

->The Great Mystery of the Tincture, or the highest ground of the Trinity of God.<-

30. **T** Is the triple **I**, indicating the Father. <note>In early modern German, 'I' and 'J' were often interchangeable; here the 'I' likely represents the vertical stroke of the divine presence.</note>
**I** Is the born **I**, which is Jesus.
**N** Is the triple **I** in the Spirit.
**C** Indicates Christ.
**T** In the fifth space is the Father in Christ.
**U** Is the Spirit of Christ in the Word, which makes one alive.
**R** Is the Royal Throne, around which Light and Darkness contend, where Satan and Christ stand against one another (namely, according to the <term>acceptability</term> <note>original: "Annehmlichkeit." This refers to how a will "receives" or "pleases" itself within a certain property.</note> of one's own will, Satan acts as the wandering spirit; and according to the Unity, Christ acts), where one understands Love and Wrath in a single ground, but in two different revelations; understood here by our own who belong to God, but for others, this place remains a locked castle.

->The Great Mystery of the Tincture, or the greatest and highest ground of the Trinity of God.<-

31. This table in seven spaces is the foundation of the angels and souls, acting as the Great Mystery of transformation where all possibilities lie within.

Up until the fire, from which the light becomes manifest, the First <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium." ...they give it; but the first form rules therein, and retains the Supreme Government.

33. Where **No. 1** stands as **Desire** or **Enclosure**, one understands that the Desire is magnetic, and it encloses and darkens itself, which is the ground of both eternal and temporal darkness. From such a drawing-in comes Sharpness, Astringency, and Hardness, which is a <term>Primal Source</term> <note>original: "Urstand." Böhme uses this to describe the foundational state or "original standing" of a thing before it takes physical form.</note> of the <term>Wrath</term> <note>original: "Grimm." This refers to the raw, fierce energy of divine power when it is not tempered by light or love.</note> from which the great eternal death originates. For this Magnet draws the power into itself and shuts it up within itself, so that all active working stands still and enters into impotence, as can be seen downward under No. 1.

34. Under **No. 2** stands **Scienz** <note>original: "Scienz." Derived from the Latin *scientia*, Böhme uses this to mean a "knowing" or "stirring" of the will—the internal friction of the mind's desire.</note> or **Drawing**; this is the second form of nature, being the movement of the magnetic drawing-in, from which the sensitivity of nature originates. It is the ground of all <term>contrariety</term> <note>original: "Contrarietät." The necessary opposition or conflict between forces that allows life and consciousness to be felt.</note>, for hardness and movement are enemies; the movement breaks the hardness apart again, yet it also gives birth to the hardness through its attraction.

35. Thus, two essences originate in the desirous, out-flowed will of God: the drawing of the magnetic power gives movement and sensitivity, and that which is drawn gives the essence, in which one understands the causes of Spirit and Body. In the drawing of sensitivity, one understands the Spirit, and in that which is drawn, one understands the Body, or the cause of physicality.

36. Now, if such drawing-in and essence cannot reach the Light of the Unity of God, by which it would be softened, it remains within itself a vain enmity, and is a torment of raging and rising up, from which self-pleasing and pride originate. For the will of self-pleasing is false and a constant breaker of itself, that is, of its own being. In these two forms—Desire and Drawing—one understands God’s Wrath in its out-flowing properties. And although they are the ground of sensitive life, if the Light shines into them, they instead become the **Kingdom of Joy**, as an inner movement of the Unity of God and a ground of the five senses, from which creaturely life also took its beginning. Within this also stands its corruption, should it lose the Light; for then it is the torment of hellish anguish, as the cause...

of painfulness, and is yet also the root of natural life.

37. Under No. 3 stands the third form of nature, called **Anguish** <note>original: "Angst." In Böhme's system, this is a technical term for the agonizing tension or "compression" created when the first two forces (contraction and expansion) collide, forming a "whirling" sensation.</note>, as a spiritual <term>sulfur-torment</term> <note>original: "Schwefel-Qual." Böhme uses the alchemical term for sulfur to represent the "burning" or "seething" quality of the soul's inner fire.</note> according to its property. This takes its ground from the first and second forms: namely, (1) from the magnetic desire, and (2) from the motion of drawing-in, wherein the outflowed eternal will stands in unrest and in anguish. This Anguish is the cause of the natural will, the mind, and the senses; it is the **Wheel of Life**, serving as the cause of the fiery life.

38. For when the outflowed will of the **Unity of God** stands in this anguish, it longs again for that Unity, as for its rest; and the Unity or Rest longs for motion and revelation. Yet, within the Unity, there could be no revelation without motion; therefore, the Divine Will flows out from itself and introduces the Divine Delight into the [outflowed] will, into desire and motion, toward a state of <term>perceptibility</term> <note>original: "Empfindlichkeit." The capacity to feel or be conscious of oneself.</note>, so that it may feel itself. Thus, two remain in One Essence: namely, the perceptible Divine Love-Delight, and the cause of that perceptibility. In this context, **God** calls Himself a "loving **God**" according to the perceptible Divine Love-Delight, and an "angry **God**" according to the cause of that perceptibility—that is, according to eternal nature.

39. And we understand that in this Anguish—so long as the Divine Light is not manifest within it—there is the hellish fire and an eternal despair and terror. There, the self-will of nature stands forever in dying torment and forever desires to separate itself from such a ground; for this reason, I call it the **little death**, because it is the eternal dying death; whereas in the "hardness" [of the first form], it is the **great death** that stands still.

40. This form, if it does not possess the light, is the fountainhead of the false mind; but if it perceives the light within itself, it is the fountainhead and ground of the sensible mind, and the true root of fire, as may be seen looking downward under No. 3.

41. The fourth form, No. 4, is the **fire** of eternal nature. Understand this as a spiritual life-fire, which originates from the constant <term>conjunction</term> <note>original: "Conjunction." A Latin alchemical term for the joining of two opposing forces.</note> or joining together of hardness and motion; understand that painfulness originates from this. However, the **fire-glance** <note>original: "Feuer-Glantz." The flash or brilliance of the fire.</note> originates from the free delight of the will,

### Explanation of the second Plate.
# MACROCOSM <note>original: "MACROCOSMUS." The "Great World" or the universe as a whole, as opposed to the Microcosm (Man).</note>

51. In this table, one understands how the hidden spiritual world has made itself visible and has created a counter-reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf." A term used by Böhme to describe how the internal spiritual reality projects itself outward into a tangible, "thrown-against" objective form.</note> for itself through its breathing-out, wherein the eternal principles <note>original: "Principia." The foundational spiritual origins of reality.</note> have flowed forth, and the powers within them have become material; for outer nature is nothing other than an outflow or counter-reflection of eternal nature.

52. The four elements originate from the first four properties of eternal nature: specifically, the Earth and the coarseness of all beings come from the dark desire, wherein the other six properties have always become material alongside it, as one may understand by the evil and the good in metals and herbs. But the dark desire has coagulated them all, as still happens today.

53. The air originates from the movement of the magnetic impression through the fire in the scattered Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio." In Böhme's alchemy, Mercury represents the principle of movement, sound, and vital expression.</note>, as the scattered motion from which the water comes.

54. The water is the scattered Mercury in which the fiery kind is killed: water is the female <note>original: "Weiblein." Literally "little woman," used here in an alchemical sense to denote the passive, receptive, or cooling principle compared to the active, "male" fire.</note> of the fiery Mercury, within which it works; from this, heat and cold, as well as thickness and thinness, are in conflict.

55. The fire originates from the spiritual fire of the inner ground. The cold is understood as being in the magnetic sharpness, which is the true root of the fire.

56. Above the seven properties over the table, it is written: **Ground of Nature**, divided into the first three forms; and in the fourth and fifth form or property, the word **Pure Element** is divided; and in the sixth and seventh form, the word **Paradise** is divided.

57. With the word **Ground of Nature**, one understands the root of the four elements, acting as the four causes of motion and sensitivity.

58. With the word **Pure Element**, one understands the temperament <note>original: "Temperamentum." A state of perfect balance or harmony where no single element or property overwhelms the others.</note> or equality of nature and the four elements, where the light transforms all properties into a single will, in which the light is also present in the sensitive, mo-

# The 2nd Plate.
## MACROCOSM <note>original: "MACROCOSMUS." The "Great World" or the universe as a whole, viewed as a reflection of the Divine.</note>

In this table it is indicated how the spiritual, eternal world, as the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhmean thought, this refers to the infinite, chaotic potential of God from which all things are expressed.</note>, was poured out through the movement of the Word of God, and became visible, audible, and material; and how creatures were created out of these properties. One should understand the inner spiritual world as being hidden within them, and how the inner forces have enclosed and formed themselves through Divine Action, so that in all things one can understand Good and Evil—even though in the Great Mystery there was no evil, rather it arose through sensitivity and the adoption of one's own desire. From this foundation all creatures, the visible ones, have come: thus it is drafted here, what flows out of each property in its working, and which property among the seven has the upper-regency, according to which a thing is formed and governed.

->68 XIX. Plates of the Principles. Plate 1.<-

Forms are merely attributes belonging to life. The fourth form is life itself, but the fifth is the true spirit; when the fifth property is revealed out of the fire, it dwells in all the others and transforms them all into its sweet love, so that no painfulness or hostility is recognized in any of them anymore, just as the day transforms the night.

47. In these first four properties, life is similar to the devils; but when the power of the light, as the second Principle <note>original: "Principium." In Böhme's system, the second principle represents the world of light and divine love, as opposed to the dark "first principle" of raw power/fire.</note>, becomes manifest in the properties, then it is an angel and lives in Divine power and holiness, as can be seen from the fifth number downwards.

48. The sixth property (No. 6) is Understanding, acting as the resonance or sound. Since the properties in the light all stand in equality, they rejoice; thus the power of the five senses <note>original: "Sensuum"</note> becomes audible, and all properties rejoice in one another, each in the other. Thus the love of the Unity leads itself into action and will, into sensation, discovery, and majesty. And thus there is a Contrary <note>original: "Contrarium." This refers to the idea that light needs darkness, and love needs a struggle, in order to be felt or "known."</note> in the eternal nature, so that properties may arise in which love may be known, and so that there may be something to love, in which the eternal love of the Unity of GOD may work, and in which the praise of GOD may occur. For when the properties of life are permeated with the Divine flame of love, they praise the great love of GOD and resign themselves all back into the Unity of GOD. Such joy and knowledge could not be revealed in the Unity if the eternal Will did not lead itself into painful, movable properties.

49. The seventh property (No. 7) is the Being or Essence <note>original: "Wesen." This refers to the substantiality or "body" that contains the other spiritual forces.</note>, in which all the others are essential, and in which they all work—like the soul in the body. Within this, one understands Nature, and also the eternal Essential Wisdom of GOD, as the Great Mystery <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." The source of all manifestation.</note>, from which foundation the visible world with its essence and creatures has sprouted.

50. Thus, with this plate, one should understand the hidden spiritual world as GOD's eternal revelation, from which the angels and the soul of man have received their origin. Because of this, they can transform themselves into Evil or into Good, for both lie in their Center <note>original: "Centro"</note>. The spiritual world is nothing other than God’s manifested Word; it has existed from eternity and remains in eternity, and within it Heaven and Hell are understood.

### Explanation of the other Plate.

## MACROCOSM <note>original: "MACROCOSMUS." The "Great World" or the physical universe.</note>

51. In this table, one understands how the hidden spiritual world has made itself visible and has created a "counter-throw" <note>an image or reflection</note> of itself through its out-breathing. In this process, the eternal Principles <note>original: "Principia"</note> are excluded, and the forces within have become material; for external nature is nothing other than an outflow or reflection of eternal nature.

52. The four Elements <note>original: "Elementa"</note> originate from the first four properties of eternal nature: specifically, the Earth and the coarseness of all beings come from the dark desire, in which the other six properties have simultaneously become material—as one may understand Good and Evil in metals and herbs. But the dark desire has coagulated them all, as still happens today.

53. The Air originates from the movement of the magnetic impression through the fire in the dispersed Mercury <note>original: "Mercurio." Here, Mercury represents the vital, moving spirit within matter.</note>, acting as the dispersed movement from which the Water comes.

54. The Water is the dispersed Mercury in which the fiery nature is "killed" <note>extinguished or calmed</note>. Water is the feminine part of the fiery Mercury that works within it; from this, heat and cold, as well as thickness and thinness, are in conflict.

55. The Fire originates from the spiritual fire of the inner foundation. Cold is understood as the magnetic sharpness, which is the true root of fire.

56. Above the seven properties on the plate stands "Foundation of Nature," divided into the first three forms. Into the fourth and fifth form or property, the words "Pure Element" <note>original: "Rein Element"</note> are divided; and into the sixth and seventh form, the word "Paradise" <note>original: "Paradeis"</note> is divided.

57. By the phrase "Foundation of Nature," one understands the root of the four elements, as the four causes of movement and sensitivity.

58. By the phrase "Pure Element," one understands the Temperament <note>original: "Temperamentum"</note> or equality of nature and the four elements, where the light transforms all properties into one single Will, wherein the light also [works] in the sensitive, mova- <note>The text cuts off mid-word; "movable" or "moving" properties is likely intended.</note>

## Explanation of the third plate.
# MICROCOSM <note>original: "MICROCOSMUS." A Greek term meaning "small world," used here to describe the human being as a miniature reflection of the entire universe.</note>
### Summaries.

Here Man is presented as an image of all 3 worlds. §. 63. The Soul is the eternal nature, 64. 65. and the Spirit, God’s Idea, 66. Through self-will <note>original: "Eigenheit." This refers to the "own-self" or ego-driven desire that separates a soul from the communal divine will.</note> however, 67. and the Devil’s deceit, 68-70. man has fallen and become a monster, 71. in that the hellish foundation, as the deluding-spirit <note>original: "Irr-Geist." The spirit of error or wandering that leads the soul away from truth.</note>, reigns in body and soul. 72. and following.

63. In this plate, Man is represented as an image of all three worlds, according to soul, spirit, and body: what he was in the beginning according to his creation; what he became in the Fall through the deluding-spirit; and what he becomes through the spirit of Christ in the New Rebirth, which is a true, substantial image out of the three <term>Principles</term> <note>The "Principles" are the three fundamental realms of existence: the Dark Fire-World (First), the Light-World (Second), and the Visible World (Third).</note> of Divine Revelation, as from the out-flowing Word of the Divine Will.

64. According to the soul, he is the eternal nature of a fiery kind, like a spark from the Center from which the fire originates; if this foundation cannot reach the Divine Light, then it is a darkness, from the magnetic, attractive, desiring power. But if it reaches the light from the fire—so that this magnetic desire eats from the out-flowing unity of the Love of God—then the true good spirit sprouts from the fire, just as the light shines from a candle.

65. These are two Principles: in the fire of eternal nature is the soul, the first Principle; and in the light of Divine power is the spirit, the second Principle. The body, however, is the third Principle, being an essence of the visible world, made into an image from the stars and elements, out of the seven properties of nature.

## MICROCOSM <note>original: "MICROCOSMUS." A Greek term meaning "small world," used here to describe the human being as a miniature reflection of the entire universe.</note> — that is, the Small World, or Man; who is a likeness of all 3 principles: according to the Soul, the Fire-World; according to the Spirit, the Light-World; according to the Body, the Air-World.

In this table, Man is presented: what he was in Paradise, when all properties stood within him in balance, without the adoption of self-desire, while he stood in the Image of God; and what he has become through Satan’s deceit and influence <note>original: "Inspicirung." Literally "breathing into" or inspection; here it implies the subtle infusion of Satanic error.</note>; what the monster <note>original: "Monstruum" or "Monstrum." In the mystical theology of Jacob Böhme, this refers to the deformed, bestial nature of fallen humanity that has lost its divine proportions.</note> of the serpent within him is, by which he became earthly and mortal; and then how the Word and Love of God came to his aid again, and rebirths him anew in Christ, daily slaying the serpent-image; and in what danger and misery he stands in such a formation, as he stands upon the foundation of both Hell and Heaven. Thus it is designed to show how he is an image of the seven properties, set into one image out of all three Principles according to eternity and time, as a likeness of Divine Revelation and Knowledge: for a greater understanding of how he should govern his life, and to which impulse he should surrender himself.

...active elemental property; thus one understands how the eternal element—as the movement of Divine power, sharpened by the ground of nature—has revealed itself in the light. This Pure Element is the movement of the inner Spiritual World and flowed into being during the creation of the world, and is understood as the <term>Quintessence<note>original: "Quinta Essentia." In alchemy and mysticism, this is the "fifth essence," a pure, celestial substance beyond the four physical elements (earth, air, fire, water).</note></term>.

59. The word **Paradise**, in the sixth and seventh properties, indicates the spiritual working within the light-essence, like a greening or spiritual growth. This spiritual essence flourished through all four elements at the beginning of the world, forming itself from the earth into all fruits, and transformed all the properties of "wrath" into a <term>balanced temperament<note>original: "Temperamentum." This refers to a state where all conflicting forces are in perfect harmony or "temperature."</note></term>. However, when the wrathful properties were awakened along with the four elements through Adam's turned-away desire and false will, gaining dominion, this "greening" fled back. That is, it remained standing in the <term>tincture<note>original: "Tinctur." A spiritual or alchemical essence that has the power to transform and "color" reality.</note></term> of the inner ground. It is indeed still within the four elements, but only in the inner, pure element, and cannot be reached except through the new rebirth of the inner man. In the material tincture, this paradisical working is also quite apparent, as understood by those of our circle.

60. This table indicates from what all beings of this world have sprung, and what the Creator may be: namely, that the Creator was the spiritual world of power, which **God** moved as the Divine Will. But the **Separator** <note>original: "Separator." The dividing or differentiating power in nature that separates one thing from another.</note> was the will that flowed out from the spiritual world; in such movement, it flowed out of itself and created an "object" or reflection of its working. In this movement, one reflection constantly flowed out from another, down to the very outermost matter of the earth.

61. This was drawn into a mass <note>original: "Massam."</note> through Divine movement, and that same drawing or movement still stands; therefore, all matter in the deep falls toward the earth. This is the reason why the power of movement remains as it is today and until the end of this time.

## Explanation of the Third Table.
# MICROCOSMUS. <note>original: "Microcosmus." Literally "the little world." In mystical thought, man is viewed as a miniature map or reflection of the entire universe (the macrocosm).</note>
### Summaries.

Here man is presented as an image of all 3 worlds.
§ 63. The Soul is the eternal nature, 64. 65. and the Spirit is GOD'S Idea, 66. But through self-interest, 67. and the Devil's deceit, 68-70. man has fallen and become a monster, 71. in that the hellish foundation, as the wandering-spirit [Irr-Geist], rules in body and soul. 72. sq.

63. In this table, man is presented as an image of all three worlds, according to Soul, Spirit, and Body: what he was in the beginning according to his creation; what he became in the Fall through the wandering-spirit; and what he becomes through the Spirit of Christ in the New Rebirth—which is a true, substantial image from the three <term>Principiis</term> of Divine Revelation, as from the outgoing Word of the Divine Will.

64. According to the Soul, he is the eternal nature of the fiery kind, like a spark from the <term>Centro<note>original: "Centro." The "Center" or point of origin from which natural forces and the properties of nature emerge.</note></term> from which the fire originates. If this ground cannot reach the Divine Light, it is a darkness, from the magnetic, attracting, desiring power. But if it reaches the Light from the fire—so that this magnetic desire eats from the outgoing unity of the Love of GOD—then the true good spirit sprouts from the fire, just as the light shines from the candle.

65. These are two <term>Principia</term>: the Soul in the fire of eternal nature is the first <term>Principium</term>, and the Spirit in the light of Divine power is the other <term>Principium</term>. The Body, however, is the third <term>Principium</term>, being an essence of the visible world, made into an image from the stars and elements out of the seven properties of nature.

### XIX. Tables of Principles. Table 3.
<meta>catchword: Proper-</meta>

66. The soul has the seven properties of the inner spiritual world according to nature, but the spirit is without properties; for it stands outside of nature in the unity of God, and yet becomes manifest in the soul through the soul's fiery nature. For it is the true image of God, as an *Idea* <note>original: "Idea." In Böhme’s thought, this is the divine archetype or the "Eternal Mirror" in which God sees His own wonders.</note>, in which God Himself works and dwells, so long as the soul leads its desire into God and surrenders itself to the will of God. But where this does not happen, this *Idea*, as the spirit, is mute and ineffective, and stands only like a faded image in a mirror, and remains without substance, as happened to Adam in the Fall. But if the soul surrenders itself to God and leads its magnetic hunger into God's love, then the soul draws Divine substance—as the substantial <term>Wisdom of God<note>original: "Weisheit GOttes." Often called Sophia, she is the mirror of God’s glory and the spiritual "corporeity" that clothes the spirit.</note></term>—into itself. Thus its *Idea* or spirit becomes substantial in the power of the Light and receives Divine life: then it is the true temple of God, wherein God's unity is active and manifest.

67. But if the soul leads itself with desire into itself, as into self-love, and turns itself with its desire into the seven properties—to test them and to eat from the pleasure of these properties—then it exalts itself and makes for itself an *Evektrum* <note>original: "Evektrum." Likely derived from the Latin "evectio," meaning an exalting or carrying out; Böhme uses it to describe a projected, false astral image of the self.</note>, as an *Astral* <note>original: "Astralischen." Referring to the "Star-Spirit" or the psychic, sidereal influences of the visible universe.</note> counter-projection. This *Evektrum* immediately hungers for the vanity of false pleasure, as happened to Lucifer and Adam, when Lucifer’s *Evektrum* formed itself into <term>Fantasy<note>original: "Phantasey." To Böhme, this is a realm of delusion and self-made shadows, separated from the divine Light.</note></term>, and the *Evektrum* of Adam’s soul formed into the animal <gloss>(or fiery)</gloss> properties of the outer world. By this, the soul was poisoned and immediately infected the body made from the *Limo* <note>original: "Limo." From the Latin "limus," referring to the "slime" or "dust of the ground" used to create Adam's physical form.</note> of the earth, so that the animal properties awoke within him and lusted after earthly, beastly food—such as heat and cold, or after harsh, bitter, sweet, and sour qualities. They led themselves with such properties into a well-spring of such pleasure and ate with desire from Good and Evil, whereby the image of God—as the *Idea*—became darkened and ineffective. Thus the true spirit, as the actual *Idea*, was mute and dead, just as an image in a mirror is dead. In this way, the soul was separated from God and stood in its own natural willing, for God’s will no longer worked in the spirit; and it began the willing of the *Evestrum* <note>original: "Evestri." A Paracelsian term for the sidereal or phantom body that mediates between the soul and the physical world.</note>, as the counter-formation of the dark and the outer world, for the Holy <term>Genius<note>original: "Genius." The indwelling divine spark or tutelary spirit that guides the soul toward God.</note></term> was transformed.

68. In this table, at the top, stands <term>Tincture<note>original: "TINCTUR." The spiritual life-force or "breath" that gives quality and vitality to all things; the transformative fire of life.</note></term>, in the seven proper-

### Table 3. Explanation of the Tables of the 3 Principles.

Properties <note>original: "Eigenschaften." In Böhme's theosophy, these are the seven foundational forces of nature that govern all existence.</note> divided; that indicates the equality of the seven properties, according to soul and body, meaning that in the first man before the Fall, the properties stood in a balanced will for the sake of distinctiveness and mutual delight, and they directed all their desires into the **Unity of God**; thus they were a true <term>Paradise</term>, for the essential Spirit was manifest within them through the **Unity of God**, and they were meant to work through all things in **God's** love.

69. But the devil begrudged them this and deceived the seven properties of life with false pleasure, and persuaded them that it would be good for them, and they would become wise, if each property led itself into its own pleasure according to its own kind; in this way, the spirit would taste and recognize what is Evil and Good. However, he did not tell them that such a state could not persist within the **Unity of God**.

70. But as they led themselves into their own pleasure, such strife and adversity awoke within them, and the properties all became figurative in their <term>Selfhood</term> <note>original: "Selbheit." This refers to the egoistic state where the creature turns away from God's unity to focus on its own individual desires.</note>; thus the Unity, which is the Element, was torn apart, and the four <term>elements</term> <note>original: "Elementa." The four worldly elements (fire, air, water, earth) which, in Böhme’s view, emerged as a result of the disruption of the one "Pure Element" of Heaven.</note> gained dominion through their struggle. Immediately, inequality fell upon them from the outside—such as heat and cold, and the constellations with their divisiveness, acting upon the body—and the <term>Wrath of God</term> according to the <term>property of the dark world</term> fell into the soul. From this came the soul's terror, anxiety, distress, and eternal despair; and in the body there awoke heat and cold, pain, sickness, and the mortal life.

71. Thus the image of **God**, the whole man, fell from his order and became a <term>monster</term> <note>original: "Monstrum."</note> and a <term>mask</term> <note>original: "Larva." This implies a hollow or distorted version of the original divine image, like a specter or a disguise.</note>, and immediately the seven properties in their ignited state began their dominion with envying, murdering, stinging, and breaking. Out of Love came <term>Pride</term> <note>original: "Hoffart."</note> and self-serving love; out of Desire came <term>Greed</term>; out of Sensitivity came <term>Envy</term>, and out of the Fire-Life came a vain, poisonous <term>Wrath</term>. Thus the <term>Foundation of Hell</term> was made manifest in the whole man and ruled in both soul and body.

72. This hellish foundation is now the <term>Spirit of Error</term>, in which man would have had to be eternally damned, to be and to remain, if Divine Grace had not immediately, after such a fall, bestowed the <term>Crusher of the Serpent</term> <note>original: "Schlangen-Treter." A reference to the "seed of the woman" from Genesis 3:15, interpreted here as Christ who overcomes the "serpent" of the fallen nature.</note> as the outflow of Divine Love in the holiest name of **JESUS**...

### XIX. Tables of Principles. Table 3.

72. ...spoken, toward a new rebirth; which Holy Name, in pure mercy and with the highest humility, surrendered itself into the human soul and body, took on humanity, and broke the power of this devilish deluding spirit. He killed the **Selfhood** <note>original: "Ichheit." This refers to the egoistic state where the creature turns away from God's unity to focus on its own individual desires.</note> of the life-will, brought the properties back into balance, united them with His love, and led them back into Divine Unity.

73. There, the true spirit, as the human **Idea** <note>original: "Idea." In Böhme's philosophy, this refers to the eternal, divine prototype or "mirror" of humanity before the Fall.</note> and the image of **God**, was renewed once more and filled with the Divine essence of love; and through Christ's soul and spirit, the human soul has received an open gate to **God** again within such love and Divine essence.

74. This is what is depicted in this table: what Adam was before the Fall, what he became in the Fall, and how he was redeemed again; what his new birth from the Spirit of Christ is. It is outlined under the word <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." An alchemical term used by Böhme to describe the spiritual "dye" or life-force that transforms and ennobles nature.</note> into the seven properties, showing in which properties the soul has its <term>Center</term> <note>original: "Centrum." The focal point of activity or origin within the soul's structure.</note>, in which the spirit, and in which the body; left for the reader to contemplate further. Below these stand the seven days of the week with their symbols, to indicate that man is exactly the same as they are.

75. This table now indicates what man is from within and without—both according to the first good Adam and according to the corrupted one—and what he has become again in Christ. Through this, one can understand how Evil and Good exist within man, and from what source Evil and Good originate in the senses and the mind.

76. By the word **Satan**, which indicates the deluding spirit, we do not mean a literal, created devil, but rather the <term>Source-Torment</term> <note>original: "Quall." A term unique to Böhme, combining the meanings of "source" (Quelle) and "torment" (Qual), referring to the inner agitation and "working" of the properties when they are out of balance.</note> of such a deluding spirit. And by the word **Christ**, the new man in the Spirit of Christ according to the inner being is understood. The other <term>Spaces</term> <note>original: "Spatia." The designated areas or sections within the diagram or table.</note> are understood as in the other tables, wherein one understands the cause of the transformation; left for the reader to contemplate further.

XX. <term>CLAVIS</term> <note>Latin for "Key." This refers to a specific work by Böhme designed to unlock the difficult terminology used in his mystical writings.</note>

->or<-

# Key.

->That is:<-

## An Explanation of the Principal Points and Words in these Writings.

### Preface of the Author to the Reader of these Writings.

->Summary.<-

*Without God's Spirit, nothing can be found. §. 1. Therefore, our undertaking must be directed toward God's glory and the benefit of our neighbor; 2. and the soul must be entirely devoted to God; 3. also, with a calm and surrendered will, one must ask God for understanding. 4. and the following.* <note>original: "sq." (sequentes), a Latin abbreviation for "and those following."</note>

It is written: "The natural man perceives nothing of what is of the Spirit, nor of the mystery of the Kingdom of God; it is foolishness to him, and he cannot grasp it." (1 Cor. 2:14). Therefore, I wish to admonish the Christian lover of mysteries, should he intend to apply himself to these high writings—to read, search, and understand them—that he should not begin to read from the outside with sharp <term>speculation</term> <note>In this context, "speculation" refers to purely intellectual reasoning or logical deduction without spiritual insight.</note> and pondering; otherwise, he will remain only in the external, figurative foundation and obtain nothing more than a "painted color" from it. For one's own reason, without God's light, cannot reach the <term>Ground</term> <note>original: "Grund." In Böhme's philosophy, the "Ground" (or "Ungrund") is the deep, essential source of all existence.</note>; it is not at all possible for it, no matter how high and subtle the <term>intellect</term> <note>original: "Ingenium."</note> may be, for it will only grasp a reflection of it. For Christ says: "Without me, you can do nothing." (John 15:5). Likewise: He is the light of the world and the life of men. (John 8:12).

2. If one wishes to search the Divine foundation, as the Divine revelation, then he should first consider for what purpose he desires to know such things, and whether he [intends to put into practice] that which he—

<summary>This page introduces Böhme's "Clavis" (Key), warning readers that intellectual rigor alone is insufficient to understand spiritual mysteries; one must instead seek divine enlightenment and approach the text with a surrendered will.</summary>
<keywords>Clavis, Key, Divine Revelation, Spirit of God, Speculation, Intellect, The Ground, Inner Light, Admonition</keywords>

--- Page 102 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface of the "Clavis" (The Key). It picks up mid-sentence from the previous page, detailing the spiritual requirements for a reader to truly understand Böhme’s mystical insights, specifically the necessity of surrendering one’s own will to God.</meta>

[high] he might desire to attain, desire to practice, and apply to God’s glory and his neighbor’s welfare: Whether he also desires thereby to die to worldliness and his own will, and to live in that which he seeks and desires, and to become one Spirit with Him.

3. Where he does not have such a purpose—namely, that if God should reveal His mysteries to him, he would want to be one Spirit and Will with Him, and surrender himself entirely to God, so that God’s Spirit may do what He will through and with him, and that God might be his knowing, willing, and working—then he is not yet fit for such knowledge and understanding. For many seek only mysteries so that they may be highly and respectably regarded before the world, only for their own benefit; but they do not come to such a <term>Ground</term> <note>original: "Grunde." In Böhme's thought, the "Ground" is the essential, spiritual source of all things.</note> where the Spirit searches all things, yes, even the depths of the Godhead, as it is written: 1 Corinthians 2:10.

4. There must only be a completely <term>surrendered</term> <note>original: "gelassener." This refers to "Gelassenheit," a central concept in German mysticism meaning a state of letting go of personal desire and self-will to be filled by God.</note> and resigned will, in which God himself searches and works; a will which constantly presses into God in surrendered humility and self-giving, and seeks nothing else than its eternal fatherland and to serve its neighbor thereby. In this way, it may well be reached. He must begin it with the work of repentance and earnest prayer, so that understanding may be opened to him from within; then the inner shall also lead itself into the outer.

5. If he reads such writings and yet cannot understand them, he should not quickly cast them aside and consider them impossible to understand. He should turn his mind to God and ask Him for grace and understanding, and read again; then he will already see more therein, until he is finally drawn into the depths through God’s power and comes into the supernatural, <term>supersensual</term> <note>Beyond the perception of the physical senses; a purely spiritual reality.</note> Ground, as into the eternal unity of God. There he will hear the unspeakable, actual words of God, which will lead him back out through the divine outflow, even to the coarsest <term>matter</term> <note>original: "Materiam," the Latin term for physical substance.</note> of the earth, and lead him back in to God. Then the Spirit of God searches all things through him and with him; and thus he is truly taught and driven by God. 1 Corinthians 2:7, 10.

<meta>catchword: 6. Because</meta>

<summary>Böhme explains that spiritual understanding requires a "surrendered" will (Gelassenheit) and a selfless desire to serve God and neighbor rather than seeking intellectual prestige. He encourages readers who find the text difficult to persist in prayer and reading until God's Spirit opens their inner understanding to the "supersensual Ground."</summary>
<keywords>Gelassenheit, surrendered will, Ground, repentance, supersensual, 1 Corinthians 2:10, divine mysteries, spiritual practice</keywords>

--- Page 103 ---
<meta>This page continues the Preface of the "Clavis" (The Key). The previous page established that the reader must be spiritually prepared and surrendered to God's will to understand the mysteries within these writings.</meta>

->of the most important points and words.<-

6. But because a <term>Key</term> <note>original: "Clavis." This refers to Böhme's work titled *The Key*, intended to unlock the difficult terminology of his earlier, more complex books.</note> or opener to my writings is desired by the lovers of these truths, I will gladly comply with their wishes and present a short composition of the foundation regarding the foreign words—which are taken partly from nature and from <term>sense</term> <note>original: "Sensu." In Böhme's context, this refers to the inherent meaning or "signature" found within the physical world and sensory perception.</note>, and are partly also the words of foreign masters, which I have tested according to their sense and found to be good.

7. Reason wants to take offense when it sees pagan <term>terms</term> <note>original: "Terminos." Böhme often borrowed terminology from alchemy, Hermeticism, and Paracelsian philosophy.</note> and words used for the explanation of natural things, and thinks that one must use only Biblical words; however, these do not always fit the thorough explanation of the properties of nature, because one cannot express the <term>Ground</term> <note>original: "Grund." The essential, unmanifested source of all existence.</note> within them. Furthermore, the wise Heathens and Jews kept the deep Ground of nature hidden under such words, because they well understood that the knowledge of nature is not for everyone, but belongs only to those whom **GOD** has chosen for it through nature.

8. No one should take offense at this, for when **GOD** reveals His mystery to a person, He also leads him into a sense of how he should express it, as **GOD** recognizes is necessary and required in every <term>age</term> <note>original: "Seculo."</note> to set the confused tongues and opinions back upon the Ground. And no one should think that this happens by chance through human reason: The revelation of Divine things is opened through the inner Ground of the spiritual world and brought into visible forms, as the Creator wishes to reveal it.

9. I will, however, write a brief explanation of Divine revelation, as much as I may grasp in this narrow space, and explain the foreign words for the greater understanding of the other books, and present a <term>summary</term> <note>original: "Summam."</note> of these writings (books) as a short <term>formula</term> <note>original: "Formulam."</note> for the beginner to contemplate; further explanation will be found in the other books.

<meta>catchword: EXPLANATION-</meta>

<summary>Jacob Böhme introduces the purpose of his "Key," explaining that he uses specialized terminology from nature and other masters because standard Biblical language cannot fully articulate the deep mysteries of the natural and spiritual Ground.</summary>
<keywords>Key, Clavis, terminology, nature, divine revelation, reason, Heathens, Ground, spiritual world, summary</keywords>

<meta>This page transitions from the general preface to the specific "Explanation of Terms" (Explicatio Terminorum). <header>XX. The Key, or Explanation</header>

# EXPLANATION OF TERMS. <note>original: "EXPLICATIO TERMINORUM"</note>
## I. How one should consider **God** outside of nature and creation.

### Summaries.

**God** is One, §. 1. 2. in Trinity. 3. 4. The Will is the Father; 5. the delight <note>original: "Lust." In Böhme's work, this refers to a divine "pleasurable desire" or the "mirror" in which the divine Will sees itself.</note> is the Son, as the receptivity of the Will; 6 7. and the outgoing Will is the Holy Spirit. 8. Thus there are three kinds of workings in the Unity. 9. The essential desire is the Magnet; 10. otherwise the Unity would be still. 11. What **God's** Word is, 12, 13. which opens the sense? 14. The Holy Name **Jehovah** is the essential sensitivity. 15. Every letter denotes a power. 16. The Divine Wisdom 17. 18. is the Divine Understanding; 19. **Great Mystery** <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." A central term in Böhme’s thought referring to the undifferentiated source or "chaos" from which all things are manifested.</note> 20. has 2 essences and wills within itself, 21. and is the **Chaos**, 22. from which Light and Darkness originate. 23. From the **Center** <note>original: "Centro"</note> of eternal nature. 24. Eternal nature 25. forms the Word into properties, 26. and is the master craftsman; 27. it stands in 7 properties, 28. which are Desire, 29. the drawing-in of Desire, 30. and the ground of Anguish: in itself the foundation of the hells stands. 31. Further, Fire, in which the Unity appears in Love, 32. Light, 33. Sound, 34. and finally the corporeality of the 6 properties. 35. In these 7 properties, 2 essences are to be understood, as the Divine of the Unity, 36. and then the nature's own essence. 37. House of the other 6 properties. 73. The power of fire and light is the **tincture**. <note>original: "Tinctur." Moses speaks: *The Lord our God is one God*; Deut. 6:4. And in another place it stands: *From Him, through Him, and in Him are all things*: Rom. 11:36. Likewise: *Am I not He who fills all things?* Jer. 23:24. Likewise: *Through his Word all things are made, that which is made.* John 1:3.

2. Therefore one must say that He is the origin of all things: He is the **Eternal and immeasurable (unchangeable) Unity**; for example: If I think, what would remain in the place of this world if the 4 elements with the stars along with nature were gone and ceased, so that no nature or creature existed anymore? Answer: That same eternal Unity would remain, from which nature and the creature received their origin. Likewise, if I think: What is many hundred thousand miles above the stars, or what is in the place where there is no creature? Answer: It is the eternal, unchangeable Unity, which is the only **Good**, which has nothing behind it or before it that could give or take anything from it, or from which this Unity might originate; there is no ground, goal, (time), nor place there, and it is the only **God**, or the eternal Good, which cannot be expressed.

## II. Further consideration of how this one God is a Trinity.

3. Holy Scripture shows us that this one God is a **Trinity**, namely a single threefold Being <note>original: "Wesen," which can mean being, essence, or substance.</note> that has three kinds of workings and yet is only a single being, as can be seen in the outflowing power in all things by whoever observes it.

4. There is especially an image in **fire, light, and air**, which are three kinds of workings and yet are only a single ground and being; and as one sees that fire, light, and air arise from the candle, where yet the candle is none of those things and yet is a cause for such: so also the eternal Unity

## II. Further contemplation on how this one God is Trinity.

3. Holy Scripture indicates to us that this One God is Threefold, namely a single Threefold Being that has three kinds of working, and yet is only a single Being, as one can see in the power that has flowed out into all things, if one observes it.

4. Particularly, there is an image in fire, light, and air, which are three kinds of workings and yet only a single ground and being; and as one sees that fire, light, and air arise from the candle, though the candle is none of these, yet is a cause for such: so also the eternal Unity

->XX. Key, or Explanation<-

the cause and the foundation of the eternal <term>Trinity: "Dreyheit," the three-fold nature of God (Father, Son, and Holy Spirit) expressed as a single essence</term>, which reveals itself out of the eternal Unity itself, and leads into a surging or will, delight, and outgoing.

5. (1) The surging <note>original: "Wallen," a term Böhme uses to describe a boiling or bubbling movement of spiritual energy</note> or the will is the Father, as the revelation or movement of the Unity, by which the Unity wills itself.

6. (2) The delight is the Son, as the only thing that the will desires, as his love and pleasure, as is to be seen at the baptism of Christ, where the Father testified: Matthew 3:17. **This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased; listen to him.** <note>Böhme combines the text of Christ's baptism with the command "listen to him" usually found in the account of the Transfiguration.</note>

7. The delight is the containment <note>original: "Einfaßlichkeit," the quality of being graspable or held within a form</note> of the will, as the will in its Unity introduces itself into an activity and a place for itself, through which the will works and desires, as a sensitivity and power of the will.

8. The will is the Father, as the act of willing; and the delight is the Son, as the power and activity within the willing, by which the will works; and the Holy Spirit is the outgoing will through the delight of the power, as a life of the will, the power, and the delight.

9. Thus there are three kinds of activity in the eternal Unity: The Unity is the willing of itself, and the delight is an actual being <note>original: "Wesen," referring here to a spiritual substance or essence</note> of the willing, and an eternal joy of sensitivity within the willing; and the Holy Spirit is the outgoing of the power, as one sees an analogy in a plant.

10. The <term>magnet: "Magnet," a metaphor for the attractive power of desire that draws spirit into matter</term> or the essential desire of nature (as the will of nature’s desire) gathers itself into an <term>Ens: "Ens," a Latin term for a living entity or the "being" of a thing</term> or essence to form a plant; and in the containment of the desire, the desire becomes sensitive, as something active; and in that same activity the power arises, into which the magnetic desire of nature—as the outflowed will of God—acts in a natural way. In such actual sensitivity, the magnetic, desiring will becomes significant and full of joy, and it goes out from the active power, from which arises the growth and the scent of the plant; and thus we see the image of the Trinity of God in all growing and living things.

11. If there were not such a desirous sensitivity and outgoing activity of the Trinity in the eternal Unity,

### III. Of the Eternal Word of **GOD**.

12. Holy Scripture says: **GOD** made all things through His Word. Also, the Word was **GOD**, John 1:1-3. This is understood as follows:

13. The Word is nothing other than the out-breathing will from the power—a <term>differentiation: "Schiedlichkeit," the process by which the unified power of God begins to distinguish itself into various parts</term> of the power into a multiplicity of powers, a division and outflow of the Unity, from which knowledge <note>Böhme often uses "Wissenschaft" to mean a conscious, structured awareness or "science" of being</note> takes its origin. For in a single essence where there is no <term>differentiation</term>, and which is only One, there is no knowledge; for even if there were, it would only know one thing: itself. But when it divides and separates from itself, the divided will goes into multiplicity, and each individual separation works within itself.

14. However, because the Unity cannot truly be severed or pull apart from itself, this <term>partitioning: "Zerscheidung," the internal division of the divine will</term> remains within the out-breathing will inside the Unity. This <term>partitioning</term> of the breath only provides distinctions, through which the eternal will—along with the delight and the outgoing—enters into a knowledge of infinite forms or understanding. This is an eternal, perceptible, active, and <term>sensory: "sensualische," relating to the senses or perception</term> knowledge of the powers. In this division of the will, in its partitioning, one <term>sense: original Latin "Sensus," referring to a mode of perception</term> or form of the will beholds, feels, tastes, smells, and hears the others; yet it remains a single sensory activity, like the great joyful bond of love and the benevolent, single (eternal) essence.

### IV. Of the Holy Name **JEHOVAH**.

15. The ancient Rabbis among the Jews understood this to a certain extent, for they said that this Name is the highest and holiest Name of **GOD**, by which one understands the active Godhead in <term>perception: original Latin "Sensu"</term>. And it is true; for in this active <term>perception</term> lies the true life of all things, in time and eternity, in the Ground and the <term>Abyss: "Ungrund," Böhme’s term for the "groundless" nothingness or the absolute void from which God emerges</term>. It is **GOD** Himself, as the divine, active sensitivity, <term>perceptibility: "Findlichkeit," the quality of being able to be found or felt</term>, knowledge, and love, as the

<summary>Böhme explains that God's "Word" is an out-breathing of divine power that creates differentiation and sensory knowledge within the Eternal Unity. He identifies the name JEHOVAH as the expression of God's active, perceptible presence in both the created world and the infinite abyss.</summary>

...true understanding in the actual Unity, from which the five senses of true life arise.

16. Each letter in this name <note>Referring to the name **IEHOUA** (Jehovah), which Böhme is about to analyze letter by letter</note> indicates a power and a particular effect, as a form within the working power. For **I** is the outflow of the eternal, inseparable Unity, as the sweet holiness (otherwise: graciousness), the ground of Divine **Ichheit** <term>I-ness: the quality of being a self or an individual "I"</term>. **E** is a triple **I**, where the Trinity encloses itself into a Unity, for the **I** goes into the **E** and is called (otherwise: encloses) **IE**, as an out-breathing of the Unity into itself. **H** is the Word or breathing of the Trinity of **GOD**. **O** is the circumference <note>original: "Circumferentz"</note>, as the Son of **GOD**, through which the **IE** speaks with the **H** or breathing; understand this as being from the conceived delight of the power. **V** is the joy-filled outflow from the breathing, as the outgoing Spirit of **GOD**. **A** is that which has gone out from the power, as the Wisdom, a subject <note>original: "Subjectum," used here to mean a container or vessel</note> of the Trinity, in which the Trinity works, and in which the Trinity is manifest. This name is nothing other than an expression of the three-fold working in the Holy Trinity within the Unity of **GOD**: read further on this in the explanation of the tables of the three principles of Divine Revelation, Number XIX, page 61.

## V. Of Divine Wisdom.

17. The Holy Scripture speaks: Wisdom is the breathing of Divine power, a ray and breath of the Almighty. Wisdom of Solomon 7:25 <note>original: "Sap. 7: 25"</note>. Likewise, **GOD** has made all things through His Wisdom. This is understood as follows:

18. Wisdom is the flowed-out Word of Divine power, knowledge, and holiness; a subject or counter-reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf," a Böhmean term for an object that reflects the subject back to itself</note> of the bottomless Unity in the Essence, in which the Holy Spirit works, forms, and builds; understand, He forms and builds the Divine understanding within Wisdom, for she is the passive part; and the Spirit of **GOD** within her is the Doer or the life, just as the soul is in the body.

19. She is the great **Mysterium** <note>Latin: "Mystery"</note> of the Divine nature, for in her the powers, colors, and virtues become manifest: In her is the <term>differentiation: "Schiedlichkeit," the process of distinguishing individual qualities</term> of the power, acting as the understanding: she is herself the Divine understanding, as the Divine contemplation <note>original: "Beschaulichkeit"</note>, in which the Unity is manifest: She is the true Divine—

## VI. Of the Great Mystery <note>original Latin: "Mysterio Magno"</note>.

<margin>20</margin>
<image-desc>Ornamental woodcut initial D featuring scrolling foliage.</image-desc>
**T**he Great Mystery is a subject <term>Subjectum: here meaning a container, vessel, or the underlying substance in which something manifests</term> of Wisdom, where the breathing Word or the active, welling power of Divine understanding flows out through Wisdom, in which also the Unity of **GOD** flows out for its revelation: for in the Great Mystery, eternal nature has its origin; and in the Great Mystery, two essences and wills are always understood.

21. The first essence is the Unity of **GOD**, as Divine power, or the out-flowing Wisdom. The other essence is the separable <note>or "distinct"</note> will, which arises through the breathing, speaking Word, which has its ground not in the Unity, but in the mobility of the outflow or out-breathing, which leads itself into its own willing and into desire for nature, such as into the properties up to fire and light; where in the fire the natural life is understood, and in the light the holy life, as a revelation of the Unity, whereby the Unity is a love-fire or light: and in this place or working, **GOD** calls himself a dear, merciful **GOD**, according to the sharpened fire-burning love of the Unity; and an angry, jealous **GOD**, according to the fiery ground according to eternal nature.

22. The Great Mystery is the chaos from which light and darkness, as the foundation of heaven and hell, have flowed and been revealed from eternity: For the foundation, which we now call hell, as its own principle, is the ground and cause for the fire of eternal nature, which fire in **GOD** is only a burning of love;
