and where **GOD**, according to the Unity, is not manifest in a thing, there is a painful fire-burning <note>original: "peinlich Feuer-Brennen." In Böhme's time, "peinlich" referred to "Pein" (torment or labor), suggesting a state of intense, unrefined energy not yet tempered by Divine Light</note>. This fire-burning is a revelation of life and of Divine love, through which Divine love, as the Unity, becomes over-inflamed and sharpened into a fiery effect of the power of **GOD**.

<margin>23.</margin> This ground is therefore called the Great Mystery <note>original Latin: "Mysterium Magnum"</note>, or a Chaos, in which evil and good originate, such as light and darkness, life and death, joy and sorrow, salvation and damnation; for it is the ground of souls and angels, and of all eternal creatures, both the evil and the good. It is a ground of heaven and of hells, and of the visible world, together with everything that is there, since everything is situated in a single ground, just like the image in the tree <note>referring to a block of wood</note> before the artist carves and forms it. One cannot say of the spiritual world that it took a beginning, but rather it has been revealed from eternity out of the Chaos; for the light has shone from eternity in the darkness, and the darkness has not grasped it, just as day and night are within each other, and yet are two in one. I must only write in such a partitioned <note>or sequential</note> manner—as if it had taken a beginning—in order to contemplate the Divine ground of Divine revelation, so that one may distinguish between nature and Deity for a greater understanding of where evil and good have come from, and what the Essence of all Essences may be.

->See Appendix of the Great Mystery, 1st Epistle 38: 5. 6.<-

## VII. Of the Center <note>original Latin: "Centro"</note> of Eternal Nature.

<margin>24.</margin> With the word Center <note>original Latin: "Centrum"</note> one understands the first beginning of nature, as the innermost ground, where the self-originated will introduces itself into an acceptance of selfhood <note>original: "Ichheit," a key term in Böhme's philosophy referring to the emergence of individual identity or "I-ness"</note>, as into a natural working. For nature is only a tool of **GOD**, with which **GOD's** power works, and yet it also has its own mobility from the out-flowing will of **GOD**: Thus the Center is the point or ground of one's own acceptance of selfhood, from which something becomes, and from which the seven properties originate.

It also introduces the "Center of Eternal Nature" as the point where the Divine will transitions into individual selfhood and natural properties.</summary>

[of the most principal points and words]

# VIII. On Eternal Nature and Its Seven Properties.

25. Nature is nothing other than the properties of the receptivity of its own arising **desire** <note>original: "Begierde." In Böhme’s thought, this is not just a human feeling, but a fundamental cosmic force—a "pull" or "attraction" that brings spirit into solid form</note>. This desire arises within the distinctiveness of the **breathing Word**—which is the breathing power—as the properties introduce themselves into a tangible essence. Then, this same essence is called a "natural being." It is not **GOD** Himself, for although **GOD** dwells throughout nature, nature only grasps Him as far as the **Unity of GOD** gives itself into the natural being and makes it substantial. This occurs in the **Light-Essence**, which works within nature itself, penetrating and saturating it; otherwise, the **Unity of GOD** is incomprehensible to nature, which is merely the receptivity of desire.

26. Nature arises in the outflowed Word of Divine sensitivity and **knowledge** <note>original: "Wissenschaft," meaning a systematic knowing or "scientia" rather than modern laboratory science</note>, and it is a constant formation and shaping of that knowledge and sensitivity. Whatever the Word works through **Wisdom**, nature builds and forms into specific properties.

27. Nature is like a carpenter who builds a house that the **Mind** <note>original: "Gemüthe," referring to the inner disposition or seat of the soul's thoughts</note> has modeled within itself beforehand. It is to be understood in the same way here: whatever the eternal Mind models in the power of **GOD** within His **Wisdom** and guides into an **Idea** <note>original Latin: "Ideam"</note>, nature then forms into a property.

28. In its primary foundation, nature consists of **seven properties**, and these seven divide themselves into infinity.

29. The **first property** of nature is **Desire**. This creates harshness, sharpness, hardness, coldness, and essence.

30. The **second property** is **Motion** or the "drawing in" of the desire. This creates stinging, breaking, and the division of the hardness; it cuts through the attracted desire and brings it into multiplicity. It is the ground of bitter woe, but also the true root of life, and is the **Vulcan** <note>original Latin: "Vulcanus," the Roman god of fire and smithing, used here to represent the "striking" of a spark</note> for the ignition of fire.

31. The **third property** is **Sensitivity** <note>original: "Empfindlichkeit," the capacity to feel or perceive</note> within the breaking of the harsh hardness. It is the ground of **Anguish** <note>original: "Angst," a central term for Böhme describing the "narrowness" or "constriction" of energy before it finds release</note> and of the natural will, wherein the eternal will

<meta>catchword: will</meta>

He begins outlining the "Seven Properties of Nature," describing the first three: the contraction of Desire, the friction of Motion, and the resulting Anguish.</summary>

## XX. Key, or Explanation

...desires to be revealed; that is, He wishes to be a fire and light, like a flash or a luster, within which the powers, colors, and virtues of **Wisdom** appear. In these first three properties <note>In Böhme's cosmology, the first three properties—Contraction, Friction, and Anguish—form the "dark world" or the necessary tension required for existence</note> lies the foundation of **wrath** and of the **hells**, and of everything that is fierce <note>original: "grimmig." In Böhme's system, "Grimm" or fierceness refers to the harsh, contracting, and severe quality of nature before it is enlightened by Divine Love</note>.

32. The fourth property is **fire**, wherein the **Unity** appears in the light as a **burning of love**, and the fierceness is the very essence of the fire.

33. The fifth property is the **light** with its power of love, wherein the **Unity** works together within a natural being.

34. The sixth property is the **sound** or resonance, or the natural understanding, wherein the five **senses** <note>original: "Sensus"</note> work spiritually, as in an intelligent life of nature.

35. The seventh property is the **subject** <note>original: "Subjectum," meaning the underlying substance, container, or vessel</note> or the enclosure of the other six properties, wherein they work just as life works in the flesh; and this seventh is rightly called the **ground** or the **place of nature**, where the properties stand together in a single foundation.

36. Within these seven properties, one must always understand two distinct beings: First, according to the **abyss** of such properties, one understands the **Divine Being**, as the **Divine Will**, with the outflowing **Unity of GOD**. This unity flows out through nature and introduces itself into a pleasantness amidst the sharpness, so that **eternal love** may be felt and become active. In this way, love has something that is **passive** <note>original: "leidende." This term carries the dual meaning of "suffering" and "passivity"—the capacity to be acted upon or to receive an impression</note>, wherein it may reveal itself and be recognized, and by which it may be loved and desired in return—namely, the painful-suffering nature, which is transformed within love into an eternal kingdom of joy. For when love reveals itself in the fire and the light, it enflames nature and penetrates it, just as the sun penetrates a herb, or as fire penetrates iron.

37. The second being is nature’s **own being**, which is painful and suffering, and is the **instrument** <note>original: "Merckzeug," an archaic term for a tool, organ, or apparatus of activity</note> of the working. For where there is no passivity, there is also no desire for redemption or for something better; and where there is no desire for something better, there a thing remains resting within itself. Therefore, the **Unity** leads itself, through its outflow and its distinctiveness, into nature,

...so that she <note>Böhme refers to "Nature" or "Wisdom" as "she" (die Natur/die Weisheit), personifying these forces as feminine aspects of the divine process</note> might have a **counterpart** <note>original: "Gegenwurf." ### IX. Explanation of the Seven Properties of Eternal Nature. *

38. The first property is **Desirousness** <term>Begierlichkeit: The primal urge or hunger that causes spirit to contract and become solid</term>, much like a **Magnet**, acting as the **receptivity** <note>original: "Einfäßlichkeit," the capacity to be contained in a vessel</note> of the will. Since the will desires to be "something" and yet has nothing from which to make itself, it draws itself into a self-acceptance, impressing and grasping itself into a "something." Yet this "something" is nothing but a sharp, magnetic hunger, a **harshness** <note>original: "Herbigkeit." In Böhme’s system, this is the astringent, bitter quality of contraction</note>, like a hardness, from which hardness, coldness, and substance arise. This impressing or attracting shadows itself and turns into **darkness**, which is the foundation of both eternal and temporal darkness. Through this sharpness, at the beginning of this world, there arose **salt, stones, bones**, and all things similar to them.

39. The second property of eternal nature arises from the first, and it is the **Pulling** or **Moving** within the sharpness. For the Magnet creates hardness, but the movement breaks that hardness apart again; it is an everlasting **strife** within itself. Whatever the Desire grasps and turns into "something," the Movement cuts and divides, so that it takes on form and shape. Between these two properties arises the **bitter woe**, acting as a **sting** of sensitivity. For where there is movement within sharpness, the property is painful, and this is the cause of all sensitivity and the feeling of pain; for if there were no sharpness and movement, there would be no sensitivity.

40. This movement is also the foundation of the **air** in the visible world, which becomes manifest through the fire, as shall be reported hereafter.

### XX. Key, or Explanation

...[He] wants to become manifest; that is, He wants to be a fire and light, like a flash or radiance, wherein the powers, colors, and virtues of **Wisdom** appear. 32. The fourth property is the **fire**, wherein the Unity appears in the light, as in a love-burning; and the **fierceness** <note>original: "Grimm," often translated as "fury" or "wrathful energy"</note> is the fire's essence.

33. The fifth property is the **light** with its love-power, wherein the Unity works together within a natural being.

34. The sixth property is the **sound** or resonance, or the **natural understanding**, wherein the five **senses** <note>original Latin: "Sensus"</note> work spiritually, as in an intelligent life of nature.

This Unity flows through nature and introduces itself into "sharpness" <note>the friction and harshness of the material world</note> through attraction, so that the eternal love might be perceptible and active, and so that it might have something that is **suffering** <note>original: "leidende." Böhme argues that without a "passive" or "suffering" counterpart, God's love would have nothing to act upon</note>, in which it may reveal itself, and in which it may be known—whereby it might be loved and desired in return. This is the **painful-suffering nature**, which is transformed in love into an eternal kingdom of joy. For when love reveals itself in the fire within the light, it enflames nature and penetrates it, just as the sun penetrates a plant or fire penetrates iron.

37. The other being is nature’s own being, which is painful and suffering, and is the **tool of the working**. For where there is no capacity for suffering, there is also no desire for redemption or for something better; and where there is no desire for something better, a thing simply rests within itself. Therefore, the **Eternal Unity** leads itself, through its outflowing and its capacity for distinction, into nature,

## IX. Explanation of the Seven Properties of Eternal Nature. *

<margin>38.</margin> The first property is **Desirousness**, which is like a magnet; it is the "containability" of the will, where the will wants to be something but has nothing out of which to make itself anything. Thus, it draws itself into a self-attraction, **impressing** <note>original: "impresset," meaning a spiritual contraction or condensing</note> and grasping itself into a "Something." Yet this Something is nothing but a sharp, magnetic hunger, a harshness like a hardness, from which hardness, coldness, and substance arise. This impressing or drawing-in overshadows itself and turns into darkness, which is the ground of both eternal and temporal darkness. Through this "sharpness," at the beginning of this world, salt, stones, bones, and everything similar were originated.

39. The second property of eternal nature arises from the first, and it is the **pulling** or **moving** within the sharpness. For while the magnet creates hardness, the movement breaks that hardness apart again; it is a perpetual struggle within itself. Whatever the Desire grasps and makes into a "Something," the Motion cuts and divides so that it may take on form and structure. Between these two properties, the **bitter ache** <note>original: "bittere Wehe"</note> arises as a sting of sensibility. For when there is movement within sharpness, the property is painful; and this is also the cause of all sensitivity and pain. If there were no sharpness and movement, there would be no sensitivity.

40. This movement is also a ground of delight in the visible world, which becomes manifest through fire, as shall be mentioned hereafter.

## XX. Clavis, or Explanation

41. Thus we understand that Desire is the foundation of <note>The word "Ichheit" refers to "Selfhood" or the quality of being an individual "I."</note> **Selfhood**, so that out of Nothing, Something might come to be. We should consider that this was the beginning of this world, through which **God** brought all things into being; for it is the very same "Something" through which **God** spoke: *Let there be!* Gen. 1: 3. It is the "Becoming" that created everything where there was once nothing but a Spirit. It has made the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." In Böhme's thought, this is the chaotic "chaos" or potentiality from which God creates all things.</note>, which is spiritual, visible and substantial, as can be seen in the elements, stars, and living creatures.

42. And the second property—**Motion**—was, at the beginning of this world, the <term>Separator</term> <note>original: "Separator," meaning the dividing or distinguishing power.</note> or Divider within the forces. By this, the Creator (acting as the Will of **God**) brought all things out of the <term>Great Mystery</term> and into a specific form. For this Motion is the flowing, moving Word through which the supernatural **God** made all things and brought them into their shapes.

43. The third property of eternal nature is **Anguish** <note>original: "Angst." In this context, it refers to a state of contraction or "agony" caused by the tension between the first two properties (contraction and expansion). It is not necessarily a human emotion here, but a cosmic friction.</note>. This is the Will that has introduced itself into Nature and Selfhood for the sake of being perceptible. When the individual will stands within that sharp motion, it enters into Anguish—that is, into **Sensitivity**. For outside of Nature, the will cannot feel anything; but within this moving sharpness, it becomes sensitive. This sensitivity is the origin of fire, and also of the mind and the senses. Through this, the individual natural will becomes "feeling" and seeks rest. Thus, the "distinguishability" of the will flows out from itself and permeates the other properties. From this arises "Taste" within the sharpness, so that one property tastes and feels the other. This is also the foundation and cause of the senses: one property penetrates the other and ignites it, so that the will recognizes from where its <note>The term "Leidenheit" implies both suffering and the state of being "passive" or "acted upon."</note> passion or experience comes. For if there were no Sensitivity, the will would know nothing of the properties, for it would be merely "One" <note>meaning a singular, undifferentiated void.</note>. Thus, the will takes Nature unto itself by feeling the sharp motion within itself.

44. This motion within itself is like a **turning wheel**; yet, it is not that there is a physical turning, but rather that this is how it occurs within the properties. For Desire draws inward, while Motion pushes outward. Thus the will, in such Anguish, can move neither inward nor outward, and yet it is pulled both outward and inward at once. Thus its form remains, which...

[the will] wants itself and out of itself, which is above itself and beneath itself, and yet can go nowhere, but is an **Anguish** and the true foundation of the hells and **God’s** wrath; for the anguish stands in the dark, sharp motion.

45. From this foundation, in the creation of the world, the **Sulfur-spirit** arose along with the matter of the sulfuric kind; this Sulfur-spirit is the natural life of earthly and elemental creatures.

46. The wise heathens <note>Böhme often refers to pre-Christian or "natural" philosophers who studied the mysteries of the physical world without the benefit of scripture.</note> understood this foundation to some degree, for they said: all things in this world consist of **Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury** <note>original: "Sale, Sulphure und Mercurio." These are the *Tria Prima* (Three Primes) of Paracelsian alchemy, which Böhme reinterprets as spiritual forces.</note>. In saying this, they did not only look at the physical matter, but at the spirit from which matter arises. For their foundation does not consist of coarse Salt, Sulfur, and Mercury—they do not mean the physical substances—but rather the spirit of such properties. Everything that lives, grows, and exists in this world consists of these, whether it be *spiritual* or *material*. For with **Salt** <note>original: "Sale."</note> they indicate the sharp, magnetic desire of nature; and with **Mercury** <note>original: "Mercurio."</note> they indicate the motion and separation of nature, through which every thing is distinguished and formed. And with **Sulfur** <note>original: "Sulphure."</note> they have indicated the sensitive, surging, growing life. For in the Sulfur-spirit lies the oil, in which the fire-life burns; and in the oil lies the <term>Fifth Essence</term> <note>original: "Quinta Essentia." Historically, the "quintessence" is the purest, most spiritualized element that transcends the four physical elements.</note>, as the sulfuric Mercury, the true life of nature, which is an outflow from the Word of Divine power and motion. In this, the foundation of heaven is understood. And in the Fifth Essence lies the <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." In Böhme's system, the Tincture is the "lustre" or the holy, transformative power that brings a substance into its highest, most divine state.</note>, as the paradisiacal foundation—the outflowing Word of Divine power, in which the properties lie in equality.

47. Thus, with the third property of nature—**Anguish**—we understand the sharpness and painfulness of fire, such as burning and consuming. For when the will is placed into such sharpness, it always wants to consume the cause of such sharpness; it always presses back toward the **Unity of God**, as toward rest. And the Unity presses with its outflow toward this motion and sharpness, and this is thus a constant joining together for the revelation of the Divine will. As one then finds in these three—Salt, Sulfur, and Oil—something heavenly within the earthly, if one understands this rightly and meditates upon the spirit...

## XX. Key, or Explanation

41. Thus we understand that <term>Desire</term> <note>original: "Begierlichkeit." In Böhme's system, this is the first property of nature: a magnetic, contracting force that pulls "nothing" into "something."</note> is the foundation for <term>Selfhood</term> <note>original: "Ichheit," or "I-ness." The quality of being an individual, distinct ego.</note>, so that out of Nothing, Something comes to be. We must consider that it was the beginning of this world, through which God brought all things into being; for it is the very same through which God spoke: *Let there be!* Gen. 1:3. It is the "Be!" that created when nothing existed but only a Spirit: It has made the <term>Great Mystery</term> <note>original: "Mysterium Magnum." The chaos or "hidden treasury" from which the manifest world emerges.</note>, which is spiritual, visible and substantial, as can be seen in the elements, stars, and creatures.

42. And the second property, <term>Motion</term> <note>original: "Bewegniß." The second property, which resists the contraction of Desire, creating friction.</note>, was in the beginning of this world the <term>Separator</term> <note>original: "Separator." The Latin term for the "Divider" or "Distinguisher" that sorts the undifferentiated stuff of creation into specific forms.</note> or divider of powers. With this, the Creator—as the Will of God—brought all things out of the <term>Great Mystery</term> into a form; for it is the out-flowing, moving Word through which the supernatural God made all things and brought them into formation.

43. The third property of eternal nature is <term>Anguish</term> <note>original: "Angst." Not merely fear, but a structural tension or "vibration" caused by the conflict between the first two properties.</note>, as the Willing that has introduced itself into the unpleasantness of nature and <term>Selfhood</term>. When the self-will stands in that sharp motion, it comes into anguish, which is <term>Sensitivity</term> <note>original: "Empfindlichkeit." The capacity to feel, perceive, or be affected.</note>; for outside of nature, the will cannot feel anything; but in the moving sharpness, it becomes sensitive. And this <term>Sensitivity</term> is the cause of fire, as well as the mind and the senses; for through it, the natural self-will becomes feeling and seeks rest. Thus the "distinguishability" of the will goes out from itself and permeates the properties; from this, <term>Taste</term> <note>original: "Schmack." The sensory or ontological "flavor" that allows one force to perceive another.</note> originates in the sharpness, so that one property tastes and feels the other. This is also the foundation and cause of the senses—that one property penetrates another and ignites the other—so that the will recognizes from whence <term>Passivity</term> <note>original: "Leidenheit," or "Suffering." The state of being acted upon or "suffering" the influence of another.</note> comes. For if <term>Sensitivity</term> did not exist, the will would know nothing of the properties, for it would be only one; and thus the will takes nature unto itself as it feels the sharp motion within itself.

44. This <term>Motion</term> is within itself like a turning wheel; yet not that there is such a literal turning, but rather that this is how it is within the properties, for the desire pulls inward and the <term>Motion</term> presses outward; thus the will, in such anguish, can go neither inward nor outward, and yet is pulled both outward and inward; thus its form remains, which—

[it] wills into itself and out of itself—that is, above itself and beneath itself—and yet can go nowhere; rather, it is an **anguish** and the true foundation of the hells and **God’s wrath**, for this anguish exists within the dark, sharp movement.

45. Out of this foundation, in the creation of the world, the <term>Sulfur-Spirit</term> <note>original: "Schwefel-Geist." In Böhme's thought, Sulfur represents the active, "boiling" principle of life and growth.</note> emerged together with the <term>Matter</term> <note>original: "Materia."</note> of the sulfuric kind; this sulfur-spirit is the natural life of earthly and elemental creatures.

46. The wise <term>Heathens</term> <note>Böhme refers here to ancient philosophers and alchemists who studied the natural world without the benefit of Christian scripture, yet still discovered deep spiritual truths.</note> understood this foundation to some degree, for they said: in <term>Salt</term>, <term>Sulfur</term>, and <term>Mercury</term> <note>original: "Sale, Sulphure und Mercurio." These are the *Tria Prima* (Three Primes) of alchemy, which Böhme reinterprets as spiritual forces rather than just physical substances.</note> all things in this world consist. By this, they did not only look upon the **Matter**, but upon the Spirit from which the **Matter** arises; for their foundation does not consist in coarse **Salt**, **Sulfur**, and **Mercury**—they do not mean that—but rather the Spirit of such qualities. In this Spirit exists everything that lives, grows, and is in this world, whether it be spiritual or material. For with **Salt**, they signify the sharp magnetic desire of nature; and with **Mercury**, they signify the movement and separation of nature, through which every thing is marked and formed; and with **Sulfur**, they have signified the sensitive, boiling, growing life. For in the **Sulfur-Spirit** lies the <term>Oil</term> <note>In this context, "Oil" is the gentle, flammable principle that provides the fuel for the light of the spirit to shine without being consumed by the "fire" of nature.</note>, in which the <term>Fire-Life</term> burns; and in the <term>Oil</term> lies the <term>Quintessence</term> <note>original: "Quinta Essentia," literally the "fifth essence." The purest, most concentrated medicinal or spiritual core of a substance.</note>, as the sulfuric <term>Mercury</term>, the right life of nature, which is an outflow from the Word of Divine Power and movement, wherein the foundation of Heaven is understood. And in the <term>Quintessence</term> lies the <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." The highest spiritual power that can transform, heal, and bring a substance to its perfect state.</note>, as the paradisiacal foundation—the out-flowed Word of Divine Power—in which the properties lie in equality.

47. Thus we understand with the third property of nature—namely, with **Anguish**—the sharpness and painfulness of fire, such as burning and consuming. For when the will is placed into such sharpness, it always wants to consume the cause of such sharpness, as it always presses back toward the **Unity of God**, which is rest. The Unity, in turn, presses with its outflow toward this movement and sharpness; this creates a constant joining together for the revelation of the Divine Will. In this way, one always finds a Heavenly thing within the Earthly in these three—namely in <term>Salt</term>, <term>Sulfur</term>, and <term>Oil</term>—whoever understands this rightly, and follows after the Spirit—

<summary>Böhme explains how the alchemical 'Tria Prima' (Salt, Sulfur, Mercury) represent spiritual forces of desire, motion, and life within all creation. He describes the 'Anguish' of nature as a driving force that seeks to return to the peace of Divine Unity.</summary>

# XX. The Key, or Explanation

thinks: for in the sharpness <note>original: "Schärfe." In Böhme’s thought, this refers to the acute, defining quality that gives a thing its distinct character.</note> lies the soul of every thing, and in the movement lies the true life of the sensory kind, and in the <term>Oil of Sulfur</term> <note>original: "Oel des Schwefels." Not literal oil, but the vital, flammable essence of life that allows it to catch the "light" of the spirit.</note> lies the powerful spirit, which originates from the <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." The spiritual or alchemical "dye" that transforms and ennobles base matter into something divine.</note>. Thus, a heavenly thing always lies hidden within the earthly, for the invisible spiritual world flowed out into the creation.

**48.** The fourth property or form of eternal nature is the **spiritual fire**, within which the light—as the **Unity**—becomes manifest; for the radiance of the fire originates from the flowed-out Unity, which has entered into the natural <term>Desire</term>; and the torment and burning of the fire, as the heat, originates from the sharp consuming nature of the first three properties. This happens as follows:

**49.** The eternal Unity, which I otherwise call the **eternal Freedom** in some of my writings, is gentle, still, and lovely, like a gentle well-being that one cannot express—what kind of gentleness exists in the Unity of **GOD** outside of nature. But the three properties of nature are sharp, painful, and terrifying. Within these three painful properties stands the flowed-out **Will**, which emerged through the Word or Divine Breathing, and the Unity also stands within them. Thus, the Will longs for the gentle Unity, and the Unity longs for <term>Sensitivity</term> <note>original: "Empfindlichkeit." This refers to the capacity to feel, perceive, and be a distinct being.</note>, as for the fiery foundation; thus one enters into the other. And when that happens—understand this longing—it is like a shock or a flash of lightning, just as if one were to rub steel and stone together, or pour water into fire, to speak by way of analogy.

**50.** In this flash, the Unity feels the sensitivity, and the Will receives the gentle Unity; thus the Unity becomes a luster of the fire, and the fire becomes a **love-burning**, for it receives <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens" (Latin: "being" or "essence"). Böhme uses it to mean the spiritual "fuel" or substance that gives life power.</note> or power from the gentle Unity.

**51.** In such an ignition, the darkness of the <term>magnetic Impression</term> <note>original: "magnetische Impression." This refers to the "contraction" or "drawing-in" force of the first property of nature which creates density and darkness.</note> or containment is penetrated by the light, so that it is no longer recognized, even though it remains eternally within itself in the Impression.

**52.** And here two eternal beginnings arise: namely, the dark, harsh sharpness and pain dwelling within itself, and the sensitive power of the Unity in the light, of which the Scripture says:

**GOD**, as the eternal Unity, dwells in a light which no one can approach <note>This is a reference to 1 Timothy 6:16. Böhme often uses biblical imagery to ground his mystical descriptions of the divine essence.</note>, for thus the eternal Unity of **GOD** reveals itself through the spiritual fire in the light; and this same light is called Majesty, and **GOD**, as the supernatural Unity, is the power within it. For from the Unity, this Spirit-Fire receives its <term>Ens</term> <note>original: "Ens." A Latin term meaning "being" or "essence"; here it refers to the spiritual substance that allows the fire to shine.</note> so that it may shine; otherwise the Fire-Ground would only be a painful, terrifying hunger and a prickly desire. Indeed, it is exactly like that where the will breaks away from the Unity and wishes to live in its own desire—just as the devils have done, and as false souls also do.

**53.** Therefore, understand here two <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principia." Böhme’s term for the fundamental realms of existence: the dark fire-world and the light love-world.</note>: the first is in the ground of the fire-burning, existing as a sharp, mobile, sensible, and painful darkness within itself; and the second is in the light of the fire, wherein the eternal Unity enters into movement and joy.

**54.** For the fire is a reflection <note>original: "Gegenwurf." Literally a "counter-throw" or "object." It refers to how the fire acts as a mirror or necessary contrast that allows the divine Love to become perceptible.</note> of the great love of the Unity of **GOD**: For thus the eternal delight becomes sensible <note>original: "empfindlich." In Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to the capacity for feeling, perception, or being "alive" to the senses.</note>, and this sensibility of the Unity is called **Love**, acting as a burning or life in the Unity of **GOD**. And according to such a Love-burning, **GOD** calls Himself a merciful, dear **GOD**; for the Unity of **GOD** loves or penetrates the painful will of the fire—which initially arose in the breathing of the **Word** or the outflowing of Divine Delight—and transforms it into the greatest joy. In this fiery will of eternal nature stand the soul of man and also the angels; this is their ground and center.

**55.** Therefore, if a soul breaks away from **GOD’S** light and love and enters into its own natural desire, the ground of such darkness and painful torment becomes manifest within it. This is the hellish fire and **GOD’S** wrath, whenever it is revealed, as can be seen in the case of Lucifer. And whatever can be thought to exist within the creature is also present everywhere outside the creature; for the creature is nothing other than an image or figure of the distinct power of the whole Being.

**56.** So now understand rightly what the Fire-Ground is: namely, cold from the <term>Impression</term> <note>original: "Impression." The first property of nature: a drawing-in or contraction that creates density and "coldness."</note>, and heat from the anguish, and the motion is the <term>Vulcan</term> <note>original: "Vulcanus." In alchemy, Vulcan is the god of fire and smithing; Böhme uses the name to represent the active, driving force of the inner fire.</note>. In these three stands the fire, but the radiance of the light originates from the

...joining of the Unity in the Fire-Ground; and yet the whole ground is but the out-flowing will. <note>Böhme suggests that all existence begins as a "will" or "desire" that flows out from the divine Unity into a perceptible foundation or "ground."</note>

**57.** Therefore, the life of all things stands in fire and light, as if in that same will—whether it be in the mute <note>referring to inanimate objects or plants</note>, the growing, or the rational—all according to whether the fire has a foundation from the Eternal (such as the soul) or from the Temporal (such as the astral and elemental). For the Eternal fire is one thing, and the Temporal is another, as shall be shown hereafter.

**58.** The fifth property is now the Love-Fire, or the power and world of the Light, which dwells within itself in the darkness; and the darkness does not comprehend it, as it says in **John 1:5**: *The light shines in the darkness, and the darkness has not understood it.* Likewise: The Word is in the light, and in the Word is the true, understanding life of man, as the true spirit.

**59.** But this fire is the true soul of man (as the true spirit), which **God** breathed into man for a creaturely life. Thus, understand the true, desiring soul as coming from the eternal ground in the spiritual Will-Fire; and in the power of the light, understand the true, understanding spirit, in which the Unity of **God** dwells and is revealed, just as the **Lord Christ** says: *The kingdom of God is within you,* **Luke 17:21**. Likewise **Paul**: *You are temples of the Holy Spirit, who dwells in you.* **1 Cor. 6:19**.

**60.** This is the place of Divine Indwelling and Revelation; and thus the soul may also be damned if the fiery will breaks away from **God's** love and unity and enters into its own natural property, as into its evil characteristics; this is to be meditated upon further. O **Zion** <note>In Böhme’s writing, "Zion" represents the state of spiritual enlightenment and rebirth, contrasted with "Babel," which represents the confusion of worldly systems and religious dogma.</note>, mark this ground, and you shall be free from **Babel**.

**61.** With this fifth property, the second <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium." One of the three fundamental realms of existence in Böhme's system: the world of light/angels.</note> is understood, namely the angelic world or the thrones; for it is the movement of the Unity, where all properties of the fiery nature burn in love.

**62.** A likeness of this ground may be seen in a lit candle: For in the candle, all things lie within one another, and yet no property is manifest before the other until it is lit; then one sees fire, oil, light, air, and water from the air; all four elements become present in it <margin>in it</margin>

becoming manifest therein, which before had lain hidden in a single ground. <note>Böhme is describing the transition from a state of "nothingness" or "hidden unity" into a state where specific qualities can be perceived.</note>

**63.** In the same way, the eternal ground is to be contemplated, for the temporal being <note>or "substance"</note> has flowed out of the Eternal; therefore, one has a property like the other, except that one is eternal and the other is transitory; one is spiritual, and the other is corporeal.

**64.** When the spiritual fire and light are kindled—as they have burned from eternity—then the great mystery of Divine power and science <note>original: *Wissenschaft*, here meaning "knowledge" or "structured wisdom"</note> also becomes manifest therein. For in the fire, all properties of eternal nature become spiritual, and yet nature remains within itself as it is; but the outflow of the will becomes spiritual. For in the fire-shock <note>original: *Feuer-Schrack*; Böhme uses this to describe the sudden "crack" or "terror" of a flash of lightning or ignition</note> or flash, the dark receptivity is consumed; so from that consumption, the pure, clear fire-spirit emerges, permeated by the light-spirit. In such an outflow, we understand three kinds of properties: 

(1) the fiery will goes upwards; 
(2) forward—as in the midst, like a center <note>original: *Centro*</note> of the fiery will-spirit—goes the oily spirit, as the *essence* <note>original: *Ens*</note> of the Unity of **GOD**, which has entered into a being within the desire of nature; 
(3) and downwards, as if sinking, goes the water-spirit, as gentleness. 

And though it is all simply spirit and power, it stands thus in the figure of manifestation; not that a separation occurs, but it is only thus in the manifestation.

**65.** This threefold manifestation happens according to the Trinity <note>original: *Dreyheit*</note>, for the center in which it stands is the one **GOD** according to His manifestation. The fire-flaming love-spirit goes upwards, and gentleness out of love goes downwards, and in the middle stands the center, which is the circumference <note>original: *Circumferenz*</note>, as the Father or the whole **GOD** according to His manifestation. And as it is to be recognized in Divine manifestation, so it is also in eternal nature, according to nature's own property; for nature is only a reflection <note>original: *Gegenwurf*, literally a "counter-throw" or "object"</note> of the Godhead.

**66.** Thus, nature is further to be recognized by us. For the flash of the fire’s origin is a shock, a <term>Salnitric ground</term> <note>original: *Salnitrischer Grund*. A term Böhme uses to describe a "saltpeter-like" explosive or volatile spiritual energy that gives rise to diversity</note>, where nature goes out into infinite divisibility, such as into the multitude of powers, from which also the many kinds of angels and spirits, as well as colors and workings, have arisen; just as—

94 XX. The Key, or Explanation

also the four elements in the beginning of time; for the temperament of fire and light is the holy element, acting as the movement within the light of the Unity. But through this salnitric ground <note>original: *Salnitriſcher Grund*; in Böhme’s alchemy, this refers to a spiritual "saltpeter" or the explosive, creative energy of nature</note> (understand it spiritually and not earthly), four elements arise: specifically, in the impression of the fiery Mercury <note>original: *Mercurius*; Böhme uses this term to represent the "active word" or the driving, moving force in all life</note>, the earth and stones; and in the fifth essence <term>quintessence<gloss>the purest or most concentrated form of a substance</gloss></term> of the fiery Mercury, the fire and the heavens; and in the movement or outflowing, the air; and in the scattering of desire through the fire, the water.

67. The fiery Mercury is a "dry water" which has given birth to metals and stones; but that which was scattered through the "killing" in the fire <note>referring to the alchemical process of transformation through intense heat</note> has given birth to the water. The "impression" <note>the densifying, contracting force</note> brought the coarse roughness into the earth, which is a coarse, salnitric, Saturnian Mercury <note>original: *Saturniniſcher Mercurius*; here Böhme combines the active force of Mercury with the restrictive, densifying power of Saturn</note>. With the word "Mercury," you should always understand here in the spirit the outflowing, natural, active word of **GOD**, which was the Separator <note>original: *Separator*; the divider or organizer of substances</note>, the sorter and shaper of all beings; and with the word "Saturn," understand the Impression.

68. In the fifth property—which is the Light—the eternal Unity is substantial, acting as a holy, spiritual fire, a holy light, and a holy air, which is purely Spirit. Likewise, a holy water, which is the outflowing love of the Unity of **GOD**; and a holy earth, which is pure power and efficacy. This fifth property is the true spiritual, angelic <note>original: *engliſche*; Böhme refers here to the world of angels (*Engel*), not the nation of England</note> world of Divine joys, which stands hidden within this visible world.

69. The sixth property of eternal nature is the Sound, the Echo, or the Understanding; for in the fire-flash, the properties all become audible. The fire is the mouth of the essence, the light is the spirit, and the sound is the understanding, in which the properties all understand one another.

70. According to the revelation of the Holy Trinity, with the outflowing of the Unity, this Sound or Echo is the Divine active Word—the Understanding within eternal nature—through which supernatural science <note>original: *Wissenschaft*; meaning structured knowledge or wisdom</note> reveals itself. According to nature and the creature, it is the knowledge of **GOD**, in which natural understanding recognizes **GOD**. For natural understanding is a counter-throw <note>original: *Gegenwurf*; a reflection, counterpart, or "object" cast out from a subject</note> and outflow from Divine understanding.

# of the most prominent Points and Words.
<meta>catchword: attri-</meta>

71. In the natural understanding lie the five senses of a spiritual nature: And in the other property, namely in movement, as in the **fiery Mercury** <term>Mercurius<gloss>the active, driving "Word" or vital force that moves all things</gloss></term>, they lie of a natural kind. The sixth property gives understanding in the resonance, as in the speech of the Word, and the other property is the leader, acting as the housing or instrument of the speech or the resonance.

72. In the other property <note>referring to the second property of nature, characterized by conflict and movement</note> the power is painful, but in the sixth property it is full of joy; yet there is no other distinction between the second and sixth property than simply the light and the darkness. They stand within one another like fire and light, and are distinguished only by that much.

73. The seventh property is the essence, acting as a **subject** <term>subjectum<gloss>a vessel, container, or underlying substance</gloss></term> or housing for the other six, in which they are all substantial, just as the soul is with the body. Primarily, according to the Light-world, this is to be understood as **Paradise** or a greening, active power; for every property creates for itself a **subject** or counterpart through its own self-outflowing. In the seventh, all properties stand in **harmony** <term>temperamento<gloss>the perfect balance where no single quality overcomes the others</gloss></term>, as in a single essence: Just as they all spring from the Unity, so they all return again into one Ground. Even though they work in diverse ways and properties, here it is only one single essence, whose power is called **Tincture** <term>Tinctura<gloss>the holy, life-giving spiritual essence that penetrates and transforms matter</gloss></term>, acting as a holy, penetrating being.

74. This does not mean that the seventh property is the **Tincture** itself, but rather it is the **body** <term>corpus<gloss>the substantial or physical manifestation</gloss></term> for it: The power of the fire and light is the **Tincture** along with the substantial body, but the seventh property is the essence which the **Tincture** penetrates and hallows. Therefore, **Paradise** stands as a spiritual "greening" in the seventh property; understand that according to the power of Divine Revelation it is so, but according to the property of Nature it is an essence of the drawn-in desire of all properties.

75. It is especially to be noted that the first and seventh properties are always counted as one, and also the second and sixth as one, as well as the third and fifth as one. The fourth is alone the **turning point** <term>Scheide-Ziel<gloss>the boundary, partition, or "decision point" between the dark and light worlds</gloss></term>, for there are only three properties of nature, according to the revelation of the Holy Trinity of **GOD**.

76. Thus the first is the Desire, which [is attributed] to **GOD** the Father

80. These are therefore the Seven Properties in a Single Ground, all seven of which are equally eternal without beginning; none can be counted as first, second, third, fourth, fifth, sixth, or last. One must only present such things in a pictorial way for the sake of understanding, showing how one is born from another, to better comprehend what the Creator is, and so that one may contemplate the life and essence of the visible world.

# X. Of the Third Principle, as of the visible world: from what it arose, and what the creation is.

## Summaries.

This has permeated through the earth and elements, and there Paradise was on earth and in man; 95. but when the wrath <note>German: *Grimm*; the harsh, dark, or severe quality of nature</note> turned itself upward, **God** fled or cursed the earth. ibid. **God** creates still without ceasing. 96. What is spiritual in the invisible world works substantially in the visible. 97. The elements flow out of the 4 properties of eternal nature. 98. Reason is the outer constellation <note>the "stars" or planetary influences on the mind</note>, 116. and it should give itself entirely to **God**. 117. A short explanation or formula of Divine Revelation, 118-125. according to the two eternal principles, 126. as well as according to the third; 127. 128. according to the kingdom of heaven and hell. 129-132. Explanation of several words in other books by the author. 133. and following.

81. This visible world has sprouted from the spiritual world mentioned above, as from the outflowing Divine power, and is an object <note>original: "Objectum" or "counter-throw"</note> or counterpart of the spiritual world: The spiritual world is the innermost ground of the visible world; the visible world stands within the spiritual.

82. This visible world is nothing other than an outflow of the seven properties, for it arose out of the six working properties, and it stands at rest in the seventh as if in Paradise; this is the eternal Sabbath and rest, in which the working of the Divine power reposes.

83. Moses says: **God** created heaven and earth along with all creatures in six days, and rested on the seventh day and commanded it to be a day of rest, Genesis 2:1-2. In these words, the meaning lies secretly hidden: He could surely have made all His works in a single day; also, one cannot speak of any "day" before the sun existed, for in the Depth <note>the vast, unmanifested spiritual space</note> there is only a single day—but the meaning lies hidden in the words.

84. By the "day-works," he understands the creation or revelation of the seven properties, for he says: In the beginning **God** created heaven and earth. Genesis 1:1. In the first movement, the Magnetic Desire <note>the primary force of attraction that densifies spirit into matter</note> impressed and compacted the fiery and watery Mercury <note>the alchemical principle of movement, spirit, and volatility</note> with the other properties: Thus the coarseness separated itself from the spiritual kind, and the fiery part became metals and stones, and one part became saltpeter <note>original: "Salniter"; a vital mineral spirit Böhme believed was the "earthly" version of divine light</note> as earth, and the watery part became water: also the fiery Mercury in the...

86. On the second day’s work, **God** divided the watery and fiery Mercury from one another, and called the fiery one the firmament of Heaven, which was from the midst of the water as of the Mercury. 87. And within this soulful ground hovered the revelation of **Divine Holiness**, as the living, outflowing **Word of God** with the eternally-known **Idea** <note>original: "Idea"; here referring to the perfect archetypal form of humanity held in the Divine mind since eternity.</note>, which had been known in Divine Wisdom from eternity as a **Subject** <note>original: "Subjectum"; the underlying "matter" or "form" of a thought.</note> or form of **Divine Imagination**.

93. This [image] was clothed in the essence of the heavenly world; thus it became an intelligent spirit and a **temple of God**, an image of Divine contemplation. This spirit was given to the soul as a spouse, just as fire and light are spouses—so it is also to be understood here. That same Divine ground blossomed and permeated both soul and body; that was the true **Paradise** within man, which he lost through sin when the ground of the dark world, with its false desires, gained the government within him.

<meta>This page continues the exposition on the mystical significance of the Creation days. Having just discussed the creation of Man on the sixth day, the text now transitions to the spiritual meaning of the Sabbath rest and the relationship between the visible and invisible worlds.</meta>

# XX. The Key, or Explanation

<meta>catchword: out of</meta>

94. On the seventh day, **God** rested from all His works that He had made, as Moses speaks in Genesis 2:2. Now, **God** surely requires no rest, for He has worked from eternity and is a pure, active power; therefore, the true understanding lies hidden here in the word. For Moses speaks: **God** commanded the seventh day for rest. The seventh day was the true **Paradise** <note>original: "Paradeis"; here used to describe a state of spiritual perfection and harmony rather than just a physical garden.</note> (understand this spiritually), namely as the **Tincture** <term>Tincture<note>In alchemical and mystical thought, a "tincture" is a spiritual life-force or essence that has the power to penetrate, nourish, and transform matter.</note></term> of Divine power, which is a **Temperament** <note>original: "Temperamentum"; a state of perfect balance and harmony where no single quality or "element" dominates the others.</note>. This power has permeated all properties and worked in the seventh, as if in the very essence of all the others.

95. The **Tincture** penetrated through the earth and all elements and "tinctured" or imbued everything; there, **Paradise** was upon the earth and within Man, for malice was still hidden, just as the night is hidden within the day. Likewise, the **Fierceness** <note>original: "Grimm"; Böhme uses this term to describe the harsh, consuming, or "wrathful" aspect of nature that becomes destructive when it is not balanced by the Divine light.</note> of nature was hidden in the first **Principle** <note>original: "Principio"; one of the fundamental "realms" or "foundations" of existence in Böhme's system.</note> until the Fall of Man. At that point, the Divine working fled with the **Tincture** into its own **Principle** <note>original: "Principium"</note>, into the inner ground of the **Light-World**. Then the **Fierceness** turned upward and gained the chief dominion; and that is the curse, that **God** has cursed the earth. For His cursing is the fleeing of His active influence (or, His fleeing or cursing is His working curse); just as when **God’s** power works in a thing alongside that thing's life and spirit, and afterwards withdraws itself and its working from that same thing, it is then cursed—for it then works only in its own self-will, and not in **God’s** will.

## XI. Of the Spirit of the World, and the four
## Elements.

96. We can very well contemplate the hidden spiritual world by observing the visible world, for we see that in the depths of the world, fire, light, and air (or: fire, air, and water) are continually born, and that there is no rest or ceasing of such birthing, and it has been so from the beginning of this world. Yet, in the outer world, we can find no causes for what the fundamental ground of this might be. Instead, Reason says: "**God** created it so," and there she stops. This is true in itself, but she does not know the Creator who creates in this manner without ceasing, acting as the true **Archaeus** <term>Archaeus<note>A term from Paracelsian alchemy referring to the "master-builder" or vital force that directs the processes of life and the formation of matter.</note></term> or **Separator** <note>The spiritual force that distinguishes and divides the undifferentiated primal "stuff" of the universe into specific forms and qualities.</note>, who is an outflowing—

102. The third foundation is the sensitivity in the movement of sharpness, acting as a **spiritual sulfur-torment** <note>original: "Schwefel-Qual." In Böhme’s thought, *Qual* refers to both the "source" (Quelle) and the "tormenting" friction or agitation necessary for life to emerge.</note> from the foundation of the **anxious will** <note>original: "ängstlichen Willens." From this arises the spirit with the five senses (namely seeing, hearing, feeling, tasting, and smelling), as the true essential life through which the fire, as the fourth form, becomes manifest.

103. These three properties were called **Salt, Mercury, and Sulfur** by the ancient sages <note>The "ancient sages" refers to alchemists and Hermetic philosophers; these three substances (Tria Prima) were believed to be the fundamental building blocks of all matter.</note>, according to the materials born from them within the four elements, in which such a spirit coagulates or makes itself substantial. Within this foundation also lie the four elements, and they are not separate from it or anything distinct; they are merely the revelation of this spiritual foundation, serving as a housing for the spirit in which that spirit works.

104. The earth is the coarsest outflow of this subtle spirit; after the earth, water is the second; after water, air is the third; and after air, fire is the fourth. These have arisen from a single foundation, namely from the **Spirit of the World** <term>Spirit of the World<note>original: "Spiritu Mundi." In Böhme’s cosmology, this is the universal soul or intermediary force that connects the physical elements to their spiritual origins.</note></term>, which has its root in the inner world.

105. Now **Reason** speaks: For what purpose did the Creator make such a revelation? Answer: There is no other cause than that the spiritual world might introduce itself into a visible, figurative form, so that the inner powers might become pictorial and formal. For this to happen, the spiritual being had to introduce itself into a material foundation in which it could build and form itself; and such a separation had to occur so that the state of separation would forever long for its first foundation again—the inner for the outer, and the outer for the inner.

106. Likewise the four elements, which inwardly are but a single foundation; there, each must long for the other and desire the other, and the inner foundation...

## XII. There follows a brief explanation or formula of Divine Revelation.

118. God is the eternal, immeasurable, incomprehensible Unity, who reveals Himself within Himself from eternity to eternity, as the <term>Trinity</term> <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note>, and is a Father, Son, and Holy Spirit in three kinds of workings, as mentioned before.

119. The first <term>outflow</term> <note>original: "Ausfluß." An emanation or flowing-forth of divine energy from the source.</note> and revelation of this Trinity is the Eternal Word, or the speaking-out of Divine Power. The first spoken essence from this Power is Divine <term>Wisdom</term> <note>original: "Weisheit." Often personified as Sophia, the mirror of God.</note> as an essence in which the Power works.

120. From Wisdom, the power of the <term>breathing-out</term> <note>original: "Aushauchens." The spiritual exhalation that gives life and movement.</note> flows forth and enters into distinction and forming, wherein the Divine Power becomes manifest in its virtue.

121. These same distinct powers lead themselves into recep- <note>The text cuts off mid-word; the catchword indicates the full word is "receptivity" or "agreeableness."</note>

<summary>This page explains that human reason and the physical body are merely external shells for an inner spiritual reality. It also begins a formal description of the Trinity and the process of Divine Revelation through the "Eternal Word" and "Wisdom."</summary>
<keywords>reason vs. understanding, Archæus, Trinity, Divine Wisdom, Spiritum, St. Paul, emanation, inner world</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the "Key" or explanation of terms, picking up from the discussion of how the spirit interacts with the material world. It specifically addresses how the "self-will" of nature develops into fire, light, and darkness, and how these form the basis of the soul and the structure of the universe.</meta>

# XX. Clavis, or Explanation

<meta>catchword: 130. In</meta>

...liness <note>Continuing from the previous page: likely "self-likeness" or "naturalness."</note> into its own self-sensitivity, and from this sensitivity arises self-will and desire.

122. This self-will is the foundation of eternal nature, which leads itself through desire into various properties, reaching all the way to fire.

123. In the desire, darkness arises, and in the fire, the eternal unity with the light becomes manifest within the firing nature.

124. From this firing and light-nature, its kind and property, the angels and souls of humans have originated as a Divine revelation.

125. The power of fire and light is called <term>Tincture</term> <note>original: "Tinctur." In this context, it refers to the spiritual "dye" or life-force that gives quality and vitality to matter.</note>, and the movement of this power is called the holy or pure element.

126. Darkness becomes substantial within itself, and light also becomes substantial within the fiery desire; these two make two <term>Principles</term> <note>original: "Principia." The fundamental starting points or laws of existence.</note>: as in the darkness is God's wrath, and in the light is God's love. Each works within itself; and there is only a distinction between them as between day and night. Yet, both are only a single foundation, and each one is a cause of the other, so that the other may be revealed and recognized within it, just as light is revealed from fire.

127. The visible world is the third <term>Principle</term> <note>original: "Principium."</note>, acting as the third foundation and beginning: this was breathed out from the inner foundation—namely, from the first two principles—and brought into creaturely form and kind.

128. The internal eternal working is hidden within the visible world; it is in all and through all, and yet it is incomprehensible to everything through its own power. The outer forces are only the passive element <note>original: "das Leidende." Literally "the suffering," referring to the part of nature that is acted upon rather than the part that acts.</note>, or the housing, within which the inner forces work. Ordinary creatures are made only from the essence of the outer world, but man was created from both time and eternity, from the essence of all essences, into an image of Divine revelation.

129. The eternal revelation of Divine light is called the Kingdom of Heaven, a dwelling place of the Holy Angels and souls. The fiery darkness is called Hell or the Wrath of God, wherein the devils dwell, along with the damned souls. In the space of this world, Heaven and Hell are present everywhere, but according to the inner foundation.

130. In God's children, the Divine working is manifest within, and in the godless, the working of painful <note>original: "peinlichen." In Böhme's vocabulary, this refers to a state of "anguish" or "torment" caused by the friction of opposing forces in nature.</note> darkness is manifest.

132. From this world, only the four elements with the constellations and the earthly creatures will perish, as they are the outer, coarse life of all things; but the inward power of all beings remains eternal.

## XIII. An explanation follows of several words used in my other books.

->THE GREAT TURBA<-
<note>original: "TURBA MAGNA." A Latin term used by Böhme to describe a state of severe disruption, "uproar," or the awakening of divine wrath within nature.</note>

133. <image-desc>Ornamental drop cap letter D featuring vine and foliage patterns.</image-desc>This is the stirred and awakened wrath of the inward ground, where the hellish foundation becomes manifest in the spirit of this world, from which great plagues and diseases arise; it is also the awakened wrath of outer nature, as can be seen in the shivering of great storms when fire becomes manifest in water. It is an outpouring of God's wrath, by which nature is disturbed <note>original: "turbiret," a wordplay on the term "Turba."</note>.

->THE HOLY TERNARY<-
<note>original: "TERNARIUS SANCTUS." This refers to the Trinity as it manifests as a "holy environment" or a spiritual, substantial realm.</note>

134. This is the inner heavenly, working power within the essence, in which the Trinity <note>original: "Dreyheit"</note> of God works; I understand this to be an essential power.

->SUL and PHUR<-
<note>Böhme frequently breaks the word "Sulphur" into two components to explain the dual nature of reality: the light/oil (Sul) and the fire/burning (Phur).</note>

135. Sul is the flowed-out Unity; it is the essence in which the eternal light works, according to the eternal spiritual kind. In outer metallic (or: material) sulfur, it is the oil in which the light ignites.

136. Phur is the essence of fire, representing nature according to the painfulness of the wrath. The other aspects are explained earlier, and also in the explanation of the Table.

->THE GREAT MYSTERY<-
<note>original: "MYSTERIUM MAGNUM." To Böhme, this is the "womb" or "chaos" of all possibilities from which God spoke the world into existence.</note>

137. God has revealed the Great Mystery—in which the whole of creation lay in an essential manner without form—out of the power of His Word, and has spoken it forth through the Great Mystery into the distinction of the spirit-

### SCIENCE.
<note>original: "SCIENZ." This heading marks the beginning of a specific definition for one of Böhme's most difficult terms.</note>

140. The word *science* is understood by me just as it is understood in the Latin language: however, I understand within it the true ground according to its *sense* <note>original: "Sensu."</note>, which in Latin and also in all languages has fallen into misunderstanding; for every word, in its grasping, formation, and pronunciation, gives the true understanding of what that thing is which is so named.

141. You understand *science* as a body of knowledge or cognition: In German, it is indeed spoken rightly, but not fully expressed.

142. *Science* is the root of the understanding, as of the senses: it is the root for the *center* <note>original: "Centro."</note> of the grasping of the Nothing into Something; as where the will of the Abyss <note>original: "Ungrund," meaning the groundless, primal depth or the "Nothing" before God manifests as a being.</note> draws itself into a *center* of incomprehensibility (that is, into the word), thus the true understanding originates. It is in the divisibility of *science*, where the will separates itself from the grasped *compaction* <note>original: "Compaction," referring to the process where spiritual force becomes "thick" or "solidified" into substance.</note>; thus one understands in the divided (as the divisibility grasps itself into being) first of all the *essence*.

### SCIENCE.

140. The word Science is understood by me just as it is understood in the Latin language: however, I understand within it the true ground according to its sense, which has fallen into a misunderstanding in Latin and also in all languages; for every word gives in its conception, formation, and pronunciation the true understanding of what that thing is which is so named.

141. You understand "Science" as a knowledge or recognition: in German it is indeed spoken correctly, but not fully expressed.

142. Science is the root of understanding, and of the senses: it is the root of the Center of the conception of the *Nothing* into *Something*; where the will of the Groundless <note>original: "Ungrund." The pre-existent "abyss" or "groundless" state of the Divine before manifestation.</note> draws itself into itself to a Center of inhabitability (that is, to the Word), so the true understanding originates. It exists in the "separability" of the Science, where the will separates itself from the conceived compaction; thus one understands for the first time the Essence <note>original: "Essenz."</note> in that which is separated (since the separability conceives itself into being).

143. For Essence is a substantial power, but Science is a hovering, flying [thing], just like the senses: and it is even
<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>109</page-num>
<header>of the most prominent points and words.</header>

the root of the senses. Yet in the understanding, where it is called Science, it is not the sensuality <note>The quality of being perceptible by the senses.</note> itself, but rather the cause of sensuality. In the manner that the understanding conceives itself in the mind, there must first be a cause which commands the mind, from which the understanding flows out into its contemplation. Thus, Science is the root of the fiery mind; and it is, in summary, the root of all spiritual beginnings, as the true root of the soul, and so on through all life, for it is the ground of life from which it comes.

144. I have been able to give it no other name, because this one matches so perfectly in its sense: for it is the cause that the Divine, groundless will draws itself in, and conceives itself into nature, toward the distinct, recognizable, and sensible life of the understanding and discernment; for with the drawing-in of the Science, as the will draws the same into itself, natural life and the Word of all lives originate.

145. The distinction from the fire is to be understood as follows: The eternal Science in the will of the Father draws the will (which is called Father) into itself, and closes itself into a center of the Divine birth of the Trinity; and speaks itself out with the Science into a Word of the understanding. And in the speaking is the "separability" in the Science; there, in every separation, is the desire for the containment of the speaking; and the containment is substantial, and is called Divine Essence.

146. Out of this Essence the Word now speaks itself out in the second separation (as if from nature): And in that same speaking, where the creaturely will separates itself into its center into a sensuality, there the separation from the fiery Science is understood; for from that is the soul and all angelic spirits.

147. The third separation happens according to the outer nature from the outspoken, formed Word, in which the bestial Science lies; as is to be seen in the treatise *On the Election of Grace* <note>original: "Gnaden Wahl." One of Böhme's major works, written in 1623.</note>, which is very sharp in its understanding, and one of the clearest among my writings.

## SCIENCE.

140. The word *Science* <note>original: *Scienz*</note> is understood by me just as it is understood in the Latin language: however, I understand within it the true ground according to its **Sense** <note>original: *Sensu*</note>, which in the Latin and also in all languages has fallen into a lack of understanding; for every word, in its composition, formation, and pronunciation, gives the true understanding of what that thing is which is so named.

141. You understand by *Science* a "knowledge" or "cognition": In German it is indeed spoken rightly, but not fully expressed.

142. *Science* is the root of the understanding, as well as of **sensuality** <note>The capacity for feeling or perceiving.</note>: it is the root of the Center of the framing of the Nothing into a Something; as the place where the Will of the **Unground** <note>original: *Ungrund*; the "abyss" or "groundless" source of God before any manifestation</note> draws itself into a Center of incomprehensibility (that is, into the Word), so the true understanding originates. It is in the "separability" of the Science where the Will separates itself from the grasped compaction; thus one understands in that which is separated (where the separability takes hold in being) for the very first time the *Essence* <note>original: *Essens*</note>.

143. For *Essence* is a substantial power, but *Science* is a hovering, flying thing, just like the senses: and it is precisely

<meta>catchword: the</meta>

<column-break/>

<page-num>109</page-num>
<header>of the most important points and words.</header>

the root of the senses. Yet in the understanding, where it is called *Science*, it is not the sensuality itself, but the cause of sensuality; in the manner that the understanding takes hold in the **Mind** <note>original: *Gemüthe*; refers to the deep seat of emotions, will, and spirit.</note>, there must first be a cause which the Mind provides, from which the understanding flows out into its contemplation. Thus the *Science* is the root of the fiery Mind; and it is, in total, the root of all spiritual beginnings, as the true root of the soul, and so on through all life, for it is the ground of life from which it comes.

144. I have been able to give it no other name, because this one in its **Sense** <note>inner meaning</note> coincides so completely: for it is the cause that the Divine, groundless Will draws itself in, and takes hold in Nature, toward the diverse, recognizable, and sensitive life of the understanding and discernment; for with the drawing-in of the Science, as the Will pulls it into itself, the natural life originates, and the Word of all lives.

145. The distinction out of the fire is to be understood as follows: The eternal *Science* in the Will of the Father draws the Will (which is called Father) into itself, and encloses itself in a Center of the Divine birth of the Trinity; and speaks itself out with the *Science* into a Word of the understanding. And in the speaking is the separability in the *Science*; there, in every separation, is the desire for the encompassing of the speaking-out; and the encompassing is substantial, and is called Divine *Essence*.

146. Out of this *Essence* the Word now speaks itself in the second separation (as if out of Nature): And in that same speaking-out, as the creaturely will separates itself into its Center into a sensuality, there the separation out of the fiery *Science* is understood; for from that is the soul and all **Angelic spirits**.

147. The third separation happens according to the outer nature from the spoken, formed Word, in which the **bestial Science** <note>original: *viehische Scienz*; the instinctual "knowing" or driving force of animals.</note> lies; as is to be seen in the treatise *On the Election of Grace* <note>original: *Gnaden-Wahl*; referring to Böhme’s 1623 work *De Electione Gratiae*</note>, which is very sharp in its understanding, and one of the clearest among my writings.

**GOD** is Nothing, compared to the creature, and is
the Groundless <note>original: *Ungrund*; Böhme’s term for the absolute, undifferentiated void or "nothingness" out of which God and the universe emerge</note> and All.

1. Will (Father), from which proceeds **Science** <note>original: *Scienz*; not "science" in the modern sense, but the spiritual seed of knowing or the "root" of all active powers</note>, the root of the knowledge of the spirit or of all powers.

2. Desire, Mind, or Heart of the Will (Son) is
the outcome from **Science**.

3. This Desire is an "uncontainability" <note>an unbounded state</note>; within that which is contained, understand the Trinity, from which is breathed out:

4. The speaking Word in **GOD** (in **Potential**) <note>original: *in Potentia*; Latin for "in potentiality" or "in power," referring to the Word before it is manifest</note> or the Divine Power.

5. Wisdom is the outcome from the Word, otherwise called the **Great Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*; a Latin term for the chaotic, unmanifested source of all things</note> or Divine Contemplation.

And so far is **GOD** to be understood as to what He is without nature
and creature, as a being begetting itself within itself.

->The following is the speaking of the Word as the<-
->sensibility or "findability" <note>the state of being able to be found or perceived</note>.<-

->I. PRINCIPLE.<-

**TINCTURE** <note>original: *Tinctur*; in theosophy, the spiritual essence or "soul" that gives life and color to matter</note> is the speaking of the Word (**the Word in Action**) <note>original: *Verbum in Actu*; Latin for "Word in Act"</note>. The first form of nature is Desire or the attractive power which overshadows itself within itself, and from which darkness arises. 1. The Sharpness is a property of Desire, which impresses itself and makes itself hard. 2. Hardness is the second property arising from it. 3. Cold is the third property. This Desire, with its three properties, is a mother of all salts.

**SALT.** The second form is **Science** or the bitter sting of "findability" or sensibility, from which the mercurial life arises (the five senses).

<header>XX. Key, or Explanation</header>

**GOD** is the Delight of eternity as the **Groundless** <term>Ungrund; the "abyss" or primordial state of God before any nature or light exists—a state of pure, undifferentiated potential</term>: This same Delight contains within itself a **Will** toward the revelation of that Delight; and the Will conceives itself within itself to be its own **Ground**, which is the **Center of the Godhead**: And it is a **Chaos** <note>here meaning a fertile, unformed primal state</note> like an **Eye of Eternity**, of the Groundless and the Ground. This same Will is a desiring to reveal itself in the highest Kingdom of Joy, as in the power of Majesty, within that which the Will has conceived in itself. Now, however, no revelation may occur without an **Impression** <note>original: *Impression*; a drawing-in or contraction that creates density</note> or movement. Thus, the Impression is **Harshness**, which is the **"Let it be"** <note>original: *Fiat*; the creative command from Genesis</note> of all beings. It is the contraction, the desiring Will.

**Will** is the Father of all being.

**Delight** is a longing to reveal itself, and is the **Angelic World** <note>original: *Englische Welt*; from "Engel" (angel), referring to the realm of light and divine inhabitants, not the country of England</note>.

**Desire** gives the **Dark World** and Nature.

**Chaos** is the Groundless, from which the Soul originates in the **Fire-Eye**. The Spirit is born out of the Soul; it is the likeness and image of GOD, which possesses the Angelic World.

1. The **Impression** gives birth to **Harshness** <note>original: *Herbe*; the first property of nature, characterized by cold, contraction, and "astringency"</note>. This Harshness makes from its own self-property **Saturn** (♄), **Mars** (♂), and **Mercury** (☿); that is **PHUR** within **SUL** <note>Böhme’s mystical breakdown of the word *Sulphur*. He associates "SUL" with the light/oil of God and "PHUR" with the fiery, stinging wheel of nature</note>.

**SUL** is of God, or the revealed God Himself. **PHUR** makes the **Wheel of the Senses**: And the essence of being makes the **Bitter Stinging**, as the second form. Or to put it even more clearly:

The 1st form is **SALT** <note>original: *SAL*</note>, **Saturn**, the Desire of the Eternal Word (which is God); it is the beginning of Eternal Nature, and is the conception of the **Eternal Nothing** into a **Something**. It is the cause of all beings, including cold and heat, as well as water and air, the formation of powers, and a cause of taste: The Mother of all Salts.

2. **Bitter Stinging**, which draws the Harshness into the Impression.

The **Drawing** is the **Bitter Sting**, which is **Mercury** (☿), the mover toward life.

The second form is **Mercury** (☿), the mobility of the Desire, acting as the "attraction"; it is the cause of all life and movement, as well as the senses and diversity.

3. Thus it creates **Anxiety**, and the Sting rages in the Anxiety:

So the desire of the free delight separates back into itself, as into the **Nothingness**, and has sharpened itself in the **Impression** <note>original: *Impression*; the drawing-in or contraction of the divine will to create form</note>, and has become a **fire**, indeed, a **love-fire**.

The third form is **Sulphur** <note>original symbol: 🜍</note> or **Mars** <note>original symbol: ♂</note>, which is **Anxiety** <note>original: *Angst*; in Böhme’s thought, this refers to the "narrowness" or tension produced when forces conflict</note>, as the sensitivity, a cause of the mind in which the senses become active.
