The **Light** creates the **second Principle** with power and joyful abundance.

The fourth form creates the truly living spirit, half in the **fire-world** according to its essence, and half in the **light-world**, according to its understanding. The power in the light is the **understanding**, and the **intelligent spirit**.

## SULPHUR; Also to be considered:

**PHUR** <note>Böhme splits the word "Sul-phur," treating "Phur" as the active, fiery driver of life</note> is the mover of life, and stands in the four forms above, as: (1) **Harsh**, (2) **Bitter-sting**, (3) **Anxiety**, (4) **Fire**. (1) **Harsh** is called **Saturn** <note>original symbol: ♄; representing contraction and coldness</note>. (2) **Bitter-sting** is called **Mercury** <note>original symbol: ☿; representing movement and communication</note>. (3) **Anxiety** is called the **agony of fire** <note>original symbol: 🜍; Sulphur</note>. (4) And in the **Sulphuric agony** the light becomes shining, as the **Groundless** becomes a radiance; and in that radiance the first three forms—Harsh, Bitter, Anxiety—are transformed.

### Fire, Mars, and the Sun.

The **fire** <note>original symbol: △</note> is a cause of the true **spirit-life**, wherein the holy powers of the **free delight** are redeemed from the **harsh roughness**; for the **fire** swallows the **dark being** of the **Impression** into its essence and carries it out into **spiritual powers** within itself.

## The I. PRINCIPLE.

This is the most fundamental source in the **seven source-spirits** <term>Qual-Geister; a wordplay by Böhme combining *Quelle* (source) and *Qual* (torment/agony), describing the seven active properties of nature</term> of **GOD**, as one considers it: when the light was not yet within, where the **sharpness**—that is, the **wrath** and **hellish agony and pain**—was and became; that is, where **Lucifer** became a **devil** out of it. Thither all damned souls belong.

Between the **First** and **Second Principle**, the **natural heaven** is the distinction.

The sixth form has spoken out this world in the seventh form.

<header>...of the most eminent Points and Words.</header>

*This seventh form.* This desire of ☿ Mercury <note>original symbol: ☿; representing the principle of motion, communication, and the "vital" spirit</note>, (as ♄ Saturn <note>original symbol: ♄; representing the principle of contraction, coldness, and the "foundation" of matter</note> was mentioned in the fifth form), is a hunger, and draws into itself the power of the light, and makes it corporeal within the desire of the **Impression** <note>original: *Impreſſion*; the divine process of "thickening" or "compressing" spirit into substance</note>; this is now the seventh form, a house of the spirit and a substantial being. This seventh form is the essence of nature, Luna <note>original: *Luna*; representing the Moon or the physical, reflective body</note>, Saturn, and is the formed essence of the power; what the first six forms are in the spirit, the seventh is in the perceptible essence, like a casing for all the others: Or like a body of the spirit, in which the spirit works and plays with itself. It is also a food of the fire, from which the $\triangle$ fire-essence <note>original symbol: $\triangle$; the symbol for fire, representing the driving force of life and transformation</note> draws for its nourishment, in which it burns; and the seventh is the kingdom of the glory of God. This seventh form or revealed power of God desires to behold itself: It grasps the heavenly divine powers and itself once more, and is the heavenly nature, the Wisdom, the angelic world, the great **Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium*; the hidden foundation from which all things originate</note>. Thus light and darkness have played with one another. In the seventh form, God's Wisdom has been revealed from eternity, out of which He created all things. The creation of all things is the revealed, formed Wisdom, which in the human property of the new birth is called Christ. The seventh form is the **Sabbath** <note>original: *Sabbath*; a state of divine rest and completion where the restless "forms" of nature find peace</note>, in which the other six rest from all their labor, give birth to themselves anew, and rest again. This is the eternal essence.

## The III. PRINCIPLE.

In the first impression, the first three forms are the dark world. In the second impression, these same three forms are the radiance of the light, and are the angelic world. What is hellish in the first dark impression is heavenly in the other impression. From these both, from the desire of the light and the dark, the 3rd Principle is born, entirely according to the property of the two inner worlds: For out of the property of light and darkness, which are One and not two, this world was spoken forth, and in the **Fiat Word** <note>original: *Verbo Fiat*; the Latin "Let there be," the creative command of God</note> (as the desire) it was grasped and formed. The third Principle is outside the nature-heaven, yet it **works within** <note>original: *inqualirets*; a Böhmean term meaning to operate within a substance or to share its qualities</note> it, and is the **sidereal** <term>sidereal; relating to the stars or the astral forces that govern the physical world</term> spirit of God. Within this, the sidereal air is the spirit, and it is the animal kingdom, for the earthly belongs to it; this arose in the wrath of God when this world was created, and is this world.

<meta>This page transitions from the general explanation of the Seven Forms of Nature into a concise "Key" or "Skeleton" of Jacob Böhme’s cosmogony. It likely links the preceding discussion of the Seventh Form (the "house of the spirit") to the underlying metaphysical process of how the "Ungrund" or Groundless void becomes manifest as God, Spirit, and physical reality.</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation

From this we must pass into the other middle **Principle** <note>original: *Principium*; Böhme describes three Principles: the dark fire-world, the light love-world (this "middle" one), and the physical world</note>, so that we become children of God. But if we do not participate in the second Principle, then the sidereal spirit <note>original: *syderische Geist*; the astral spirit or "star-spirit" that governs the natural mind and physical body</note> rules us within the light of nature, which is a subtle deception; thus, we are heathens.

->This is from Mr. Friedrich Krause, M.D.’s<-
->own hand.<-

->The Author’s Skeleton of the aforementioned, a<-
->key to his writings.<-

**Groundless** <note>original: *Ungrund*; the absolute, bottomless void or nothingness that precedes existence</note>, Nothing. The unfathomable will of the Father proceeds from the Groundless.
The proceeding is the Spirit of the Will.
That which has proceeded is that which is found, acting as the mirror.
The Will imagines within the mirror of what is found, draws into itself, and becomes pregnant; this is the **Heart** <note>representing the Son or the center of divine manifestation</note>. Thus the Will dwells as the Father, the Heart as the Son, and the proceeding as the Spirit within the mirror.
The mirror is the eye of the **Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterii*</note>.
The Spirit, which proceeds from the Will and the Heart, is the seeing or the essence within the mirror. The mirror is its glass with its wonders.
The wonder is a **flash** <note>original: *Bliß*; a spark of divine intuition or the "lightning" of creation</note> with all colors, yet not one of them is yet recognized. The wonder is a desire for nature, in which it may reveal itself; and it receives nature into that desire.
The desire imagines toward the light of freedom, and becomes manifest out of itself in the fire: and within itself in the dark world.
The fire becomes manifest in the light, in freedom; and the **Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium*</note> with the mirror of wonders—which in its origin stands and also remains in freedom—becomes manifest with the light, and is called **God**, encompassed by essentiality. The fire of God lusts after being, and receives being.
Within being is the **Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the spiritual "dye" or transformative power that brings life and quality to matter</note>.
The Tincture desires a body, which is the Element.
The Element desires the **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*; the fundamental "being" or soul-substance of a thing</note>;
The **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*</note> is Paradise or the being of the essence within the first being. The **Essence** <note>original: *Ens*</note> of the fire and of the light is called the **Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; the purest, most refined substance beyond the four physical elements</note>.

<meta>catchword: Die (The)</meta>

<summary>This page provides a metaphysical "key" to Jacob Böhme’s system, tracing the journey from the "Ungrund" (Groundless Nothing) through the formation of the Trinity and the manifestation of the divine "flash" into nature and being. It defines the hierarchical relationship between the Will, the Mirror of Wonders, Fire, Light, and the Quintessence.</summary>

All this desires substance out of itself; that is the external world, a complete likeness of the internal world.

### Another Key by the Author, from the own hand of the same Mr. Friedrich Krause, M.D. <note>original: *Medicinae Doctor*; Friedrich Krause was a physician and a close friend and supporter of Böhme.</note>

**Primal Ground** <note>original: *Urgrund*; the absolute beginning of all things</note>: Eternal Will of the groundless being.
The **Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium*</note> of the Trinity <note>original: *Dreyzahl*; literally "the number three," referring to the Triune nature of God</note> with **Wisdom** outside of Nature is Will, Heart, Spirit, and Mirror; it is not yet called "God." But it is called so in the kindling of the **Principle** <note>original: *Principii*</note>.
A **Principle** is, according to the Father's nature, his own property;
In Majesty, he is called **God**. For in Majesty is the Deity, and it is Spirit, Life, and Power.
Wisdom is the housing of the Mystery outside of Nature; it is like a luster or mirror of the Mystery. Here, it is only the mirror of Wisdom, of the wonders.
Wisdom is the revelation of the Deity, and the cause of the **Imagination** <note>Böhme uses "Imagination" to mean the process of "forming into an image" or bringing thought into substance</note> of the Father toward the Son.
**Dark World** is the foundation, the sinking.
**Fire-World**, Father. **First Principle**.
**Light-World**, Son. **Second Principle**.
The Holy Spirit proceeds from the Father and the Son.
Wisdom is the housing of these three. Here, the Trinity dwells in Wisdom.
**Substance** <note>original: *Wesen*</note> is **Tincture**, the highest, and Tincture is substance.
**Substantiality** <note>original: *Wesenheit*</note> is dead in itself; the **Tincture** <note>term: *Tinctur*; in alchemy, a Tincture is a substance that can transform and "color" another; for Böhme, it is the life-giving spirit that animates dead matter</note> is its life.
Substantiality is Divine, heavenly corporeality.
**Aquafer** <note>likely *Aqua-Vera* or "True Water," the spiritual substance of the heavens</note> is heavenly substantiality, made from heavenly water, wherein are Mercury ☿, Sulphur △, and Salt 🜔 <note>These are the *Tria Prima*, the three primary alchemical principles representing Spirit, Soul, and Body</note>.
**Fire** is the true Principle; the strong, great, almighty life [the Father (says Christ) is greater than I. John 14:28. For the fire is greater than the light] between all three worlds; it gives to all three their **Source-Torment** <note>original: *Qual*; Böhme uses this word as a pun on *Quelle* (source), *Qualität* (quality), and *Qual* (torment/pain). It refers to the agonizing tension required for life to exist</note> and essences. Out of the Source-Torment arises the true, intelligible life. What the fire gives, from that comes understanding. Fire is called substance, and it gives spirit. Fire gives wind: Light gives air.
**Essence** <note>original: *Essentz*</note> is the whole being within itself: it is the life of substantiality. Essence has no life nor being of its own; it is like a mirror and the "finding"; it is more subtle than substance; it draws into

<meta>This page continues the "Key" (Clavis) or explanation of Jacob Böhme’s mystical vocabulary. The previous page ended while discussing the Trinity (Dreyzahl); this page picks up by explaining how the Divine will takes on "substantiality" or a spiritual body, moving from essence to physical existence.</meta>

## XX. Key, or Explanation

[it forms] itself, so that **substantiality** <note>original: *Wesenheit*; the state of having a real, spiritual body or substance</note> comes into being. Substantiality comes from **essence** <note>original: *Essentz*</note>. From the Sun’s essence, water is formed. That is fire in the **Language of Nature** <note>original: *Natur-Sprache*; the idea that the sounds of words reveal the inner essence of the things they describe</note>: *sentz* <note>the second half of the word 'Essentz'</note> is the housing, the becoming-a-body; it holds the fire within itself. Essence is like a greening, a welling up (like the **life-spirits** in the flesh), or like rain, or small lives within **Wisdom** without the true Spirit. Yet a spirit arises from it—the true Spirit and life—as if from its cause.

**Ens** <note>Latin: "Being" or "Entity"</note> is the greening.

**Iliaster** <note>A term adapted from Paracelsus, meaning the primary spiritual foundation or "prime matter" of nature</note> is the beginning of nature.

**Cagastrum** <note>A Paracelsian term referring to the "outbirth" or the external, often corruptible world that arises from the internal</note> is the external fire in the outbirth.

**Necrolice** <note>Böhme's term for the dark, proto-matter or the state of nature before the "flash" of light</note> is the dark world in the first three forms <note>the first three properties of nature: attraction, friction, and anxiety</note>.

**Necromantice** <note>Böhme's term for the soul-power or fire-spirit</note> is the fire-spirit, the **Principle** <note>original: *Principium*</note> with its... Necromantice in the Son, in the light-world.

The **Heavenly Virgin** <note>Divine Wisdom personified as a feminine mirror of God</note> is a luster and mirror of the Holy Spirit. He is the "seeing" within her. She does not see herself; she is the wonder, the colors. The Holy Spirit is the power within her. If she were the power herself, she would be the mother <note>original: *Gebärerin*; literally "the one who gives birth"</note>.

**Iliaster** is the **Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium*</note> of the Divine Being outside of nature, which contains within itself the will toward nature. That which is "contained" in the will is the substance of nature with its forms and properties. That is the beginning and the **dark Fiat** <note>Latin for "let it be done"; the creative command</note>, and it stands in **harshness** <note>original: *Herbigkeit*; the first property of nature, a contracting, cold force</note>. It is in itself the dark world; in the outbirth of the third Principle, it is the Earth. The two forms, Good and Evil (pertaining to the dark, severe form), are born from it. But that which is the spirit-form in itself is called **Cagastrum**, the center of nature.

**Necrolice** are the forms before the fire and the Principle, in the **Chamber of Anguish** <note>original: *Angst-Kammer*; the intense state of spiritual tension and longing before the light breaks through</note>.

**Necromantice** is the Principle; the true fire-world or soul which begets the second center in the light. That is called Necromantice: this is the revelation of the Iliaster, where the Iliaster becomes manifest in fire and light. This is the noble image according to God, a "little God" (containing the **Trinity** <note>original: *Dreyzahl*</note>) or God Himself. It is also the Holy Trinity outside of form, insofar as the Principle is ignited. By the Trinity, the image is ignited from the majestic light unto Divine understanding.

**Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctur*; the transformative spiritual power or "inner life-blood" of the universe</note> is the Virgin, as the life of Wisdom; it has a flying, spiritual life; it is the sharp spirit in Wisdom. According to substance, it is the Virginal Spirit: it is in Wis-

<meta>catchword: ...dom (senheit)</meta>

<summary>This page defines technical terms from Böhme’s cosmology, such as Iliaster and Cagastrum, explaining how the Divine Will manifests as "substantiality" or matter. It describes the Heavenly Virgin as a mirror for the Holy Spirit and the process by which the soul (Necromantice) is born from the fire-world into the light.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the alphabetical index and glossary of Jacob Böhme’s complex theological terms. The previous page ended while discussing the nature of "Substantiality" (spiritual substance) as the "body" of the divine. This page continues that definition and moves on to defining the Fifth Essence, Zion, and Wisdom.</meta>

### Of the Most Distinguished Points and Words. 119
<meta>catchword: Light</meta>

...substantiality <note>the text continues from the previous page's definition of spiritual substance</note>, the other opener of Wisdom according to the Spirit of God: Is the splendor of **Wisdom**: Is the body of the Holy Spirit or of the Divinity. In her, the Holy Spirit opens the wonders; here she is dissolved: Belongs to the wonders.

**Fifth Essence** <note>original: *Quinta Essentia*; in alchemy, the purest "fifth element" beyond the physical four</note> is **Paradise-quality** <note>original: *Paradeis-Qual*; for Böhme, "Qual" implies a "source" or "quality of movement" rather than just "pain"</note> in the heavenly world: In the external world, it is enclosed. When it is dissolved, it acts as a **Tincture** <note>original: *Tinctura*; a transformative spiritual force or "dye" that can change the nature of a thing</note>: Yet because it is in the fire, it is only half open: If it were fully open, what could it not do? Outside the fire, the Tincture and the thing tinted are enclosed again. In the **new man** <note>the spiritually reborn person</note>, it is open in the Fifth Essence, yet covered by the **old man** <note>the fallen, physical nature of humanity</note>; it can perform deeds and wonders, and move mountains.

**External Mystery** <note>original: *Aeusser Mysterium*; the physical universe as a hidden sign of God</note> (to which the stars belong) has all the power and being of the **Internal Mystery**. Only that it is an **outbirth** <note>original: *Ausgeburt*; a physical manifestation or "offspring" of a spiritual reality</note>, and is as a likeness: Everything that exists and can be named in this world belongs to and enters into it: within it are fire, water, earth, and air.

**Air** is the spirit of the Mystery.

**God’s Kingdom of Joy** is when one is placed out of the **chamber of anxiety** <note>original: *Angst-Kammer*; the dark, contracted state of the soul before it finds God's light</note> through death into freedom, into the light.

**Zion** is in itself the paradisiacal being and form of **Paradise**: Is the **greening** <note>original: *Ausgrünen*; Böhme frequently uses botanical metaphors to describe spiritual growth</note> of the paradisiacal being, which is hidden in the old **Adamic man**; and is understood in the new man.

The **governance** <note>original: *Regiment*</note> in Zion is the **eternal element**, in which everything lies. Zion greens from the being of the Element, just as the essence greens from the earth: It is the greening of the new body in Christ.

**Wisdom** however is the eye of the Element and also of Zion, where all wonders are perceived; and from the wonders comes the greening according to the form of the sight in that same **mirror** <note>Böhme views Wisdom as a mirror reflecting God's ideas</note>. Wisdom is no being <note>or "substance"</note>, nor does it take any being into itself; rather, it is the eye and understanding of the being: It dwells in the being; It is the wonder within it. Wisdom gives colors, or the form of colors: Wisdom is the wonders that the Spirit beholds; it encompasses them all.

**Paradise** is **substantiality** <note>original: *Wesenheit*</note>: That is true essence or substantiality.

The **Holy Element** is the life of that same being: It has the same life and being within itself, and is closest to this world.

**Element** is substantiality and the life thereof.

<vocab>Fifth Essence, Mystery, Zion, Wisdom, Paradise, Element, Tincture, Substantiality, Spirit, Wonders</vocab>

<summary>This page defines key terms in Böhme’s mystical system, focusing on the "Fifth Essence" as a transformative power and "Zion" as the spiritual growth of the "new man" within the physical body. It concludes by describing Wisdom as the divine "eye" or "mirror" that perceives the wonders of God's creation.</summary>

--- Page 156 ---
<meta>This page concludes the glossary of mystical and theological terms found in Jacob Böhme’s writings. It follows the previous page's definitions of "Substantiality" and the "Fifth Essence," completing the alphabetical or thematic guide to his unique spiritual vocabulary.</meta>

# XX. Key, or Explanation of etc.

**Light** is Majesty. **Son** is the Heart of the Light, and the Power of Majesty.

**Father** is the Power of the fiery essences: HE is the hidden Fire-World. This is not made manifest in the Kingdom of Heaven.

**Soul** has the form of nature: It is the Principle of the Father: it contains both the Dark-World and the Fire-World: It is those worlds itself. If the fire cannot burn, then the soul has no light and is a Devil; whose fire also cannot burn <note>Böhme suggests that a soul without the "light" of God becomes a dark, cold fire of self-centeredness</note>.

The **holy soul** possesses the Spirit of Christ: That is the Holy Spirit, which proceeds out from the soul.

**Turba** <term>Latin for "turmoil" or "disturbance"; Böhme uses it to describe a state of divine wrath that disrupts and breaks down natural forms</term> is: When wrath opens itself in a thing, the good is destroyed: It is led into an anguish-torment <note>original: *Angst-Qual*; Böhme often links the word "Qual" (pain/torment) with "Quelle" (source), implying a painful state of becoming</note> within the property of the dark world. **Turba** is the will of wrath and anger, a disrupter: Just as when sweetness becomes sour, like vinegar in milk, so that the milk is no longer what it was before.

**Tincture** <term>an alchemical term for a transformative substance; here, a spiritual "dye" or life-force that perfects nature</term> is in all three Principles: It is the essence of the Holy Trinity. It is not a "maker" of things, but rather the life of fire and light; an opener of all colors, leading everything to the highest degree among the wonders. Just as the external Tincture makes gold; yet it does not "create" the gold, it only opens its potential. **Tincture** is the life of Wisdom; the Holy Spirit is the life of all. Power proceeds from the Center of Nature; it is painful at first, then in the light it is changed into something lovely and friendly.

**Great Mystery** <note>original: *Mysterium Magnum*; the primordial "chaos" or "vast hiddenness" from which God brings forth the universe</note> outside of nature is entirely one with Wisdom: it has all forms, such as the Elements and Paradise: The Tincture is its highest point. Everything proceeds from the **Great Mystery**. This is present in all three Principles: In every thing, according to its property. Every thing is something secret: It has come out of the **Great Mystery** and must go back into it.

Thus **Man** performs his wonders in the elements, and in his own internal **Mystery**, and leads them into the root of the elements, and thus into the **Mystery**. When the elements dissolve, the **Mystery** remains.

The soul, together with this **Mystery**, is a fire; therefore, man must give an account for it within the **Mystery**.

**Urge** <note>original: *Sucht*; in Böhme's context, this relates to a driving longing or the "seeking" (*Suchen*) that originates in the roots of nature</note> is the original state of nature.

**Will** is GOD the Father of eternity.

It emphasizes the soul's origin in the "Fire-World" and its need for the Light of Christ to avoid becoming "a Devil."</summary>

## THEOSOPHICAL EPISTLES, <note>original Latin: *Epistolæ Theosophicæ*</note>
->or<-
# Theosophical Epistles,
->Of the Man of God<-
->Enlightened by God through Grace,<-
# Jacob Böhme,
->containing<-
### Various Godly Admonitions
### toward True Repentance and
### Amendment of Life,
->as well as<-
### a simple account of the most worthy
### knowledge of Divine and natural
### Wisdom,
### alongside an examination of current times.
->Written from the year 1618 to 1624.<-
